- **0.1.9**: known vulnerabilities in dependencies are now imported through RoamSwitch. PersonalSOC has RoamSwitch's `run_package_cve_scan_languages` match the project folders registered in RoamSwitch (its watched folders) and adds the known vulnerabilities of npm, PyPI, crates.io and other dependencies to the report (PersonalSOC passes no path and sends nothing; only the 30 with the highest CVSS are listed and the rest is reported as a count; if no folder is registered, this is noted instead of being counted as a source failure). The LLM investigation gained a ledger of confirmed facts, built only from detections and tool output, so nothing the LLM writes goes into it; the LLM's hypotheses are kept apart, shown under separate headings each round and in separate sections of the report. A new tool, `search_evidence`, searches only the collected log text with secrets masked, for a fixed string (not a regex; case-insensitive), and reads no files (results are capped at 1 to 20 lines, 200 characters a line and about 4 KB in total; a search term appears in the facts only when it is a short plain token). The LLM can also call `verify_security_findings` (through RoamSwitch's MCP), which re-evaluates the diagnostic items right now and returns, per item, still present (stillPresent), resolved (resolved) or cannot be confirmed (inconclusive); an item that cannot be confirmed is counted as "unconfirmed", never as safe or resolved, and without RoamSwitch, or on an older version that lacks the tool, it returns an error and adds no fact (`run_active_vuln_scan` and `verify_fim` are still not allowed). opencode is now treated as an LLM whose tools can be locked: we confirmed with a live model that the shell, file creation, file read and edit, URL fetch and MCP tools all fail with a permission error, so the confirmation in Settings is no longer needed (your own opencode settings can undo this lock, so before each run PersonalSOC checks the resolved permissions; if any allow rule remains, it does not start opencode and shows the rule that causes it). claude is started without loading your settings (`--setting-sources ""`), so that your hooks and CLAUDE.md files do not enter an investigation; a side effect is that a setup that signs in through `env` or `apiKeyHelper` in `~/.claude/settings.json` (a proxy, Bedrock, Vertex) fails with "Not logged in", and the error carries a hint, while adding the LLM's name to `llm_user_settings` in the settings file (hand-edited only) lets it read those settings (its tools and MCP stay switched off). When an investigation reaches the round limit, the output is now the reason and the last hypothesis rather than raw JSON. Characters that act as line breaks, such as U+2028, are removed, and search normalization now matches what is displayed. The third-party license list was rebuilt, because dependencies such as the updater were missing from it (only permissive licenses (MIT, Apache-2.0, ISC, BSD, Zlib) were added). The added texts are in 10 languages (8 of them machine-translated and not reviewed by native speakers).
- **0.1.10**: consent to automatic log collection can now be given from the command line (`personalsoc consent`), for environments without the app window. Until then consent could only be changed in the settings screen, so on Linux installed without a screen, and on the Server Edition, the "consent in Settings" that `personalsoc scan` asks for had nowhere to go. `personalsoc consent` lists what will be read (the sources enabled in this environment and the RoamSwitch MCP) and saves only when you answer `y` or pass `--yes`. Where it cannot ask and there is no `--yes`, it refuses and saves nothing. `--status` shows the current state and `--revoke` withdraws consent (scheduled runs stop too). Your other settings are kept as they are. Nothing was read until you consented, as before. The consent error of `personalsoc scan` also pointed to `personalsoc consent`. The new and changed strings are in 10 languages (other than Japanese and English they are machine translations and have not been checked by native speakers). The consent mechanism itself was removed in 0.1.11.
- (0.1.11) **Changed: the "consent" to reading logs automatically is gone.** Until now, neither `personalsoc scan` nor the "Check status" button read anything until you ticked the consent in Settings (or ran `personalsoc consent` where there is no screen). Whether you run it by hand or on a schedule, it was a step with no substance, so it was removed. `personalsoc scan` now reads without consent. The `personalsoc consent` command and the consent checkbox in Settings are gone. Scheduled runs work with the "Check automatically on a schedule" switch alone. A `consented` entry in an old settings file is read and discarded. Content is handed to an LLM only when you pass `--llm` (in the app, when you turn on LLM integration), as before. It reads only the fixed sources listed in the README and stays read-only.
- (0.1.11) **Note: logs that only root can read (authentication, web server, auditd) are read by running with `sudo`.** PersonalSOC does not raise its own privileges. Without `sudo`, a log that cannot be read is reported as unreadable, and a result of "no detections" then is not proof of safety. This is now in the README. I checked it on a Debian-like host, comparing an ordinary user with `sudo`.
- (0.1.11) The text is in 10 languages (the dictionary was regenerated).
- (0.1.12) **Improved: the message for a log that could not be read now includes an example with `sudo`.** Before, for a log that needs more privileges (authentication, web server, auditd), the message only said "cannot read with this privilege (root or the adm group is required)" and left you wondering what to do. Now it also says "Run it with sudo, for example: `sudo personalsoc scan --report`". The text is in 10 languages.
- **Web log collection** (0.1.13): fixed Web logs being read as empty on web servers that keep one log per virtual host (Linux; it now reads `other_vhosts_access.log` and the current `*access*.log` files), and lines in Apache's `vhost_combined` format. On the Mac it reads the logs of the macOS Apache and of Homebrew's nginx and httpd. On Ubuntu desktops, OS package CVEs are capped at confidence "hypothesis" and severity "low" (a stopgap while the matching used Debian data).
- (0.1.14) **Fixed: on an Ubuntu machine, results that RoamSwitch had matched with Ubuntu's data were being lowered to "hypothesis, low"** (0.1.13). RoamSwitch Linux 1.11.28 matches an Ubuntu machine with Ubuntu's data (only CVEs for which Ubuntu has published a fix), but PersonalSOC 0.1.13 treated every result on an Ubuntu machine as "likely a false positive from Debian's data" and lowered the confidence to hypothesis and the severity to low. That made real vulnerabilities look minor. 0.1.14 does not lower a result whose `ecosystem` is Ubuntu (for example, a CVE in openssl whose fix is published appears as "medium" according to its CVSS, with confidence "confirmed"). It lowers only the results of an old RoamSwitch (1.11.27 and earlier, which also applied Debian's data to Ubuntu machines), and the note now says "Update RoamSwitch to Linux 1.11.28 or later to be matched with Ubuntu's data". As a check, I read the real output of the published RoamSwitch 1.11.28 (in an Ubuntu 24.04 container) with the new PersonalSOC and confirmed that the openssl finding appears as "medium, confirmed", with no note.
- (0.1.14) **Added: a notice when the OS package CVE matching could not be completed.** When RoamSwitch cannot complete the matching (`complete` is false), for example for an unsupported release, an Ubuntu derivative (Linux Mint, Pop!_OS and so on) or data not fetched, PersonalSOC used to show nothing, which looked the same as "no vulnerable packages". It now shows one notice of severity "low" saying "the matching could not be completed", with the reason.
- (0.1.14) **Changed: the order of the Settings tabs now matches AccessScope and PathScope.** "Automatic status check" comes first, then "LLM integration", "Display language" and "Updates" (before, "Updates" came before "Display language"). The Traditional Chinese label for "LLM integration" is now "LLM 串接".
- (0.2.0) **Added: the values the LLM writes are checked against what it was given.** An IP address, domain, CVE ID or hash in the report or the hypotheses that appears nowhere in the log text, the analysis summary or the tool results is listed in the report under "Values not found in the collected material", as a possible invention. Only the strings are compared; the LLM is not asked.
- (0.2.0) **Added: the report ends with an appendix of the investigation results exactly as they were given to the LLM** (secrets masked, hidden characters removed), so a reviewer can read what the LLM read.
- **Fixed: `--llm` with a file now prints the detections first** (`personalsoc sshd <file> --llm name` and the like). Before, it printed only the LLM's draft, although its heading says to check the facts in the detections above. Now it prints the same detections as without `--llm`, then the draft and the list of values not in the collected material. The detections stay even if the LLM fails. (0.2.2)

## RoamSwitch Sensor

Software you install on your own general-purpose hardware (PC, Raspberry
Pi, etc., x86_64/arm64) — a separate product: pairing-code-based mutual
trust (fixed IP + short-lived code) with RoamSwitch-equipped endpoints
(Mac / Linux Client / Server Edition) for active vulnerability audits,
plus detection of new devices and spoofing on the LAN. Still under
development — see
<https://lafine.net/roamswitch-sensor-manual.html> for current status and
setup instructions.

### 1.0.10

Reads NBNS roles and SNMP default communities passively.

- **Added: reads, passively, NBNS roles (an unexpected domain controller) and SNMP default communities.**
- **Added: `audit-unmanaged --plan`.** The all-port scan uses adaptive concurrency, tries the ports that were open last time first, and spaces its probes out.
- **Added: `favicon_md5` as a product-identification marker.** One `GET /favicon.ico` is sent, only to a port that answered.
- **Added: `network-threats` output can be filtered with closed fields, no regular expressions** (`--filter`, `--filter-or`).
- The default is unchanged: `schedule.full_sweep_every_hours` stays 0 (every run scans the whole range).
- Verification: 979 workspace tests pass (Docker; 0 failures).

### 1.0.9

- **Fixed: the notification cooldown (`cooldown_minutes`) did not hold across a daemon restart.** The same audit that was already notified before the restart was notified again afterwards. The cooldown record now carries over a restart.
- Verified: The 944 workspace tests pass (Docker; 0 failures).

### 1.0.8

- **Fixed: English `diff` used the plural even for one newly opened port ("newly open ports 80").** One port now reads "newly open port 80", and two or more "newly open ports 80,443". The count of "masked answer summaries" in the LLM explanation, and "and N more" after the KEV CVEs in French and Italian, now use the form that matches the number too.
- **Fixed: `--version` of `roamswitch-sensor-daemon`, `roamswitch-sensor-collector` and `roamswitch-sensor-tui` started the program itself instead of printing the version.** They now print "<name> <version>" and exit.
- Checks: 943 workspace tests pass (Docker, 0 failures).

### 1.0.7

- **Fixed: on a newly installed Sensor, the control API's TLS dropped from required to optional at the restart after the first pairing.** The same happened after unpairing (an empty `trusted_endpoints.json` stays behind). Only a Sensor that paired devices with an old version is now moved to the old settings. A Sensor installed with the previous version and already paired cannot be told apart from an old one, so it stays optional. Run `sudo roamswitch-sensor config set control.tls required` and restart the daemon.
- **Fixed: on a Sensor with nmap, the pairing answer waited for the nmap that looks up the device's MAC.** Without DNS it took 13 seconds, so `sensor pair` on the device failed at 10 seconds while the Sensor had registered the device as trusted. The answer is now sent first and the MAC looked up afterwards (the nmap that looks up one device's MAC gets `-n` and a 5-second timeout). For a few seconds after pairing, the device's record has no MAC.
- **Fixed: fetching KEV and sending to the collector followed redirects.** Fetching KEV refuses a redirect to anything but https and follows up to 3. Sending to the collector follows no redirect and treats a 3xx as a failed send. The device list and the check results do not flow to the redirect target, and what could not be sent is not marked as sent.
- **Fixed: the daemon's journal contained color codes (ESC[2m and the like).** No color is added when the output is not a terminal (`NO_COLOR` and `TERM=dumb` are honored too).
- Checks: 941 workspace tests pass (Docker, 0 failures).

### 1.0.6

- **Added: a notification when the daemon or the collector exits abnormally and systemd starts it again.** The daemon sends one notification (high) and one operation-log entry; the collector sends one to the channels of notify set. A crash loop is bundled into one an hour with the count. When either stops responding for 30 seconds, systemd ends and restarts it. Nothing is sent for `systemctl restart`, an update or a stop.
- **Changed: when the settings are broken at start, the last settings read correctly (`config.json.last-good`) are used.**
- **Fixed: a Sensor past its offline limit (`lockdown_after_offline_days`) locked down right after start without ever sending to the collector.** For 10 minutes after start it no longer locks down even past the limit, and sends to the collector on every tick. When a send is accepted, the limit counts again from then; when nothing gets through, it locks down as before (a stolen Sensor also runs for up to 10 minutes after start).
- **Changed: the SSH banner is no longer counted as a finding.** An audit of a device with only sshd says "no problems were found", with the banner on an ℹ line. New audits no longer raise a "finding" notification for it. Earlier records are read the same way.
- **Fixed: the display language.** With `language` set, the CLI follows it too (the locale when empty). The audit log (`audit-log`, the TUI, the HTML report), finding titles, descriptions and recommendations, CLI and TUI errors and `config set` errors are shown in the reader's language (earlier records and CVE findings keep the text they were recorded with). Results of audits requested by RoamSwitch carry keys so the requesting device can render them in its display language. Notifications to webhooks, syslog and the collector are in the Sensor's language, with word forms that match the number. The collector's CLI and notifications are in the host's language, and its dashboard is available in 10 languages.
- **Changed: the material given to the LLM is in English** (`design-hypotheses --show-prompt`). Secrets are masked as `[REDACTED]` in every language.
- **Fixed: when the Sensor refused a send (not registered and the like), the sender saw "connection reset".** It now answers 401 and the like. When RoamSwitch pairing requires TLS, a "TLS required" answer arrives as well.
- **Changed (affects scripts):** `informational` is added to records, collector sends and control API results, `error_msg` to IPC replies, and `msg` and `args` to audit-log records. `network-threats --json --lang ja` fills `summary_text` (without `--lang`, the JSON is as before). The notification of newly opened ports reads "web-01: 1 newly open port: 6379", without the `[6379]` brackets. Some English validation errors now read like `must be between 1 and 720`.
- Checks: the 720 tests of sensor-daemon and sensor-core pass (Docker).

### 1.0.5

- **Changed: a freshly installed Sensor no longer sends probes into the LAN.** Until now, the inventory refresh right after start and every hour sent an ARP sweep (`nmap -sn -PR`) across the whole directly connected subnet when nmap was present, and the DHCP probe and IPv6 router solicitation were on by default. nmap is installed as a recommended package, so `apt install` alone reached the whole LAN before the user had chosen anything. `inventory.active_sweep` is added and, with `dhcp.active_probe` and `ipv6.active_probe`, all three are off by default. By default the Sensor only reads the ARP, neighbor and routing tables and runs passive capture, and it no longer sends an ICMP echo to the gateway at start (the gateway is pinged only with `inventory.active_sweep` on). `status` (CLI and TUI) has a new "probes into the LAN: off/on" line.
- **Changed: on upgrade, probes that were saved as on under the earlier default are turned off once.** With the earlier versions, using `config set` even once saved the default "on" of the probes to `config.json`. In a settings file without the marker, those of the three that are on are turned off, with a notification (`probes_off_by_upgrade`, medium) and an operation-log entry. `status` says "probes were turned off by the upgrade" with the command to turn them on. Probes the user turns on after the upgrade are left alone.
- **Fixed: postinst said "started successfully" even when the service could not start.** It uses `deb-systemd-invoke` when present (following policy-rc.d) and reports only after checking whether the service actually started. It also says the probes are off by default.
- **Fixed: auditing again after changing the language made `diff` count "1 new, 1 resolved".** Findings now store a language-independent rule id and the language, and are compared by those (older records are still compared by title). `report` and `history` say in one line when a record's language differs from the reader's. Scheduled audit texts are in the Sensor's language instead of always English.
- **Fixed: `scan` said it also ran the nmap NSE checks when nmap was missing.** The English "run::" of `issue-code` and its `--addr`, which could be unreachable, are fixed too (other addresses and interfaces are listed). A Japanese lockdown refusal no longer carries the daemon's English details.
- **Fixed: `roamswitch-sensor-collector enroll` did not check the shape of the public key.** It refuses anything but a base64 32-byte Ed25519 public key.
- **Changed (affects scripts):** `unpair`, like `scan`, `history`, `diff`, `report` and `export --endpoint`, accepts a prefix of the public key. When nothing or more than one matches, the exit code is 1 (some commands returned 0). `--help` describes each command, and `--version` is added. An unknown command is reported on standard error with exit code 2. Piping the output to `head` no longer prints a panic message (the CLI and the collector's one-shot commands).
- **Changed: the license is now stated as Proprietary to match the actual terms, and the deb and rpm ship the Sensor terms of use (EULA)** (`/usr/share/doc/roamswitch-sensor/copyright`).
- Checks: with the bundled roamswitch-core moved to 1.17.37, all workspace tests pass (592 in sensor-core and others, 0 failed).

### 1.0.4

- **Added: a check for gaps between a published specification and the actual responses, and for boundary values (`design_flaw`).** For paired devices only, it compares what a published API specification (OpenAPI / Swagger) declares with what the device actually answers, and reports a declared path that returns 404 or a response that the specification does not declare (it does not call this a "vulnerability"). It also sends each declared query parameter one safe value at a time from a closed set in the rule data, and reports only 5xx responses, a sharp change in response size, and stack-trace-like text as "anomalies". It sends read-only (GET) requests only, at most 20 per device. The few boundary-value requests go first to parameters whose names suggest identifiers or numeric bounds (`id`, `page`, `limit`), then to search and sort names, then to the rest, and every parameter gets its first value before any gets a second. The order depends only on the parameter names, is the same every time, and no AI chooses it. It is off by default, runs only when `design_flaw.enabled` is turned on, and keeps the same two-step consent as the active audit. It is never used on unmanaged devices.
- **Added: an AI tool can write unverified leads (optional).** The result keeps a short summary of each answer (request line, status, server name, type, length, and an excerpt of at most 240 characters with secrets masked). `roamswitch-sensor design-hypotheses` (in the TUI, `h` on a History row) hands only these summaries to the AI tool chosen in `design_flaw.llm.tool` (`claude`, `opencode`, `codex` or `agy`) and has it write leads such as "this API may show one account another's data". It is started the same way as `--llm` in AccessScope, PathScope and PersonalSOC (no shell, an empty temporary folder, limits on time and output). `claude` is started with its own tools switched off; `opencode`, `codex` and `agy` are not shown to be lockable, and the confirmation says so. **The daemon never starts it.** It runs with the rights of whoever runs the command, so as root it runs with root's rights (to avoid that, hand the text printed by `--show-prompt` to the tool as a normal user). The tool's provider may receive the summaries, and the confirmation and the command's output say so. A lead that points at a request this run did not send is dropped, and the rest are only shown as unverified: the Sensor checks nothing and sends nothing to any device. The TUI settings screen has fields for the tool, the model name and the seconds to wait (10 languages).
- **Added: display and notification of the results.** They appear in the CLI, the TUI (10 languages, including the settings screen), the scan record (JSON), Markdown and HTML reports and exports, and the diff from the previous scan. A newly found gap or anomaly is sent as a notification (`audit_design_flaw`, severity high) and counts toward "changed since the last scan" (the first audit has no diff). The CSV export does not include it yet.
- **Changed: the rules of the product check and the no-authentication check now live in versioned rule data (JSON).** 15 products and 9 kinds of checks were moved over without adding any. The kinds of request are a closed list, there is no field for headers or credentials, and the ports are a fixed set. Paths allow only letters, digits and `/ _ . - ~`; regular expressions have no backtracking, at most 64 per file, and at most 32 MiB of matching per device. A rule file that exceeds a limit is refused as a whole and the built-in rules are used. Signed replacement is supported (a file with a bad signature, one that is not newer than the built-in rules, a rollback to an older version, a link, or an oversized file is refused, and the reason is logged once in the audit log as `probe_rules_rejected`), and the rule version, its origin and the rule id are kept in the records (JSON and CSV), in `unmanaged-audits --detail` and in the TUI device detail (10 languages).
- **Changed: a redirect that a device returns is no longer followed when the target is a path with a query.** The specification (`docs/PROBE_RULES.md`) already said queries are not followed, so the code was brought in line with it.
- **Fixed: when the scan task of a scheduled audit crashed, an empty "clean" result was recorded in the history.** It now writes `audit_failed` to the audit log, sends a notification (`audit_failed`, severity medium), and records no result.
- **Changed: the bundled roamswitch-core now matches Linux 1.17.24.** An internal change for the components that `UpdateComponents` gained (`node_runtime_cve_map` and `epss_scores`); the Sensor fetches neither.
- Checks: the workspace tests pass (869 tests; Docker).

### 0.2.1 - 1.0.3 (summarized)

The full text of each item is in this file's git history.

- (1.0.3) **Changed: the bundled roamswitch-core now matches 1.17.12.** It is an internal change only, to follow `kev_catalog` being added to the Linux edition's list of update data (`UpdateComponents`); Sensor does not fetch the KEV list. Sensor's features and settings are unchanged from 1.0.2.
- (1.0.3) Checks: 779 workspace tests pass (Docker). `cargo audit` reports 0 vulnerabilities.
- (1.0.2) **Added: passive capture reads VLAN tags (802.1Q / 802.1ad)** (the tag that Linux strips is received through `PACKET_AUXDATA`). The VLAN is recorded in the device inventory. IPv6 flows also enter behavior learning (in units of /24 for IPv4 and /56 for IPv6). The model is read from LLDP, mDNS TXT and WS-Discovery. The receive buffer can be set with `capture.rcvbuf_mb`, and truncation is counted only when it reaches the part that is read.
- (1.0.2) **Added: the audit of paired endpoints was widened.** Non-standard ports are audited from the ports and banners that the endpoint reports about itself. An endpoint behind a router gets a periodic audit only when a signed request, no ARP answer, and no change before and after the audit all hold. A released endpoint gets `unpaired_by_sensor`.
- (1.0.2) **Added: the light audit of devices without RoamSwitch was widened.** `noauth_check` now covers etcd, CouchDB, Kubelet, ZooKeeper and Ollama, and `product_check` covers QNAP, Hikvision, Dahua and SonicWall. FTP and SSH banners are also used to decide the version.
- (1.0.2) **Added: the collector gathers the audit results of devices without RoamSwitch and a summary of behavior learning, and notifies about an interruption, an abnormal audit log, and roaming between sites.**
- (1.0.2) **Added: `lan_overview` and `lan_threats` (IPC), and `roamswitch-sensor lan-overview` and `network-threats` (CLI).** They are read-only and can be called without root. PersonalSOC takes them in.
- (1.0.2) **Fixed (three false positives found by running it on a real LAN):**
  - A public IP (arriving with the router's MAC) was recorded as a "new device", and the learned list kept growing. Only sources with a private, link-local or CGNAT address are counted.
  - For every packet of the same connection, an IoT-behavior detection and a malicious-IP contact were recorded, and one connection filled the 200-slot retention. A (source, destination, port) triple is recorded once per hour.
  - The outbound flood detection counted per packet, so a single `ssh` or `scp` raised "high". It counts only when the (destination, source port) pair is new.
  - On the real LAN the same operation went from 97 detections before the fix to 1 after it.
- (1.0.2) **Changed: the description of `collector.send_unmanaged_audits` now matches what is actually sent.** Besides services that answered without authentication, services that asked for authentication are also sent, with their state (10 languages, README, docs).
- (1.0.2) The translations in the 10 languages were reviewed (for example, French `poste` and `terminal`, and Italian `dispositivo` and `endpoint`, were aligned so that they are not confused with a device).
- (1.0.2) Checks: the 779 workspace tests pass. The daemon of the new version was run, with passive capture only, on a real LAN (Ubuntu 24.04).
- (1.0.1) **Added: the light audit can check whether a service answers without authentication (`unmanaged_audit.noauth_check`, off by default).** The light audit only checked that a port was open, so it could not tell a Redis open without authentication from one protected by a password. When on, it sends one read-only request each to Redis (6379), Memcached (11211), Docker API (2375) and Elasticsearch (9200) that were found open (`PING`, `stats`, `GET /_ping`, `GET /`). It runs only under the same consent as the light audit (`enabled`, `authorization`, `allowed_cidrs`, `exclude`). It never writes, changes settings, sends `AUTH`, or sends or guesses passwords. The answer is one of `open` / `auth_required` / `refused` / `inconclusive`, and a case that cannot be judged is called neither "open" nor "protected". `open` becomes `no_auth` (high) and `auth_required` becomes `auth_checked` (medium). MongoDB is not checked, because the command that tells whether authentication is required is itself a query for data.
- (1.0.1) **Changed: the first DHCP server found is checked against the default gateway before it is learned.** Before, "the first server that answered is learned as the legitimate one", so a network that only ever had a rogue server, or a rogue server that answered before the real one, was remembered as legitimate. When the default gateway is known, the first server is learned only if it is the gateway itself (same MAC or IP) or hands out the gateway as its router. Anything else is not learned and is reported as `unverified_first` (critical when it hands out another router, as a hijack of the traffic path); register a legitimate one in `dhcp.allowed_servers`. When no comparison is possible (the gateway is unknown, an answer on another interface, DHCPv6 and so on), the first server is learned as before. **A rogue server that hands out the same router as the real gateway and only rewrites the DNS cannot be told apart from a real second server from outside. Use `dhcp.allowed_servers` to pin it.** A baseline learned before this change is not judged again.
- (1.0.1) **Added: the Sensor deb and rpm are now built for arm64 too** (Raspberry Pi and others). The README and the description mentioned arm64, but the deb and rpm were amd64 only. The rpm release now carries the distribution name (`fedora` / `opensuse`) and both are built (the release stayed `1` and went to `rpm/1/`, so `dnf install roamswitch-sensor` did not work).
- (1.0.1) **Changed: the deb and rpm now depend on the `ip` command (`iproute2` / `iproute`).** `ip` is used for the LAN range and for the IPv6 neighbor table and default route. Without `ip`, detection of a rogue IPv6 router becomes "not visible". nmap stays recommended.
- (1.0.1) **Changed: dependencies were updated** (tokio 1.54.1 for advisory RUSTSEC-2026-0386, and the yanked `yoke-derive`). The README now explains installation with apt and dnf, arm64, and the rpm variant.
- **The version is now 1.0.0** (1.0.0): it moved to 1.0.0 together with PersonalSOC, PathScope and AccessScope.
- **An automatic feature that had stopped showed up neither in `status` nor in a notification** (1.0.0): the periodic audit, the periodic audit of unlisted devices and the automatic update of the device list (finding quiet devices) silently did nothing when nmap was not installed, when there was no address to audit, or when there was no identity binding (nmap is a recommended package, so this happened on hosts installed without it). The reason is now recorded as a condition that lasts until it is resolved, and `roamswitch-sensor status` shows it as a warning under "What is visible". If a notification destination is configured, one `coverage_gap` arrives, and `coverage_restored` arrives when it is fixed. `status` also shows whether nmap is installed and the state of the automatic features (CLI and TUI, 10 languages). A missing nmap has severity medium (it is notified). Detection thresholds and defaults are unchanged.
- **Notifications that had stopped arriving could not be noticed** (1.0.0): for each webhook and syslog destination it now keeps the last delivery result (success or failure, the time, the error). While failures continue, `notify_delivery_failed` appears in `status` and in notifications. The result of `roamswitch-sensor notify-test` goes to the same place, and the record clears when a delivery succeeds or `notify-test` passes.
- **An audit that did not reach the device, and an empty list, were shown as "no problem"** (1.0.0): when an audit finds no open port and gets no ARP reply either, it now says "may not have reached the device". The nmap NSE line tells "not run (nmap is not installed)" from "no match", and the "no events" display of network-threats carries a note about what is not visible.
- **A passive-capture setting took effect only after a restart** (1.0.0): after adding the environment variable you need `sudo systemctl restart roamswitch-sensor`; `systemctl edit` alone does not reach the running daemon. This was not written anywhere, and the unit name `roamswitch-sensor-daemon` in the setting's description does not exist. The name is now `roamswitch-sensor` (10 languages), and the README and the service file state the restart. Check that it took effect in `roamswitch-sensor status`: "passive capture: receiving on eth0".
- **Control API and pairing hardened** (0.3.14): signatures gained a v3 bound to the Sensor's public key (sent by Mac 1.10.17 and later and Linux 1.10.18 and later), and a new install refuses v1 and, on a plaintext connection, v2. We tested that a downgrade from v3 is refused. When pairing, the client sends a proof of possession of its key (PoP, `control.require_pairing_pop`) so that, over a connection without a certificate pin, nobody can register another party's public key (confirmed on a real Mac). A new install defaults to required TLS, and a corrupted configuration file keeps the previous configuration. The local IPC is authorized by the uid and groups seen at connect time (fixing a race on a reused PID), and write operations and active scans are limited to root or the `roamswitch-sensor` group. A read timeout, limits on requests and simultaneous connections, a per-IP lockout, a per-key nonce limit and an expiry for pending audits were added, and `last_addr` is updated only over TLS, with changes recorded.
- **Collector hardened** (0.3.14): an endless pre-authentication body read is now bounded by a 32 MiB shared read budget and a 20-second deadline per body, and a stalled, timed-out or short body counts as a failure for the source IP. Per-IP connection limits, trusted-proxy support, replay rejection, a push limit, a default retention and streaming body reads were added, and new `http://` settings are refused. The push signature gained a v2 bound to the receiving collector, which stops a replay to another collector (`--collector-id` or ROAMSWITCH_COLLECTOR_ID on the collector, `collector.collector_id` on the Sensor; `--require-collector-binding` refuses unbound pushes).
- **Secret exposure and persistence fixed** (0.3.14): a failed webhook notification could put the URL, which carries a secret token, into the error text; only the kind of error is shown now. State files are written atomically and a corrupted one is moved aside, the private key is created exclusively with mode 0600, control characters are stripped, and a failed append to the audit log is reported. The TUI settings screen has rows for the settings above (10 languages).
- **Audit report dates** (0.3.13): HTML and Markdown reports showed raw UTC strings such as `2026-09-19T08:00:03.415602838+00:00`; they now show this machine's local time in each language's order, with the weekday and the UTC offset (English: "Sat, 2026-09-19 17:00:03 (UTC+9)"). The "Generated (UTC)" heading is now "Generated". CSV is meant for machines and stays in UTC.

- **Better network inventory** (0.2.1 to 0.2.7): always-on tracking of the network
  layout, plus extended detection (botnet/DDoS participation, DNS tunneling). An active
  ARP sweep equivalent to `nmap -sn` closed detection gaps (and a bug that dropped the
  Sensor itself from the list was fixed), and the network layout and ARP events were
  reworked to carry enough information to act on (refreshed hourly). Device notes were
  added, and TUI display bugs were fixed.
- **Audit operations features for security teams at larger organizations** (0.3.0):
  scheduled audits, diffs against the previous run, notifications (webhook and syslog),
  a tamper-evident operation log, export, retention, and a central collector that
  aggregates several Sensors. Everything is opt-in, and nothing is sent anywhere by
  default.
- **Fixed automatic updates of the CVE maps and the NSE script DB failing silently**
  under systemd's write restrictions (0.3.1 to 0.3.2).
- **Added a diff tab, an operation-log tab and export to the TUI** (0.3.4).
- **Broader active audits** (0.3.5): Elasticsearch, CouchDB, VNC, RDP and SMB added.
- **Slow ARP sweeps detected** (0.3.8): a long window notifies once 64 distinct targets are reached within 24 hours (a 2.5 s-per-address sweep fired at the 64th target). Requests for hosts known to be alive do not count toward it, and the number of tracked MAC addresses, targets per MAC and the live-host table are capped, so a flood of spoofed sources cannot grow memory without bound.
- **Repository size** (0.3.7): the apt and rpm repositories had kept every past version, so they were cut to the latest one (1.10.5 and later keep the latest three).
- **Control API TLS support, replay protection, and a fuller TUI** (0.3.9): the control API now speaks TLS 1.3 with certificate pinning (`control.tls`, default `optional`). Signed requests carry a timestamp (±300s) and a one-time value, with pairing brute force locked out per source and globally. The TUI now covers every function of the CLI and daemon (config editing, TLS management, manual pairing, event views, 10 languages). Added a bind-address setting, a switch to stop the CVE-map fetch, and a distinction between unpaired and unknown devices.
- **Pairing command guidance fixed** (0.3.10): the pairing command shown by the TUI and `sensor-cli issue-code` did not include the Sensor's own fingerprint (`--fingerprint`), so it always failed with Linux 1.9.90 and later clients. This is fixed.
- **Manual pairing screen explanation improved** (0.3.11): it now says that the fingerprint field is for the GUI (Mac) input field.
- **False-positive and alert-fatigue audit** (0.3.12): fixed 6 issues, including detections that spike in count (destination spread, destination concentration flood, malicious-destination contact, IoT behavior) sending an empty destination identity, so that the same kind of detection on another device was taken to be in cooldown and silently suppressed.
- **Data-protection fix** (0.3.15): when a state file could not be read or was corrupt, it was treated as empty and saved, wiping other data (the list of trusted devices, scan history, device inventory, settings, the ARP and passive-monitoring baselines, the audit log, the collector's per-Sensor state and the daemon's send position). Now only a missing file counts as empty; an unreadable file is not rewritten, a copy is kept as `<file>.corrupt-<time>` (0600, newest 3), and changing operations fail with a clear error. If the list of trusted devices cannot be read, nobody is trusted and pairing is refused (`sensor_storage_unavailable`; the pairing code is used up, so issue a new one after repair). Silently regenerating the TLS certificate when the private key was corrupt, and the collector accepting replays when a Sensor's state was corrupt, were fixed too. While a file is corrupt, ARP spoofing detection and new-device detection from passive monitoring stop (they resume after repair or deletion). The new errors are shown in 10 languages in the TUI and in Japanese and English in the CLI.
- **Data-protection and crash-resistance fixes** (0.3.16): the collector's retention prune stopped reading at a single line with invalid UTF-8 and replaced `scans.jsonl` and `events.jsonl` with the shortened content, losing every later record; only the damaged line is skipped now. The collector reported success even when appending a record failed, and since the Sensor did not resend, the record was lost; a failed append now returns a storage error and does not advance the state. After a crash in the middle of an append, the next record was glued onto the torn fragment and became unreadable (the collector's records and the audit log); fixed. In the audit log, one line with invalid UTF-8 hid all later entries and made a sequence number get reused, and an unreadable log "verified" as empty and fine; only the bad line is skipped now, an unreadable log counts as a failed verification, and a new chain is never started. An evidence export came out empty, like a clean "no scans, no devices" report, when a stored file was damaged; it is now aborted with an error (the IPC has a new `strict` parameter that reads strictly; the TUI shows it in 10 languages and the CLI in Japanese and English). `tls rotate` (the key and certificate update) writes the new key and certificate to temporary files, checks that they match and only then puts them in place, and at startup an interrupted rotation is completed or discarded so that the pair is consistent, so a crash in the middle can no longer leave a mismatched pair that stops the Sensor from starting (when it cannot be recovered, the Sensor stops with an error as before and keeps the files).
- **Keeping configuration and data across a downgrade** (0.3.16): when an older version saved a configuration or data written by a newer version, the newer fields were dropped. The configuration, trusted endpoints, scan history, device inventory, the ARP and passive baselines, the collector's per-Sensor state and the push cursor now keep unknown fields when saved (the audit log is unchanged because of its hash chain). When unknown fields are present, the keys of the configuration file are written in alphabetical order. The collector's state, `enrolled` and push-cursor files are written pretty-printed (the content is the same).
- **Safer file writing** (0.3.16): the CLI's `export --out` and `scan report --out` followed symbolic links, kept an existing file's mode, and defaulted to 0644. A symbolic link or a non-regular file is now refused (with the reason shown in Japanese or English), the file is written to a temporary file and then replaced, and the mode is 0600. Under sudo, a link planted in a shared directory could have redirected the write. In the TUI, overwriting a credential file (`collector-token.txt`) did not tighten its mode to 0600, and a crash while creating the identity file could stop the next start; both fixed.
- (0.4.0) **Added: detection of rogue DHCP servers (IPv4 and DHCPv6)**, on by default. Every hour (and on each `refresh`) the Sensor sends a single DHCP discover packet (a SOLICIT for DHCPv6) from a random MAC and counts every server that answers. It sends no request, so no address lease is taken. The first server it hears is learned as the legitimate one, and a server that appears later is reported. A server that hands out a gateway or DNS the legitimate one does not takes over where traffic goes, so it is critical. If several servers answer the first probe at once, none is learned and all are reported. `dhcp.allowed_servers` names the legitimate servers.
- (0.4.0) **Added: IPv6 detection and support**, on by default. It reports router advertisements from anything other than the IPv4 gateway, an IPv6 address changing hands (NDP spoofing), and devices visible only over IPv6. The device inventory now holds IPv6 addresses (the `ipv6` CSV column, `ipv6_addrs` in JSON). The control API (50543) can also listen on IPv6 with `control.listen_ipv6` (off by default, applied on restart). Connection limits and lockouts count IPv6 per /64. A network whose IPv6 router is a different device from its IPv4 gateway is a normal case, so list that router in `ipv6.allowed_routers`.
- (0.4.0) **Added: a light audit of devices without RoamSwitch**, opt-in and off by default. Only for devices inside the ranges the operator authorized, it makes TCP connections to a fixed set of 54 ports and reads what the services announce themselves. It does not attempt logins, exploit vulnerabilities, run nmap NSE or scan every port; deep diagnostics in the style of OpenVAS are done only to endpoints that agreed (paired). To enable it, first set `unmanaged_audit.authorization` (who approved it) and `unmanaged_audit.allowed_cidrs` (the ranges; IPv4 wider than /16 and IPv6 wider than /48 are refused). The device's MAC is checked before and after, and the run stops if the address changed hands. The authorization text is written to the operation log and the exports with every run. New: `audit-unmanaged <IP|MAC> --confirm` and `unmanaged-audits`, and in the TUI an "Unmanaged audits" tab and the `a` key.
- (0.4.0) **Added: revocation and lockdown for a Sensor that is lost, stolen or retired.** After `revoke --reason` at the collector, that Sensor's uploads are refused with `403 sensor_revoked` (given only to requests with a valid signature), and the Sensor stops itself on its next upload. Locked down, it stops pairing, audits, uploads to the collector, notifications and probing of the network, and the control API answers only `sensor_locked_down`. The stop is stored in a file, so it survives a restart, and it stays in force if that file cannot be read. `security.on_revoked` chooses `lockdown` (default), `wipe` (overwrite with zeros and delete the keys, settings, paired endpoints, audit results and inventory, keeping only the operation log) or `none`. `security.lockdown_after_offline_days` stops a Sensor that has not reached the collector for that long (off by default). New: `lockdown on|off|status`, `decommission --confirm` and the `K` key in the TUI. `revoke` and `decommission` print the endpoints that were paired with the Sensor and how to unpair them.
- (0.4.0) **Note: the endpoints that were paired with a revoked Sensor keep trusting it until each one removes the pairing.** A Sensor has no way to notify them (Linux: `roamswitch sensor unpair <this Sensor's public key>`; Mac: Settings > Sensor > Unpair). A Sensor carried off while powered down never receives the instruction to stop; disk encryption is the real protection.
- (0.4.0) **Added: an `unmanaged` table in the evidence export** (CSV) and a light-audit section in the JSON and HTML reports. The inventory CSV has an IPv6 column. The collector dashboard lists revoked Sensors.
- (0.4.0) **Verified with real packets.** On a throw-away Docker LAN, 35 checks cover DHCP and DHCPv6 servers, a rogue router advertisement, NDP spoofing, the light audit, the IPv6 listener, and revocation, lockdown, wiping and the offline stop (`scripts/lab/run.py`). It has not yet been checked under a real systemd (.deb) or on a physical switch. DHCP probing does not work on a Sensor connected over Wi-Fi; wired operation is assumed.
- (0.4.1) **New: detecting changes from each device's usual behavior** (on by default, `behavior.enabled`). The Sensor learns the hourly behavior of each device (identified by MAC, grouped by the existing device class) and reports unusual changes. It looks at bytes sent to public addresses, the number of public address ranges contacted for the first time, the number of LAN peers, DNS queries, data-carrying packets, and the ports used (including admin ports the device has never used). After the learning period (14 days by default, `behavior.learning_days`) it compares each hour with the past values of the same weekday-or-weekend hour (median and spread) and explains in words what changed compared with usual, such as "this camera used port 22 for the first time" or "outbound volume is 40 times the usual". Severity is low or medium, and **this finding alone never triggers lockdown or any other automatic blocking**. Sensitivity is `behavior.sensitivity` (low / normal / high).
- (0.4.1) What it learns is stored in a state file (0600). Destination addresses are not stored, only a keyed Bloom filter. A new device that is still learning borrows the history of devices of the same class and reports only large changes. General-purpose devices such as PCs and phones are left out by default because their behavior changes by the hour (`behavior.include_general_purpose`). The CLI gained `behavior [--detail]`, `behavior reset <MAC>` and `network-threats`; the TUI gained a "Behavior baseline" tab. TUI text is in 10 languages and the CLI in Japanese and English.
- (0.4.1) **Measured effect**: measured on simulated devices (cameras, printers, plugs, speakers, NAS boxes, IoT modules) and simulated anomalies. It has not been checked on real devices or real traffic. False alarms were about 0.05 to 0.09 per device per week at normal (about 0.44 for NAS boxes, caused by cloud-sync days). Detection at normal was 100% for a new port, 97% for LAN fan-out, 94% for a DNS surge, 83% for 3 a.m. activity and 100% for a surge of new destinations. Outbound volume increases were caught only 40% of the time (10%, 35% and 76% at 3x, 10x and 40x the usual) and are harder to see on low-traffic devices. A slowly growing upload was caught during the ramp 68% of the time.
- (0.4.1) **Notes**: the thresholds were fitted to the simulated noise, so a real LAN may produce more false alarms; if so, set `behavior.sensitivity` to `low`. A device that was already compromised while it was learning has that behavior as its baseline. A Sensor on an access port sees only broadcast and multicast traffic. IPv6 and daylight-saving changes were not checked.
- (0.4.2) **New: the light audit of unmanaged devices can now check whether a device is a product with vulnerabilities in the CISA KEV catalog** (`unmanaged_audit.product_check`, **off by default**). It reads the product name from the web page a device normally returns and tells you whether that product has KEV-listed vulnerabilities. Covered: Fortinet FortiGate, Ivanti Connect Secure, Citrix NetScaler, Cisco (the ASA SSL VPN and the IOS XE web UI), UniFi OS, GeoVision, TerraMaster, Check Point Security Gateway and MikroTik RouterOS (Aruba devices have no entry in the KEV and are not covered).
- (0.4.2) **All it sends is a `GET /` to the web ports that accepted a connection (80, 8080, 8181, 443, 8443, 10443), the redirects the device itself points to at the same address (at most two), and a read of the HTTPS certificate's issuer and subject names.** It sends no logins, no password attempts, no guessed paths and nothing that exploits a vulnerability. The limit is 12 requests per device, 150 ms apart. The consent conditions (`enabled`, `authorization`, `allowed_cidrs`, `exclude`, the exclusion of paired devices and the MAC re-check) are those of the light audit, and nothing is sent while the light audit is off.
- (0.4.2) **Verdicts**: a product is named only when its on-screen markers all appear in the same response. It is reported as affected (high) only when the version the device displays can be read and falls in an NVD affected range; when the version cannot be read it stays a notice (medium): this product has KEV-listed vulnerabilities, the version could not be read, so it is unconfirmed. Today only MikroTik RouterOS shows its version, so other products normally get the unconfirmed notice.
- (0.4.2) A copy of the KEV ships with the product and is refreshed, only when both `product_check` and `updates.fetch_cve_map` are on, by fetching one public CISA feed file every 24 hours (nothing about your LAN is sent).
- (0.4.2) **Limits**: the markers are based on public detection signatures and have not been checked against real devices; a marker that does not match errs on the side of detecting nothing. The traffic with and without consent was checked against dummy web servers in Docker.
- (0.4.2) The new texts are in 10 languages (based on machine translation, not reviewed by native speakers). The design notes are in `docs/SENSOR.md`.
- (0.4.2) Fixed: the update-settings initializer now matches the latest RoamSwitch for Linux core (it no longer built after the Web Rules fields were added).
- **Following the core** (0.4.3): the update settings are initialised again to match RoamSwitch for Linux core 1.12.7, which added the Ubuntu package CVE map. Sensor does not fetch that map, just like the other package CVE maps. Sensor's network behaviour is unchanged.
- (0.4.4) **Added: the Sensor now counts what it could not see and shows it in `status` and in notifications.** Before, not seeing something left either one log line or nothing at all, and could not be told apart from "nothing was found". What is counted: passive capture that could not be opened (before, only a log line, and passive monitoring stopped silently), receive errors (before, a busy loop, so it now waits 100 ms), frames cut off above 2048 bytes (detected with `MSG_TRUNC`), packets the kernel dropped (`PACKET_STATISTICS`), frames that could not be parsed, and checks that failed (the ARP table, the IPv6 neighbour table and routes, the baseline files). It shows in `status` of the CLI and TUI, in the push to the collector, and on the collector's screen.
- (0.4.4) **Fixed: an unreadable ARP table was treated as an empty table.** An empty table could replace the baseline and blind the spoofing check for that cycle. A cycle that could not be read is now skipped and counted. The IPv6 neighbour table and routes (when `ip -6` fails) are likewise counted instead of looking empty.
- (0.4.4) **Added: it tells a quiet capture from a broken one.** The capture loop wakes every second, so a stale wake-up time means it is stuck. A capture that is quiet because the link is down is reported as a loss (`capture_silent` after 5 quiet minutes, `capture_link_down` when the link is down). The Sensor's own transmissions are counted separately, so a deaf capture cannot look alive because it hears itself.
- (0.4.4) **Added: events for the start and end of a gap** (`coverage_gap`, `coverage_restored`). They go only to the notification destinations the operator has already configured. Notes about quiet stretches stay on screen and are not notified. **No new network traffic is added.** The push to the collector gains the items `capture` and `coverage_notes` (additions only, length-limited, control characters removed).
- (0.4.4) Checked with: a lab using real packets, real webhook delivery and a downed link (`scripts/lab/coverage.py`, 11 checks), and the existing lab (`run.py`); both pass. A capture that stayed quiet for over 5 minutes was reported as `capture_silent`, and then taking the link down gave `capture_link_down`. On a Docker bridge it reported that the kernel dropped 263 of 1484 packets (about 15%), which looks like real loss under bursty traffic; the receive buffer was not changed. On a network without IPv6, the IPv6 check now shows up as a skipped check (before, only a debug log line).
- (0.4.4) Limits: the part where the collector silently skips broken lines is unchanged this time because it would change the shape of its responses. The Sensor was built and tested only on Linux (it does not build on macOS). The screens were checked through tests only, not on a real display. The TUI texts (12) are in 10 languages (other than Japanese and English they are based on machine translation and have not been reviewed by native speakers).
- (0.4.4) The RoamSwitch for Linux core it depends on is now 1.12.8 (`Cargo.lock`).
- **The collector counts and shows what it could not read from its history files** (0.4.5): in the scan history (`scans.jsonl`) and the event history (`events.jsonl`), files that cannot be opened, broken lines, the position where a read error stopped the read, and failures of the retention pass are shown on the dashboard and in the startup log. `/api/overview` gained `storage_health` (an added key). No new network traffic is added.
- **Fix: webhook delivery no longer follows redirects and treats them as a failure**. The destination is a URL the operator typed in, so a 3xx response never sends events to another host. `ureq` returns a 3xx as a success once redirects are off, so it is now converted to a failure. Tests confirm that a redirect target listening on another port is never contacted and that a 204 counts as success. (0.4.6)
- **Change: the "please wait" wording is removed from the notice shown while a scan is running** (TUI text, 10 languages). (0.4.6)
- Follows the RoamSwitch for Linux core 1.14.0 (`Cargo.lock`). Sensor is built and tested on Linux only. (0.4.6)


### 0.2.0

- **Added: IoT-device-security-focused ARP events, in four tiers.**
  Previously an ARP event was just a bare MAC/IP diff with nothing
  actionable in it.
  - Tier 1: MAC-vendor classification against a curated, verified subset
    of the IEEE OUI registry (camera/smart-plug/etc. categories), a
    persistent per-device inventory (`sensor-cli device-inventory`), and
    detection of a single MAC issuing ARP requests for an unusual number
    of distinct IPs (possible LAN-internal reconnaissance).
  - Tier 2: reads self-announced mDNS/SSDP/DHCP identifiers (extends the
    existing passive packet-capture path) for more precise, model-level
    device identification than the OUI alone.
  - Tier 3: cross-references classified IoT devices' traffic against
    known-malicious destinations and admin-port contact on other LAN
    devices, with device context attached (`sensor-cli iot-events`,
    requires passive-capture to be configured).
  - Tier 4: a small, hand-verified reference table of real public CVEs
    for a handful of IoT vendors (e.g. Hikvision camera auth-bypass/RCE
    flaws) via `sensor-cli iot-advisories` — static, not a
    network-refreshed feed; that's out of scope for this release.
  - All four tiers are detection/reference only — Sensor never blocks
    traffic. Device classification is also shown inline in the
    `sensor-tui` ARP events tab.

### 0.1.0 - 0.1.7

- **Distributed as deb/rpm packages** (0.1.0), running as a systemd service. Extended
  passive LAN visibility (opt-in) was added too.
- **Pairing moved from mDNS discovery to a pairing code** (0.1.3). mDNS works only
  within one LAN segment, broadcasts constantly, and has no strong authentication by
  default. The Sensor sits at a fixed IP and issues a one-time code (8 characters,
  expires in 10 minutes), authenticated with Ed25519 signatures. Accepting audit
  requests from clients and making the nmap NSE supplementary scan always-on came in
  the same release.
- **Audit status badges for paired endpoints, and viewing the latest report** (0.1.4).
  Manual pairing was removed in favor of the code method, and the IP address is always
  shown.
- **Fixes** (0.1.5 to 0.1.7): several audits running in parallel against the same
  endpoint; the daemon crashing on `Too many open files` and losing in-flight audits
  (the file descriptor limit is now 65536); the detailed description missing from
  audit findings.

## RoamSwitch for Linux

The Linux edition (systemd + nftables), distributed via apt / dnf / zypper
(GPG‑signed). See <https://lafine.net/linux>.

### 2.0.0

The Linux and Mac editions are both 2.0.0. The diagnostic score now shows the distance to the strictest settings preset, outgoing-scan detection no longer writes to the kernel log, and the Client detects more things at the moment they happen instead of on a timer.

- **Changed (Client): the diagnostic score is counted against the "Maximum security" preset as the ideal.** A protection that the settings presets switch counts as passed when it is on and as failed when it is off. A protection that an existing item already checks is not counted twice, and protections that need Pro or whose prerequisites are not met are not applicable. A new item, "Gap to the preset" (`preset_gap`, the 34th on Client), is not part of the score; it lists the difference between the selected preset and "Maximum security". A lower score with a lighter preset is the correct behavior.
- **Changed: detection of scans from this machine to internal networks (off by default, notification only) reads NFLOG instead of the kernel log.** Nothing is written to the kernel log or journald any more; the daemon counts in memory. The same destination, port and account is recorded once per 240 seconds, so a repeated connection no longer fills the log, and a new destination is never hidden by it. The rules use NFLOG groups 4911 (output hook) and 4912 (egress hook, Linux 5.16 or later; it also sees raw frames such as masscan's). If another program (ulogd or similar) already uses a group, the rules are not loaded, the reason is logged, and the daemon tries again every minute. After another tool's `nft flush ruleset` the rules come back within 5 minutes. A setting change takes effect when the daemon restarts. The notification names the account; the program is shown when the sockets are still open.
- **Added (Client): detection when it happens.** Persistence (autostart, cron, systemd units, shell start files) and network settings (leases, proxy files) are scanned right after the file changes, and the timed scan stays as a backstop. A reverse shell is scanned right after a shell or interpreter starts (about 1.2 seconds to the finding on a test machine; the timed scan, every 2 seconds, stays).
- **Added (Client, on by default, notification only): a notification when a new kernel module is loaded.** It fires for a `.ko` that is not on the list of known modules and is loaded with `insmod`; `kernel_module_watch_allow` excludes modules. "Loaded by" shows `insmod` or `modprobe`, and a late `depmod` does not cause a false notification (the daemon waits up to 30 seconds, 6 seconds when the target is under `/lib/modules`).
- **Fixed (Client): the packaged systemd unit had `ProtectKernelModules=yes`, which hides `/usr/lib/modules` from the daemon, so the kernel module detection could not read `modules.dep`.** The line is removed from the Client unit (the Server unit is unchanged).
- **Changed (Client): when the pre-execution block refuses a program, the refusal is kept in the notification history** (also when a cool-down or an earlier approval is pending), and the approval text says the run was refused. The check of the file content is faster (about 55 times for 256 KiB; the first run of a new 66 KiB file went from 8.7 ms to 1.15 ms, and of a 7.6 MiB file from 30.7 ms to 1.97 ms). The result of the check is unchanged. If `/`, `/home`, `/tmp` and `/var/tmp` are one filesystem, the block's scope is in effect every program run on the machine (the daemon warns at start).
- **Added: the evidence chain has a start mark and a copy of its head.** Cutting the end off a chain can now be found (`verify --witness`; MCP `get_forensic_evidence_bundles` and `get_incident_timeline` take `chainHead` and `witness`). The manifest records `duration_ms` and `elapsed_ms` for each piece of evidence, and the bundle has `process/network.json` with the address, port and state of both ends of the process's TCP and UDP sockets (unspecified addresses are `*`, UDP shows no state, the content of the traffic is not kept).
- **Added (Server, off by default): a pre-roll of the traffic before a detection** (`incident_preroll_enabled`, `incident_preroll_seconds`, default 30, 10 to 120; both need a restart). A capture that starts after the detection cannot hold the traffic before the isolation (starting `dumpcap` takes about 130 to 160 ms). When it is on, the daemon runs `dumpcap` (package `tshark`) and keeps the first 96 bytes of each packet for the last seconds in a ring under `/var/lib/roamswitch/preroll` (readable by root only, up to about 128 MiB). When a detection saves a bundle, each packet is cut at the end of its transport header and put into `packet_preroll/` (an `index.json` and one file per capture), so the bundle has the headers and no content. The ring itself can hold a few dozen bytes of content after a short header. Turning it off removes the ring; if `dumpcap` cannot run, a notification says why. In the test on a VM (10 runs, IPv4 TCP), the first SYN of the attack was in the bundle at a time about 3.2 seconds before the detection (10 of 10), and the strings the attack's shell sent were not in the bundle (0 of 10). `dumpcap` used 7.2% of a CPU on average at about 168,000 packets per second, with 6.8 MiB resident. The bundle gains one more piece of evidence, so `capturedArtifactCount` is 1 higher; the chain status `anchor_removed` is new, so scripts that count status names should be checked.
- **Fixed: `verify` no longer says `manifest.json` changed when only `--witness` does not match.**
- **Changed (Server): a notification just before the Air-Gap isolation is sent once per 10 minutes for the same kind of detection.** Repeated detections used to send dozens of the same notice within seconds, and the queue sent them again after the isolation was lifted. The history keeps every one.
- Verification: the core library tests (4,284), the CLI, MCP (130) and the evidence-capture tests pass in Docker with 0 failures; the Server notifier tests (36) pass. On Ubuntu with kernel 7.0 (a disposable VM) the NFLOG receiving, scans by normal sockets, raw frames on the egress hook, the group already in use, and the return after `nft flush ruleset` were checked. With a raw-frame generator (millions of packets per second, 4 CPUs), the egress hook cost about 0.4 microseconds per packet (about 13% of one core at 300,000 packets per second), with no overruns. The kernel module detection and the pre-execution block were checked on the same kind of VM. Not yet checked: a `depmod` that starts more than 6 seconds after `insmod`, DKMS and vendor drivers, live patches, and outgoing-scan detection on a real server under load.

### 1.17.44

Aligns the name of the strictest network protection level with the Mac edition and adds defenses inspired by ARTEX and gogo.

- **Changed: the strictest network protection level is now named "Maximum lockdown", the same as on the Mac.** Notifications, the tray, the help, the knowledge base and the README showed it as "Away", "Lockdown Defense" and other names; it is now one name in all 10 languages. The name of the setting that applies to unregistered networks, "away protection level", is unchanged.
- **Added: `roamswitch agent-check`.** Called from an AI coding agent's pre-tool hook (PreToolUse), it asks for confirmation when the destination is on the list of malicious IPs and refuses cloud metadata destinations. It prints nothing when it has no verdict. `--print-hook-config` prints an example setup.
- **Added: `roamswitch audit-browser-extensions`.** Checks the permissions of Chromium-family browser extensions (notification only; `--accept` saves your own baseline).
- **Added (off by default, notification only): detection of scans from this machine to internal networks.** Raw frames such as masscan's are also seen through the egress hook.
- **Added (off by default, notification only): a notification when an AI agent repeats the same operation (EXR-018).**
- **Added: identification of the offensive AI platform ARTEX, and an information-level notification when it is exposed to the network.**
- **Added: the delivery state of notifications (sent, queued, dropped) in the notification history (Server TUI and GTK GUI).** An item being sent in the notification queue is not lost if the process dies. Text sent to chat and webhooks is made harmless first.
- **Added: SNMP exposure to the diagnostics (33 items on Client, 44 on Server).** Ports of debug and proxy services (JDWP, Node inspector, PHP-FPM/Xdebug, SOCKS) reachable from the network are shown with the process name.
- **Changed: incoming port-scan detection counts a connection to a closed sentinel port (135, 445, 1433, 3389, 5985, 5986) as four closed ports.**
- **Fixed: the detection coverage table has rows for T1046 (incoming port scan) and T1595 (Active Scanning) matching the implementation.**
- **Fixed: `roamswitch daily-report test` printed "Sent" and succeeded even when it reached no channel.** It now fails with the reason. `roamswitch server config` showed `webhook_block_private`; it now shows the real key, `webhook_block_private_addresses`.
- **Fixed: `roamswitch help` prints its list even when roamswitch-mcp is missing.**
- **Removed: `assets/locales.json`, which nothing read.**
- Nine items were added to the knowledge base in 10 languages.
- **Server only:**
  - **Added: approval of outbound limits by name and IP** (`roamswitch agent-egress pin add|list|renew|remove|accept-held NAME:PORT`, `observed`, `approve IP:PORT`). Adds a name a person approved to the outbound traffic of an AI agent's dedicated account. The daemon resolves the name through the system resolver every 10 minutes and follows the IP set (opt-in). An answer with even one internal address is refused; up to 32 entries per name and 8 new public addresses per hour. The limit stays in place when an approval expires.
  - **Added: `roamswitch audit-env-secrets`.** From the environment variables of AI agent accounts and web worker processes, reports only secret-looking names, with names and counts (values are not kept; information level).
  - **Added: `roamswitch config-ledger show|verify`.** Writes weakening of settings (`server.conf`, `web_guard.conf`, `web_rules.conf`, `guard.yaml`, Fleet policy) to a hash-chained ledger and sends one notification for what got weaker.
  - **Changed: the web guard.** On common-format logs (no Referer) it stops the detections that need a Referer and says they are unavailable. The notification for a block on common-format logs says the shared address is not protected. Each block pins the access-log lines it is based on as evidence. A decoy page aimed at AI agents (experimental, off by default, notification only) was added.
  - **Changed: the investigation agent's instructions were tightened, and a fixed line is added to the notification when the input contained text aimed at an AI.** `doctor` warns when the investigation agent's account is not in `agent_users`, and `setup` offers to add it.
- Verification: 5,778 workspace tests pass. Not yet checked: whether a real AI agent follows the decoy page, the default count of EXR-018 on real hardware, and the outbound scan detection against a real server.

### 1.17.43

Fixes gaps in the reasons shown in the protection status and tells settings presets and network protection levels apart.

- **Changed: "settings preset" and "network protection level" are always shown with their category.** For example "Settings preset: Standard security" and "Network protection level: Standard protection", in the app, the CLI (`roamswitch preset`, the protection level display), notifications, `doctor`, `status`, MCP, the README and the man page. The English level name is now "Standard protection" (to tell it apart from the preset "Standard security").
- **Changed: the Server daily report marks a critical that was not in the previous day's report as new.** The notification gets a line "New critical: N (not in the previous day's report)" and a `[critical, new]` tag on the item (with no previous report, every critical is new). What counts as an important problem is still `daily_report_important_min_severity` (default high), and a critical is always included. The saved JSON gains `problems` and `new_critical_count`.
- **Fixed: reasons in the protection status contained raw English strings.** The details of the web access guard, the web-root scan, entropy watch, the error log, `agent_users`, canary placement, Tailscale and WireGuard are kept as a key and arguments and worded in the display language.
- **Fixed: some steps were not concrete.** The web access guard now shows how to find the log location with `nginx -T` or `grep CustomLog` and how to set it. The canary shows, for each folder it could not use, `ls -ld` and a `systemctl edit` with `ReadWritePaths=-<path>`. Unreadable inputs carry a command to check them.
- **Fixed: the `doctor` line of the web access guard had a different shape from the other guards and the same content appeared twice.** It is now one line in the form feature, role, place, situation, effect, steps.
- **Changed: in Japanese, the time in a reason is followed by the Japan time** (for example `2026-11-14 07:13 UTC (Japan time 2026-11-14 16:13)`).
- Verified: the 5,543 workspace tests pass (Docker; 0 failed, 17 ignored as before).

### 1.17.42

Linux Client gets settings presets and Server gets a once-a-day report. The reasons in the protection status now always say which feature they are about, where to look and what to do. AccessScope 1.0.8 is bundled.

- **Added: settings presets (Client Edition, desktop).** Choose one of four: Light, Balanced, Standard security and Maximum security. Light stops the always-running work that costs resources (ClamAV, the process-exec recorder and so on) and lengthens scan intervals. Balanced balances resources and security. Standard security is the standard setting without regard to resources (items that cause many false positives or stop operations are left out and explained one by one). Maximum security includes those items too and shows a strong warning for each one before it is applied. Use it from the card at the top of the Diagnostics tab, the first-run wizard, or `roamswitch preset [status | list | show <name> | set <name> [--dry-run] [--yes]]`. Changing even one setting by hand shows "Custom". It is not in Server Edition (`roamswitch preset` answers "Client Edition only").
- **Added: two setting keys so that Light is really light.** `download_guard_poll_ms` (default 800, 200 to 60000) and `sentinel_interval_secs` (default 3, 1 to 60). The defaults are the same as before. No restart is needed.
- **Changed: a fresh install starts on Balanced.** Only when there is no `config.json` yet. An existing configuration is not touched. The default of RoamSwitch OS is Standard security.
- **Added: the Server daily report (off by default).** Once a day it makes the PersonalSOC report (and the AccessScope report when PersonalSOC cannot cover everything) and sends one message to the notification channels you set (Telegram, LINE, the notification webhook). With no important problem it sends a short summary only; with one it states the problems briefly. The full report is kept for 14 days in `/var/lib/roamswitch/reports/` on the Server, and the notification gives the path. Nothing is masked before sending. Turn it on with `sudo roamswitch server config set daily_report_enabled true`. With no channel set, no report is made even when it is on. Commands: `roamswitch daily-report run | status | test`. What counts as an important problem is `daily_report_important_min_severity` (default high), and AccessScope can be automatic, always or never.
- **Changed: every reason in the protection status (`doctor`, `status`, the app, notifications, the TUI and MCP) now says which feature it is about, where to look, what is happening, what is not being seen and what to do.** The order is: feature (what it does) / place (setting key, screen, command to look) / situation (since when) / effect / steps (`systemctl restart …`, `journalctl …`, `sudo roamswitch server config set …` and so on). Every guard has a one-sentence role in 10 languages and a place.
- **Fixed: because repeated runs are folded, the `events tree <pid>` that a notification pointed to said "no record".** The run that a notification is about is written at once. `events tree` exits with 1 when there is no record (2 when no pid is given).
- **Fixed: a change written before a package update started could be notified as "written while the update command ran, before unpacking".** `/etc/group` and `/etc/gshadow` written by `useradd` got a different reason from `/etc/passwd` written by the same `useradd`. Anything written before the update started is "it already differed from the baseline before the update".
- **Fixed: the periodic check wrote an already notified, unchanged violation to the journal at ERROR level.** Only a new violation is ERROR; a notified one is INFO.
- **Fixed: only the first freeze right after a restart had more files written before it.** The information it needs is read at start-up.
- Bundled: AccessScope 1.0.8 (attribution of sources from AI crawlers and LLM SDKs; see the AccessScope section).
- Verified: the 5,531 workspace tests pass (Docker; 0 failed, 17 ignored as before).

### 1.17.41

Fixes for defects found by re-running the lessons on the previous version (1.17.40).

- **Fixed: deleting a folder that held decoys made the decoys be re-placed and the host isolated over and over, using up the automatic-response limit.** Removing `/var/www` produced 60-odd detections and isolations in about 35 seconds, and a real decoy tamper afterwards did not isolate the host. Decoys in a folder that is gone are no longer watched, and detection and isolation stop after one. Re-applying while already isolated does not count against the limit (`airgap_per_hour`).
- **Fixed: freezing was slow, and a second decoy whose writer could not be identified became a separate incident.** The freeze now happens right after detection (before re-placing decoys, notifying and collecting evidence), about 0.09 s instead of 0.32 s. A decoy rewrite within 10 seconds of the previous incident joins the same incident even when the writer is unknown (one notification set, one isolation, one evidence bundle).
- **Fixed: when the automatic-response limit prevented isolation, the notification said isolation had failed.** It now says the host was not isolated because of the automatic-response limit (`max_auto_tier` or `airgap_per_hour`), and the advance notice reads "the host will not be isolated".
- **Fixed: a change announced on the spot during a package update got a second notification afterwards, with the wrong reason.** The closing note now says "it was written while the update command ran, before the packages were unpacked" (it used to say "it already differed from the baseline before the update"), and the second notification for the same file is gone, even if the daemon restarts during the update.
- **Fixed: right after the clock was moved forward, "Protection you turned on is not working (Exec Recorder: the state file is stale)" could appear.** The single check right after a clock change is skipped; an execution recorder that really stopped is still reported at the next check (5 minutes later). Retention of the notification history (7 days) is unchanged.
- **Fixed: the account that logged in was sometimes missing from a writer filled in from the execution log.** The account known from the SSH login line is now added in that case too.
- **Fixed: after a daemon restart, the port-scan guard notified and blocked again for the same audit.** Notification, blocking and the once-per-10-minutes limit for same-shaped notifications now carry over a restart.
- **Fixed: a source that fail2ban banned and unbanned right away was never notified.** The Ban line in the fail2ban log is also read, and the notification says the ban was already lifted.
- **Fixed: with `sudo roamswitch vuln-accept`, the files under `~/.config/roamswitch/` became owned by root, and `--list` and `--revoke` without sudo failed.** The owner stays the user who ran sudo.
- **Fixed: after an evidence bundle was taken, `roamswitch uninstall --purge` could not remove `/var/lib/roamswitch` and left the package half removed.** The append-only flag on the evidence chain record is cleared before removal. The "automatic blocks in the action journal" line, printed twice by `uninstall`, now appears once.
- **Fixed: putting an internal address in `webhook_url` did not tell you at save time that it would be refused.** The saving line and `doctor` now say so and show the command to allow it. Right after installing, `doctor` also says when the web rules have not been reported yet.
- **Fixed: RoamSwitch's own activity matched EXR-017 (a series of recon commands).** Activity of roamswitch programs under the system folders (`/usr`) and their children is not counted.
- **Fixed: a single `nohup` run could produce two EXR-002 notifications.** Hits by the same process on the same program within 10 seconds are merged into one.
