- **Fixed: adding an AI agent setting (such as `.mcp.json`) and reverting it right away produced no notification.** A change that differed from the baseline since the last check and was then reverted is now notified as "an AI agent setting changed once and was restored", together with the process that wrote it (Server and Client).
- **Fixed: in English `roamswitch timeline`, Resource Guard lines showed internal names.** They read as sentences, e.g. "Crash loop: 3 restarts in 300s" and "Sustained high CPU usage: …".
- **Fixed: `roamswitch forward status` and the notification history mixed languages in the note about internal destinations, and printed the address twice.** The note is in the display language and the address appears once. The hint for allowing an address now says it replaces the list.
- **Fixed: profile-switch notifications stayed in the language they were recorded in.** They are shown in the reader's language (notifications recorded before this version stay in their recorded language).
- **Fixed: for a USB drive whose identity differed from the allowed one with the guard off, the journal line said "treating it as unapproved" although it was not held.** The wording now matches what happens. A rare race that left "its identity differs" out of the notification is fixed too.
- **Fixed: the description of `roamswitch audit-mcp-configs` did not list Codex and OpenCode.** The item count in `roamswitch server --help` status also matches the actual count.
- Verified: The 5,416 workspace tests pass (Docker; 0 failures, 17 ignored as before). The shell tests, the Arch tests and the no-network audit pass too.
### 1.17.40

Fixes for defects found when the learning-site lessons were run again on the previous version. Limits on automatic responses, an action journal, maintenance windows, rotation of the update signing keys and more are added too. If your scripts rely on exit codes, the shape of the output, what is forwarded or the default settings, check the "Changed (affects scripts)" items.

- **Fixed: the FIM hold during a package update applied to changes made by anyone.** While apt or unattended-upgrades ran, even a systemd unit placed by an `sh` unrelated to apt waited until the end of the update to be notified. Only what the process holding the package manager lock, or its children, writes is now held. Changes by other writers (a login shell, another service, cron) are notified at once. The hold record is removed after the notification is sent, so the notification survives a daemon restart in the middle of releasing it.
- **Fixed: a critical file created and then removed during an update, or changed and then put back, showed up nowhere.** For example, a setting placed in `/etc/sudoers.d` or `/etc/ssh/sshd_config.d` and removed before the update ended. What was held because its writer could not be confirmed is notified at the end of the update as "created during a package update and removed before it ended" or "changed during a package update and put back to its old content before it ended", with the writer recorded when it was held. Temporary files written by the package manager (`*.dpkg-new`) are not notified.
- **Fixed: "The package update at … did not take this change into the baseline" was also attached to later, unrelated changes.** For example, the notification for `/etc/shadow` changed by `chpasswd` 7 minutes after the update. The content at the time of the update is now remembered, and the note is attached only while the content is the same. The count "N of these were left out …" in `fim verify` works the same way.
- **Fixed: the fail2ban ban notification came from a few seconds to over 20 seconds after the ban, and did not come at all when the address was unbanned right after.** The fix in the previous version was not enough. Writes to the fail2ban log are no longer dropped but looked at again, and the notification comes in about 1 second. The same goes when fail2ban is started later. When fail2ban bans the same source again after an unban, it is notified again (it was silent for 10 minutes).
- **Fixed: name resolution right after lifting isolation could time out.** For example, `curl` right after `emergency-restore` failed with "Resolving timed out after 5004 milliseconds". Queries that systemd-resolved started during the isolation kept retrying. systemd-resolved is now restarted when isolation is lifted (in a VM, name resolution right after lifting took about 0.4 seconds). When the isolation did not change DNS, the list of DNS servers is not rewritten (the IPv6 DNS server announced by the router used to drop out).
- **Changed: with the USB storage guard off, the notification for a USB stick with an allowed model but a different fingerprint says so** (client edition). "Its identity differs from the device you allowed." is added at the end of the USB storage connection notification. The stick is not held and stays usable. With the guard on, holding it as "identity changed" is unchanged.
- **Fixed: English `roamswitch timeline` showed internal names.** "action taken: notify" and "notified" on Exec Recorder and FIM lines are now "Logged & Notified", the FIM kind "new_file" is "new file", and the reason "proc" for an unknown login path is a sentence. Records from earlier versions are shown this way too.
- **Fixed: the 2 decoy (honeytoken) lines of `roamswitch timeline` were in the server.conf language at the time of recording instead of the reader's language.** They are in the reader's language (for lines recorded by this version on).
- **Fixed: the "command" in FIM and other notifications could be cut off without saying so.** The command line of a process that is ending can be read only partly. When the execution record has the full command, it is shown with "(/proc gave only the start; the full text is from the exec record)"; otherwise "(cut off: the rest could not be read)" is added (the same in `text` of `--json`).
- **Fixed: the SSH line of notifications had double parentheses** ("The login path could not be determined (the process's details could not be read (cgroup))"). It now reads "The login path could not be determined: …" ("ログインの経路は調べられませんでした: …" in Japanese).
- **Fixed: in English and other languages, the "Known Kernel CVE Exposure" line of the health check listed every matched CVE on one line** (Server Edition; 517 CVEs, about 12,800 characters, in a VM). As in Japanese, it now shows CRITICAL first, 12 of them, and "… (+505)".
- **Fixed: `--version` of the bundled commands did not print the version.** Some printed the usage (`roamswitch-incident-capture`, `roamswitch-prn-recorder`, `roamswitch-honeytokens`), one started an update (`roamswitch-updater`), and others started running (the daemons, webgate, the MCP server and the like). All of them now print "<name> <version>" and exit with code 0.
- **Fixed: `roamswitch doctor` said it was waiting for the timer's first run while the signed data was being fetched right after installation.** It now says "The first download of the signed data is running now. After it, the updates run once a day."
- **Fixed: "Note: server.conf changed after roamswitch-server started" in `server allowlist test` did not appear when the save came within the same second as the restart.** It now appears for a save within the same second too.
- **Fixed: log following (the SSH guard and web server logs) read a file from the middle after copytruncate when more was written than before.** Truncation is now recognized by a hash of the first 256 bytes, and the file is read from the start. rsyslog's "message repeated N times" lines are counted as N lines (they were dropped as unreadable, so the password failures in them were not counted).
- **Added: limits on automatic responses.** They set how far automatic SSH, port scan and web bans, process freezes and Air-Gap go (`max_auto_tier`: none, ban, freeze or airgap; airgap by default) and how many per hour (`ban_per_hour` 60, `freeze_per_hour` 20, `airgap_per_hour` 6). Beyond them, the response is not taken but only recommended, and "Automatic action reduced to a recommendation" is notified once an hour per action. Responses taken by a person and re-applying during isolation are not counted. The Server uses server.conf and the client edition config.json. `max_auto_tier` is added to the signed Fleet policy too (the lower of the device's and the policy's value is used).
- **Added: an action journal.** Bans, freezes, isolation, account locks and file quarantine are recorded with their start and done, failed or refused (as root, `/var/lib/roamswitch/action_journal.jsonl`, 0600). The real state is read back right after each action, and an action that did not take effect is recorded as failed. `emergency-restore` first undoes the recorded Air-Gap, port scan and web bans, newest first (SSH bans stay, as before).
- **Added: maintenance windows** (`sudo roamswitch maintenance start --hours N --reason …`, `stop`, `status`). They last from 15 minutes to 4 hours and need a reason. During one, notifications that are not protected detections are kept in the history without being sent, and one summary is sent when it ends. Detection, recording and automatic responses do not stop.
- **Added: a protected-detection class.** Decoy reads, `authorized_keys`, sudoers, PAM, a new uid 0 account, `ld.so.preload` and package checksum mismatches are notified regardless of maintenance windows and of the 10-minute interval for the same notification. When the FIM baseline is taken in as a whole, the records of changed protected files are kept (those matching the package checksums are taken in).
- **Changed: the check that keeps the same notification from being sent again compares the wording with the values left out.** It compared the exact title and body, so a different port, address or count made it a new notification. Notifications of the same form are sent once per 10 minutes, and every suppressed one is still kept in the notification history. The Server's alert sending uses the same check.
- **Added: `roamswitch timeline --since <yyyy-mm-dd|7d>` shows daily changes.** Once a day, the daemon records hashes of the listeners, the packages and the main settings (`/etc/hosts`, `sshd_config`, `pam.d`, sudoers, `ld.so.preload`, cron, systemd unit names, `authorized_keys` and others) in `/var/lib/roamswitch/system_timeline.json` (0600, up to 90 days). The content of the settings is not stored. Without `--since`, it is the incident timeline, as before.
- **Added: incident records are chained by the hash of the previous record.** The chain is checked when the records are read; if it is broken, the CLI warns and MCP `get_incident_timeline` says so (reading goes on). A record is marked when the detection's input was interrupted around it. Incident grouping now also links the same source address (within 60 minutes) and the same SSH session.
- **Added: whether the timeline's evidence bundle references still match the current record chain is shown.** For example, the bundle is gone, the manifest differs from the recorded one, or the chain is broken (CLI, Server TUI, MCP). When the chain cannot be read because the user is not root, the reason is shown.
- **Added: revocation and rotation of the update signing keys.** The key list, revoked keys and the lowest accepted version in the signed manifest are checked, and only keys that are signed by an already trusted key, not revoked and not expired are used. A manifest signed with the old key that lists a new key hands trust over to the new key. Revocations and the lowest version are kept in `/var/lib/roamswitch/update_key_trust.json` and never go back. If this file is damaged, updates stop. A manifest without the new fields is accepted with the built-in key, as before.
- **Added: when a package update fails, the previous programs are put back.** Before the update, the commands, `/usr/lib/roamswitch` and the systemd units are backed up, and after it, they are checked to work. If the check fails, they are restored from the backup and the services restarted. The version shown by the package manager stays the new one, so `roamswitch doctor` reports the rollback (deb, rpm and pacman).
- **Changed: the check that refuses internal addresses as Webhook and syslog destinations is on by default.** It now covers the forwarding syslog (udp, tcp, tls) in addition to the notification Webhook and the forwarding Webhook. Internal means loopback, private, link-local (including cloud metadata) and similar addresses. Add destinations inside the LAN to `webhook_allow_addresses` or `forward_webhook_allow_addresses` (which also applies to syslog). With `false` written in server.conf, nothing is refused, as before. A refusal is shown in the notification history once a day per destination, with the command to add it to the allowlist (on the Server, in each notification channel too).
- **Added: a digest notification for low-severity alerts** (Server Edition, `notify_digest_low_severity_enabled`, off by default). When on, Info and Low alerts are sent as one message every `notify_digest_interval_minutes` (60 by default, 5 to 1440). High and Critical are sent at once. The notification history keeps every alert at once.
- **Changed: changing the Telegram chat_id or the LINE user_id to another value clears the token and asks for it again** (Server Edition). It works the same with `config set`, the TUI and the setup. A first-time setting does not count as a change.
- **Added: limits on runs of the investigation agent** (Server Edition; `investigation.max_runs_per_hour` in guard.yaml, 6 by default, and `max_runs_per_day`, 30 by default; 0 means no limit). At the limit, the agent is not started, and only the first time until it can run again, the limit and the waiting time are notified. The counts survive restarts.
- **Added: execution-record rule EXR-017 (a burst of reconnaissance commands).** When the same process group runs 4 or more of `ip addr`, `ip route`, `ip neigh`, `arp -a`, `cat /etc/hosts`, `netstat` or `ss`, and `find -mtime` from the root within 60 seconds, outside a person's own login session (a service, cron, a container's child, an agent's tool call), it is notified as Medium. It is not part of the isolation policy.
- **Added: when the process that read a decoy runs under an AI agent (Claude Code, Codex and the like), the alert says so.** One line with the agent's name and pid and what to check is added. When the agent itself opened the file, that is said. The timeline line gets it too.
- **Added: 3 findings in the MCP configuration audit.** A remote server without authentication (`remote_no_auth`), a server listening on all interfaces (`mcp_wide_bind`), and a server that reads outside text in the same file as a server that can send data out (`egress_exposure`), all medium. It only reads the configuration files and does not connect to the servers. Gemini CLI's `httpUrl` and OpenCode's `headers` and `oauth`, which were missed, are read too.
- **Added: SARIF output.** `roamswitch audit-mcp-configs --format sarif` and `format` of MCP `audit_mcp_configs`, `run_package_cve_scan` and `run_package_cve_scan_languages` return SARIF 2.1.0.
- **Added: a class and a basis for each package CVE finding.** The class is listed in KEV, EPSS 0.9 or higher, or other; the basis is a match against the distribution's security data or by name and version only. The verdicts do not change. The CLI list and the GUI rows show the basis on one line.
- **Changed: when an event forwarding destination fails 5 times in a row, nothing is sent to it for 1 minute, and then one try is made.** If it fails again, the wait doubles (up to 15 minutes). Events not sent go into the disk queue and are not lost. The waits for resending and for retrying notifications get some jitter.
- **Changed: control characters, ANSI escape sequences, invisible characters and bidirectional controls are removed from MCP answers.** The prompt injection check runs on the text before removal. Words with a single Cyrillic or Greek look-alike letter ("ignоre" and the like) are found too.
- **Changed: JSON with the same key written twice is treated as damaged in the settings and the USB trust file.** The last value used to be taken silently. The client's config.json keeps running on the last values that were read correctly, and `doctor` says so. When the USB trust file is damaged, the fingerprint of the device connected now is not learned automatically (so a spoofed device's fingerprint does not overwrite it). A device signing key file that cannot be read is kept as `.corrupt-<time>` before it is overwritten.
- **Changed: state files are all written the same way.** They are written to a temporary file, synced and swapped in. Nothing is written to a symbolic link or a file that is not a regular file. Files written by several processes (the notification history, pending notifications, allowlists, Sensor pairing, CVE risk acceptance, the MCP tool call record) get a `<name>.lock` lock. The WireGuard configuration is written 0600 from the start.
- **Changed: the root daemons allow no memory dumps and do not read configuration files that are hard links.** The systemd units of the 2 daemons no longer limit the number of restarts (so that crashes in a row do not leave them stopped).

- **Fixed: `emergency-restore` did not call the undo of the action journal.** Its first step now undoes, newest first, the Air-Gap, port-scan and web blocks in the journal that are still in effect, reads the state again after each undo, and writes `undone` or `undo_failed`. SSH blocks are left in place (lift them with `roamswitch server unban`).
- **Added: the Linux MCP server gets `get_system_timeline` and four tools that only the Mac version had.** They are `get_browser_credential_watch_status`, `get_forensic_evidence_bundles`, `get_ransomware_recovery_snapshots` and `run_dependency_source_scan`. The Linux version now has 50 tools and the Mac version 41.

**Changed (affects scripts)**

- Forwarding: the `summary` of forwarded notifications (`source: notification`) and timeline events is in English whatever the language in server.conf. FIM lines from earlier versions are forwarded as sentences, not internal names.
- Settings: Webhook and syslog sending to internal addresses is refused by default. Destinations inside the LAN get nothing until they are added to the allowlist. `max_auto_tier`, `ban_per_hour`, `freeze_per_hour` and `airgap_per_hour` (1 to 1000; input out of range is refused), `notify_digest_low_severity_enabled` and `notify_digest_interval_minutes`, and `investigation.max_runs_per_hour` and `max_runs_per_day` in guard.yaml are added. A config.json or server.json with the same key written twice is treated as damaged.
- Automatic responses: bans, freezes and Air-Gap beyond the hourly limits are not taken. `emergency-restore` first undoes the recorded bans, newest first.
- Notifications: notifications of the same form that differ only in values are sent once per 10 minutes (each is kept in the history).
- Output: `--version` of the bundled commands prints "<name> <version>" and exits with 0, and `roamswitch-updater --version` does not start an update. English `timeline` shows "Logged & Notified", "new file" and a sentence for the reason the login path is unknown. "The login path could not be determined: …" in notifications has one level of parentheses. `ports -a` puts a space between the address and the marks, as in "127.0.0.1 (local only) [MED] [DevServer]". The save line of `server config set forward_syslog_format ""` is "(forward_syslog_format = rfc5424)". The known CVE line of English `status --server` ends with "… (+N)". `tier` and `basis` are added to `--json` of the package CVE scan and to MCP, and `chain`, `chain_break` on each record and `evidenceChecks` to MCP `get_incident_timeline`. `last_error` of `forward status` can show "not tried: N failures in a row; next try in S s". Control and invisible characters are removed from MCP answers.
- Files: `action_journal.jsonl`, `system_timeline.json`, `investigation_runs.json` and `update_key_trust.json` appear in `/var/lib/roamswitch/`. A `<name>.lock` appears next to files written by several processes.
- systemd: `StartLimitIntervalSec=0`, `OOMScoreAdjust=-500` and `LimitCORE=0` are added to the units of the 2 daemons.

**Other fixes**

- The error of MCP `audit_secrets` became invalid JSON for a path containing a quote.
- Checks: 5352 workspace tests pass (Docker, 0 failures, 17 ignored as before). The shell tests for adding the repository and for taking in updates, and the tests that run as root, also pass in an Arch Linux container.

### 1.17.39

Fixes for defects found when the learning-site lessons were run again on the previous version. If your scripts rely on exit codes, the shape of the output or where it goes, check the "Changed (affects scripts)" items.

- **Fixed: decoy tampering was checked only at the next patrol.** The patrol ran every 20 seconds on the Server and every 3 seconds on the client, so detection took from a few seconds to more than ten, and other files were rewritten in the meantime. The decoy files are now watched with inotify, checked the moment they are written, and the writer is frozen (in a VM, in all 3 runs, detection took about 10 milliseconds and no file was rewritten before the freeze). When the same writer rewrites the next decoy within 10 seconds, it is folded into the same incident, with one notification and one isolation.
- **Fixed: on a first install (including after purge) and on reinstalling after `uninstall`, starting the service timed out.** Before taking the baseline of critical file integrity monitoring (FIM), the daemon waited for the end of the apt run that was installing it, so the last line of postinst was "…is not running". The baseline is now taken separately once apt has finished, and the service starts at once (about 5 seconds in a VM). The client daemon also stopped taking CLI and GUI requests for up to 120 seconds during the same wait.
- **Fixed: upgrading the package did not fetch the CVE data.** It was fetched only on a first install, so right after upgrading from an earlier version `scan-packages` said the local CVE data had not been fetched, and there was no KEV matching or notification until the timer's first run. An upgrade now fetches it once when the data is missing (on the Server when update fetching is on, on the client when the timer is enabled).
- **Fixed: the port scan notification interval (once an hour per source) was checked before deciding whether the source is a paired Sensor and before banning.** After a Sensor audit was notified and the device then unpaired, scans from the same source within that hour were neither notified nor banned. When the treatment changes, and when the scan goes on after the ban ends, it is notified and banned for 10 minutes regardless of the interval.
- **Fixed: when Docker was installed later, containers went unwatched for up to about 5 minutes.** Containers started with `--privileged` or with `docker.sock` mounted in that time were not notified. While waiting, `docker.sock` is now checked every 2 seconds, and watching starts as soon as it appears (no `server restart` needed).
- **Fixed: the same listener was notified as "a new program started listening on the network" at every daemon restart** (Server Edition). The notified processes are kept in `port_guard.json`, and after a restart only the block is applied again. A process started again is notified again.
- **Fixed: FIM notifications for files written during an update.** The same file was notified twice, at the end of the update and at the periodic check about 3 minutes later; it is now once. A file written while apt was downloading packages (before unpacking) was described as "(it already differed from the baseline before the update)"; it now says "(it was written while the update command ran, before the packages were unpacked)".
- **Fixed: the web rules reported the same file again and again** (Server Edition). The write watch, the periodic check and File Scan Guard each reported it, and reported it again after a daemon restart. Reported files are remembered in `/var/lib/roamswitch/web_hits_seen.json` (0600) and are not reported while unchanged. A changed content or another matching rule is reported again.
- **Fixed: the 5-minute hold of log wiping was lost at a daemon restart, and the "N more times" notification never came** (Server Edition). The hold is now recorded and notified after a restart too.
- **Fixed: fail2ban ban notifications arrived 15 to 25 seconds after the ban, and not at all after an earlier unban.** fail2ban's log is now read the moment it is written, and the notification comes in about 1 second.
- **Fixed: `sudo roamswitch emergency-restore` with nothing isolated removed rules such as port scan detection without asking.** With no isolation record and no isolation rule left, it now removes nothing and says so.
- **Fixed: eBPF notifications showed a user slice as "container: user-1000.sl", cut short and as a container.** It is no longer called a container and is shown on the service unit line. When the direct parent is the user's own systemd (a systemd that is not pid 1), the automatic first-look triage adds one line with the reason and raises the false-positive likelihood by 2 points.
- **Fixed: installing Falco with the install script made Falco listen on 0.0.0.0:8765 at its first start, raising "a new program started listening on the network".** RoamSwitch's Falco settings (drop-in) are now put in place before Falco is installed.
- **Fixed: showing the notification history in the reader's language did not work for some notifications.** They were the execution-record rules (EXR), Sigma matches, decoy credential files (honeytokens), expired acceptances, log template anomalies, the reason a scanner was judged spoofed, investigation agent notifications and the notifications the client GUI shows. These too are now shown in the reader's language in `roamswitch notifications`, the GUI, MCP and the TUI, whatever language they were recorded in. Reading a weakened-setting notification in another language replaced the state with "Action Required"; the value text ("Enabled (Router mode)" and the like) is now translated. The Server's decoy notifications come in any of the 10 languages in server.conf (they were Japanese or English). Notifications recorded so far keep the language they were recorded in.
- **Fixed: some `roamswitch timeline` lines were internal names or English.** The kind of a FIM line uses the name in notifications and `fim verify` ("内容の変更" in Japanese, not the internal "modified"), Resource Guard lines and Exec Recorder's "action taken: notify" are in the display language, and Incident Capture and expired-acceptance lines are in the reader's language. The line "rebuilt the baseline for the log tampering comparison" had severity Critical and action "logged and notified"; it is now informational and "logged only" (with no ATT&CK technique).
- **Fixed: when `roamswitch install` could not create its sandbox on a host running the Server, it gave only AppArmor advice.** The cause is `kernel.unprivileged_userns_clone=0`, written by the Server's `harden_userns_enabled` (on by default). That cause and how to undo it (`sudo roamswitch server config set harden_userns_enabled false`, then `sudo sysctl kernel.unprivileged_userns_clone=1`) are now shown. A value that is already the same is not written, so the "could not set …apparmor_restrict_unprivileged_userns" warning at every start is gone.
- **Fixed: when another apt run (unattended-upgrades and the like) was active during `uninstall`, it waited silently at "Removing the program...".** It now says "Another package manager run (name) is in progress. The removal waits for it to finish (up to 5 minutes)."
- **Fixed: on the client, reading `/run/roamswitch/state.json` right after `sudo roamswitch airgap disable` could still show `"air_gap":true`.** Turning Air-Gap on and off now writes state.json before answering.
- **Fixed: on the client, the notification for a USB stick held under Lockdown read "🛑 Unauthorized USB Storage Blocked" and "… was blocked".** It now reads "⏸ Unauthorized USB Storage Held" and "'…' was held due to Away Protection (Lockdown). Allow it in the approval dialog to use it."
- **Fixed: when the first `server config set` ran with no `server.conf`, `allowlist test` then printed "Note: server.conf changed after roamswitch-server started" after every save.** The warning now appears only when the list of protected processes (`protected_processes`) differs from the one the daemon uses.
- **Fixed: in the Server TUI's settings screen, discarding all staged changes with U left the bottom line at "Change staged (1 pending)…".** It now says "Discarded 1 pending change. The files are unchanged." Entering the value already saved says "That is the value already saved, so nothing was staged".
- **Fixed: the AI agent setup audit said nothing when it hit its limits (2,000 files in all, 200 files per skill) and showed only the number it read.** The number of files it did not read (at least so many) is now shown in the CLI, the GUI, the Server TUI, JSON and SARIF. Skill folders one level deeper (`<root>/<group>/<skill>`, names starting with `.` included) are read too.
- **Fixed: `roamswitch-updater` also followed a redirect from https to http.** A redirect to anything but https is now refused. Errors show only the host name, not the URL.
- **Added: a "Tailscale (tailnet) exposure" item in the health check** (`overlay_exposure`; `server_overlay_exposure` on the Server). The client fails when ShieldsUp (inbound block) is off, the firewall does not drop inbound traffic either, and a risky port listens where the tailnet can reach it. The Server fails when the node has no tags. Both fail when ngrok or cloudflared runs next to Tailscale. Exit node and subnet routes are shown only. It only reads `tailscale debug prefs` (keeping only ShieldsUp, AdvertiseRoutes and RunSSH and throwing the private keys away), `tailscale status --json` and `/proc`; it changes no setting and sends nothing. When Tailscale is not running the item does not apply; when its settings cannot be read it says it could not be checked.
- **Added: an outbound limit toward the tailnet (`tailnet_egress_guard`, off by default, Server Edition).** When on, new connections out of `tailscale0` are dropped except to the destinations in `tailnet_egress_allow`. Replies and connections opened by the other side (an SSH login over the tailnet and the like) are not stopped. Applying it needs `sudo roamswitch server restart`. A port scan notification for a scan coming over the tailnet says that a tailnet device is scanning (on the client too).
- **Added: importing IP blocklists the operator chooses (`egress_ip_feed_urls`, empty by default, Server Edition).** With up to 8 `https://` URLs set, `roamswitch-updater` fetches each directly from that URL once a day and adds it to the outbound block. RoamSwitch does not distribute the lists, and the operator checks each source's terms of use. Entries that overlap loopback, private, CGNAT and similar ranges, IPv4 ranges wider than /8, or `whitelist_ips`, `ssh_allow_only_sources`, `ssh_guard_allow` and the resolv.conf nameservers are not taken in. A failed or empty response keeps the previous list. It runs only while `egress_ip_blocklist_enabled` and the updater's update fetching are on. The setting takes effect from the next daily fetch, without a restart (to fetch now, `sudo systemctl start roamswitch-update.service`). Article 5 of the terms of use (EULA) now describes this import.
- **Changed: the 3 attack-server IP addresses published by JPCERT/CC are removed from the bundled outbound blocklist and from link checks.** Fixed addresses go stale, and blocking a destination reassigned to someone else is a false positive. The bundled list is empty. Addresses come from the `egress_ip_feed_urls` import and from lines you write yourself in `/var/lib/roamswitch/threatfeed/malicious_ips.txt`.

**Changed (affects scripts)**

- Exit codes: `forward status` without sudo exits with 1 when it cannot read the settings (it exited with 0).
- Where output goes: `quarantine list` without sudo prints the reason it cannot read to standard output instead of standard error, right after the Vault location line (the exit code stays 1). The reason no longer disappears with `2>/dev/null`.
- `emergency-restore`: with nothing isolated, it removes nothing. To remove the rules that can stop traffic anyway (port scan and web guard blocks included), add `--force`.
- Output: `text` in `roamswitch notifications --json` now comes with the notifications listed above too. English `quarantine list` reads "Quarantined: 1 file", `web-guard-check` uses the form that matches the number ("1 browser kind" and the like), and the reason in `bans` is "it has an authenticated SSH session open", the same as `bans --json`. The pinpoint isolation line of `emergency-restore` reads "Pinpoint isolation (blocked: …)" ("ピンポイント隔離中(止めている範囲: …)" in Japanese). `ports -a` no longer marks a port listening on 127.0.0.1 only with "[Isolated]" ("[Isolator遮断中]" in Japanese). The count of `fim update` leaves out the range marks, as `fim verify` does. The baseline-rebuild line of `timeline` has severity info, action logged only (`Logged`) and no technique. `skipped_file_count` and `truncated` are added to the JSON of the AI agent setup audit, and they appear in SARIF too.
- Color codes: the logs of the daemons (roamswitch, roamswitch-server), roamswitch-updater and roamswitch-xdp-gate get no color when the output is not a terminal either (`NO_COLOR` and `TERM=dumb` are honored too). ESC[2m and the like no longer end up in the journal.
- Language: `roamswitch-incident-capture verify` and `--help`, and `roamswitch-prn-recorder status`, `export` and `purge`, are in the language of the person running them in the terminal (the setting, otherwise LANG) instead of the language in server.conf. `snapshot` and `run`, which the services run, stay in the host's language.
- Diagnosis: one item is added, making 32 on the client and 43 on the Server (it does not apply on a host where Tailscale is not running).
- Settings: `server config set forward_syslog_format ""` puts the default (rfc5424) back (it was an error; the same goes for `forward_syslog_framing` and `forward_min_severity`). `egress_ip_feed_urls` rejects a URL that is not `https://` and 9 or more URLs when set. `tailnet_egress_allow` refuses a wrong value when saved.
- Network: only when `egress_ip_feed_urls` is set, `roamswitch-updater` sends one HTTPS GET a day to each of those URLs. The source sees this host's IP address and a fixed User-Agent. It is empty by default, and nothing is fetched.

**Other fixes**

- Command-line masking hid ssh settings that are not passwords, such as `-o NumberOfPasswordPrompts=1`. The password of an `sshpass -p` behind `ip netns exec`, `setsid` or `chroot` was left unmasked. Both are fixed.
- In the execution record, a user just created could show as "user: ?", and the name (`comm`) of a program that ended at once could be empty. An unknown uid is now looked up again, and the name is filled in from the executed file (`comm` in `events search --json` is filled too).
- "The oldest record is about N minutes ago" in `roamswitch-prn-recorder status` showed a time older than the real first record. It now counts from the time of the first record.
- Japanese `forward test` with no destination printed "失敗 config: no destination configured" in English. It now prints "送信先が設定されていません(…)" (the exit code stays 1).
- The description of the "domain check" in the health check now matches what it does (Server Edition). The result is remembered for 6 hours by the running daemon and the MCP server; commands such as `roamswitch status --server` check again at every run.
- Chinese and Korean text that contained the Japanese middle dot, wave dash, full stop or kana now uses the way of writing of each language. In the knowledge base's execution-record entry, paragraphs that had been swapped with another language's are fixed too.
- Checks: 5105 workspace tests pass (Docker, 0 failures, 17 ignored as before). The shell tests for adding the repository and for taking in updates also pass in an Arch Linux container.

### 1.17.38

Fixes for defects found when the learning-site lessons were run again on 1.17.37. If your scripts rely on exit codes, the shape of the output or file permissions, check the "Changed (affects scripts)" items.

- **Fixed: a notification about tampering during an update was lost when the daemon restarted.** Rewriting `sshd_config` or root's `authorized_keys` while unattended-upgrades runs defers the notification to the end of the update. When needrestart restarted roamswitch-server in the middle, the deferred notification never came, and only `fim verify` showed it. The deferred items are now kept in `/var/lib/roamswitch/fim_deferred.json` and are notified, with the writer, at the end of the update even across a restart. Deleting a watched file is now notified in about 1.5 seconds, without waiting for the full check.
- **Fixed: encrypting with openssl and deleting the original was sometimes not detected.** openssl and rm run from a shell loop end within milliseconds, so the writer could no longer be looked up and no process reached the count (in a VM under load, only 1 run in 6 was detected). Files whose writer cannot be read are now counted per folder (20 files created in the same folder within 5 seconds, with as many removed). Rewrites when the run is repeated in the same folder count too. After the fix, the VM detected every run: 20 under load, 10 repeated runs and 5 right after a restart.
- **Fixed: a broken setting or record could drop a protection.** When a value in `server.conf` is broken, the SSH source allowlist and the other guards keep running with the last values read correctly (`/var/lib/roamswitch/server.conf.last-good`; `client-config.last-good.json` on the client; both root, 0600), and `ssh-allow status`, the TUI and `doctor` say so (the SSH allowlist sends one notification). Without a record of earlier values, the allowlist table is left alone. A broken ban list does not lift the bans in effect. A broken quarantine record or notification history now says "cannot be confirmed" with the reason instead of "0", and is not overwritten. Broken files are kept as `.corrupt-…`, the newest 3.
- **Added: a notification for an `ssh -R` listener opened from another machine.** When an SSH session (a child of sshd) opens a listener, one notification is sent with the port, address, account and source (High to the channels and SIEM on the Server, a desktop notification on the client). Allow your own `-R` with `sudo roamswitch server allowlist add ssh-remote-listen <account>:<port>`. An `ssh -R` / `-D` tunnel started on this machine is also notified by the new rule EXR-016 (notify only) when it is a tunnel-only session (`-N`, `-f`), runs without a login session (a service, cron, a web application's child) or as a service account.
- **Fixed: the socket of a banned SSH source stayed open in sshd.** Because of that, `ssh-allow enable` could refuse by counting the banned attacker as a source connected right now. The socket is now closed at the ban (`SOCK_DESTROY`), and "also cut the connections" is written only when it was closed. `ssh-allow` counts only logged-in connections. When "brute force detected (not banned)" came first for a source, fail2ban's ban notification (with the command to lift it) was suppressed; that is fixed too. The notification of a successful login after brute force said "no action needed" even while an attacker's session was open; that sentence is gone.
- **Fixed: the web access guard did not ban the same source again after `unban`.** A source once reported was not counted again for the same kind. From about 2 seconds after `unban`, the same probing is banned again.
- **Fixed: removed the paths by which an Air-Gap release could go ahead unchecked** (the Linux GUI). Return and Space right after the emergency window appears press no button (choose one with a click or Tab). The tray's "release Air-Gap and restore the network" shows the same confirmation as the emergency window when the cause or the gateway cannot be confirmed. The "restored" notification is shown after the daemon has released, and is resent for up to about 37 seconds while the daemon restarts; if it does not get through, "Air-Gap is not released" is shown. A notification is also sent when sharing services could not be stopped or restored.
- **Fixed: turning the clock back put the dates of allowlist and acceptance records in the future.** Records are now dated by the current clock; only the expiry check uses the highest time seen so far. Once NTP has set the clock, allowances and acceptances that expired because the clock had been set forward work until their real expiry.
- **Fixed: restarting the daemon right after a decoy was read lost that notification from the history.** Notifications waiting to be bundled are sent before stopping.
- **Fixed: `emergency-restore` said "no isolation record" while traffic was blocked per service or per account.** It now shows the scope (a partial eBPF isolation, the isolation table, frozen processes). The action text of notifications such as a reverse shell names the unit actually chosen ("blocked the traffic of service x" and the like). `systemctl stop` on a frozen unit no longer waits 90 seconds or more (0.016 seconds in a VM).
- **Added: the webgate and the rollback watch are also reported by the main daemon when they exit abnormally.** systemd restarts `roamswitch-webgate` and `roamswitch-ransomware-rollback-watch` when they stop responding (the rollback watch when a watch loop has not moved for 300 seconds or more).
- **Fixed: the notification history stayed in the language it was recorded in.** Notifications recorded from 1.17.38 on are shown in the reader's language in `roamswitch notifications`, the GUI's notifications tab and CSV, the MCP `get_notification_history` and the Server TUI (values such as addresses, paths and command lines, and records up to 1.17.37, are kept as they are). The text sent to the channels (Telegram and the like) and the pop-ups is unchanged. A Fleet notification is kept once in the history (it was twice). The headings and "blind spot" texts of `detection-coverage` are in 10 languages too.
- **Fixed: in English, German, French, Spanish, Italian and Portuguese, a plural or a "(s)" was shown even for 1.** The form now matches the number.
- **Fixed: an autostart file or a FIM-watched file deleted and put back with the same content was not detected again.** Putting it back is notified, and the client also lowers trust. On the client, fixing the protection level by hand now also sends "protection profile switched".
- **Fixed: a cloned VM or a freshly upgraded host reported a suspected wtmp wipe at every start.** The first start after upgrading to 1.17.38 rebuilds the baseline once (that start does not compare edits made while stopped, and leaves one line in the timeline). An old decoy that has gone no longer shows `/root/.ssh/id_rsa (deleted)` in the update output and FIM notifications. System accounts created by a package's own scripts are taken in even while the daemon is stopped.
- **Fixed: `roamswitch install` ended with "✓ done" although it could not create the sandbox (bwrap).** It now stops before the download and exits with code 1; a failure partway also exits with 1.

**Changed (affects scripts)**

- Color codes: none are added when writing to a pipe or a file, or with `NO_COLOR=1`. Use `--color=always` to add them.
- Exit codes: `quarantine list` as an ordinary user no longer says "0 items"; it points to sudo and returns 1. `forward test` returns 2 when something failed and 1 when there is no destination (it returned 0). `events search` / `export` with `--pid ""` and the like return 2. `roamswitch-ransomware-rollback`, `roamswitch-honeytokens` and `roamswitch-server-tui` writing to a closed pipe exit with 141 without a panic message. `roamswitch-prn-recorder status | head` no longer panics either.
- Output: `text` is added to the records of `roamswitch notifications --json` and MCP. The kind marks of `fim verify` and the TUI change from `[NEW_FILE]` to `[new file]` (`[新しいファイル]` in Japanese) and the like. `roamswitch sensor results` and the TUI's "results" print a report instead of JSON. Some fixed English texts change (the SIEM detail's "output path(s):" becomes "output:", for example; old records' English is still read back). `unreadableReason` is added to the MCP `get_quarantine_status` and `unreadableFiles` to `get_notification_history` (existing keys are unchanged). The last line of postinst states the service's actual state (it waits up to 60 seconds while the service is starting).
- Diagnosis: the sudo audit for an ordinary user, Wi-Fi when the encryption cannot be read over wireless, ARP when `/proc/net/arp` cannot be read, and SSH enabled but stopped are now "unconfirmed" (they were passed or not applicable). A one-shot backup service with neither a timer nor cron fails. The count in the heading of `status --server` matches the rows of the table.
- Settings: `server config set agent_users` with `root` in it is an error. `server allowlist add ssh-remote-listen` accepts only the form `<account>:<port>` and refuses one without a port or with `--exe`. Saving with `config set` is atomic (the inode changes; it stays 0600 and root), and leaves `server.conf.corrupt-<time>` when there are lines it cannot read.
- Permissions: CSV and Markdown newly exported from the GUI are 0600 (an existing file that is overwritten keeps its mode). After `sudo roamswitch audit-logs` and the like, `~/.config/roamswitch` stays owned by the user (one an earlier version made root's is given back at the next sudo run).
- Network: the signed data is fetched once right after the first install (not where policy-rc.d forbids starting services).

**Other fixes**

- The Server's `uninstall` also removes the blocking rules it put in Docker's DOCKER-USER chain. The ✓ list of `uninstall` is shown once, and its opening line names "ARP lock" only when there is a record of it.
- An external command writing more than 64 MiB to one stream is stopped there and treated as a failure. Results of Sensor audits and NSE over 64 KiB can now be read (they used to come back empty at the timeout).
- When the source was judged spoofed and the scanner was not banned, a notification gives the reason. The hold of a USB stick with a different fingerprint says that its identity changed.
- English FIM notifications cut a public key on the command line partway instead of masking it; it is now collapsed into `SHA256:<fingerprint>`. Secrets containing `/`, such as AWS secret keys, and long tokens inside URLs are masked too.
- `timeline` is in time order. Adding one MCP definition gives one notification. The same exec written twice to the record file is fixed. The file name of the FIM first-look report now has milliseconds.
- In the TUI, `n` at the confirmation before saving cancels. `ports -a` no longer marks a listener on 127.0.0.1 only as "blocked from outside by the firewall". The Japanese kernel-modules on the Server says "this machine".
- The instructions the Server's second-stage investigation gives the LLM are in English, and the answer is written in the notification language.
- Checks: all crate tests (without the eBPF XDP crate) pass: 4965 tests (0 failed, 17 ignored; Docker). The package-script tests (`test_server_postinst.sh` 76, `test_client_postinst.sh` 47, `test_fim_package_hooks.sh` 11) and `audit_no_network.sh` pass too.

### 1.17.37

Fixes for defects found while running the learning-site lessons for real in disposable VMs. If your scripts rely on exit codes or on the words a report prints, check the "Changed (affects scripts)" items.

- **Fixed: blocking of Docker's published ports did not take effect.** RoamSwitch appended its DROP to the end of the DOCKER-USER chain, so it sat behind the `-j RETURN` that Docker puts there and never matched (Ubuntu's docker.io 24.0.7). The once-a-minute check did not look at the order of the rules either. The DROP is now inserted right after RoamSwitch's own rules, ahead of Docker's RETURN, and the check puts it back when the order is broken. While a DROP sits behind a RETURN, the DOCKER-USER item of `status --server` is a warning. On the Server Edition, the default-deny of RoamSwitch's forward chain was already dropping this traffic, but the DOCKER-USER layer was not working.
- **Fixed: a package update took tampering unrelated to the update into the file-integrity (FIM) baseline.** The apt, RPM and pacman hooks ran `fim update --quiet`, which takes every file as it is at that moment, so a key an attacker had added to `authorized_keys` went into the baseline too. Files written during the update by something other than apt were also taken in without a notification. The hooks now record the state before and after the update (`fim package-begin` / `fim package-end`) and take in only changes whose owner is a package of that update and whose content matches the package's record. Files outside a package's file list, such as `sshd_config`, are not taken in but notified (once you have checked them, run `sudo roamswitch fim update`). For `passwd`, `group`, `shadow` and `gshadow`, only system accounts a package adds are taken in, with a one-line note in the apt output. That needs lines that were only added, UIDs and GIDs below 1000 and not 0, no login, a locked password, and a writer that is an account tool (`useradd`, `adduser`, `systemd-sysusers` and the like) started by a package manager (dpkg, apt, rpm, pacman and the like), both root-owned in system folders. UID 0, an account that can log in, a change to an existing line and an account added outside a package manager are still not taken in and raise a critical notification, as before (with dnf4 and yum the writer cannot be confirmed, so nothing is taken in). The number of changes left out is printed in the apt and pacman output and keeps showing in `fim verify`. `fim update --quiet` no longer retakes the whole baseline (`fim update` does).
- **Fixed: a web shell placed by rename was not detected at once** (Server Edition). The watch on the web roots only saw a finished write (`FAN_CLOSE_WRITE`). A file moved in from a temporary folder by `move_uploaded_file()` or `mv` stayed until the scheduled scan, 120 seconds after start and then every 6 hours. Moves (`FAN_MOVED_TO`) are now watched as well, and the file is scanned and quarantined at once (about 0.05 seconds in a VM). When a whole folder is moved in, up to 2,000 files and 16 levels inside it are read. On kernels older than 5.9 only the scheduled scan runs, as before.
- **Fixed: the log audit showed keys almost in full.** The "template" of a new pattern was redacted after its digits had been folded into `<NUM>`, so `AKIAIOSFODNN7EXAMPLE` became `AKIAIOSFODNN<NUM>EXAMPLE`, no longer looked like a key, and showed up in `audit-logs`, the Server TUI, the GUI's copied report and the MCP `audit_security_logs`. The baseline file `log_template_baseline.json` also stored the unredacted templates and the last line read as they were, world-readable at 0644. Redaction now runs before folding (`aws_access_key_id=<SECRET>`), the baseline file is 0600 and the last line is stored as a hash. Notification bodies and the notification history were already redacted.
- **Fixed: a high-confidence isolation opened just because the process that caused it had exited.** Isolations from canary tampering, a ransomware entropy burst, eBPF Critical / Emergency / Alert, a repeated rule and a HIGH execution rule were released at the first recheck after a short timer (about 5 minutes on the Server, about 10 minutes on the client) once the process that had finished encrypting exited. A process that has exited or a rule that has gone quiet has only stopped; that is no proof the incident is over. Such isolations are no longer released automatically: they move to degraded at the cap (60 minutes by default), and degraded does not open with time either. The only isolation released automatically is one caused by ARP spoofing alone, once the gateway has been confirmed to keep answering with its trusted MAC. Low-confidence isolations (such as Falco's warning stage) are still released by their timer, as before. The isolation notification now says how to release it.
- **Fixed: the Server Edition's ransomware decoys could be fetched from the web server.** Decoys were placed in document roots such as `/var/www/html`, and `curl http://127.0.0.1/.roamswitch_security_canary_do_not_delete.pdf` returned 200. Folders a web server serves (`/var/www/html`, `/srv/http` and the document roots in the Apache, nginx and Caddy configuration) no longer get decoys, and the ones an earlier version placed there (files whose content is exactly a decoy's) are removed at start. Document roots are covered by the entropy watch (see `entropy_docroot_action` below).
- **Fixed: the SSH decoy key is now a real-format key made on each host.** Every ssh run by root and by `agent_users` accounts printed "Load key … error in libcrypto". `deploy` now makes a throwaway ed25519 key with the host's `ssh-keygen` (no passphrase, registered nowhere, marked in its comment); the file name stays `id_rsa`. An ordinary ssh offers the key to the server and is turned down, and is not notified. `ssh -i ~/.ssh/id_rsa`, `ssh-add ~/.ssh/id_rsa` and ssh run with `LD_PRELOAD` and the like are notified as a decoy read. Old decoys are rewritten at the next start. On a host without openssh-client the SSH decoy is not placed and `roamswitch doctor` says why. The false decoy alerts on every `docker` and `aws` run by root are gone, and a read right after a cascade decoy (such as `~/.kube/config`) is placed is no longer missed.
- **Fixed: FIM did not notify about new files in watched folders until the scheduled check.** A file placed in `/etc/sudoers.d`, `/etc/cron.d`, `/etc/systemd/system` and the like is now notified at once, with the writing process and its login. The `authorized_keys` of an account that had no `~/.ssh` at start, or of an account added later, is watched from then on without `fim update`. A file placed by `mv` is reported as "placed by a move (mv), so the writing process cannot be identified".
- **Fixed: `roamswitch airgap` with no argument actually cut the network.** The default with no argument was `test`, and `test` did the same as `enable`. With no argument it now shows the state and the usage only; only `enable` cuts the network. On a terminal without a display, `airgap enable` no longer claims to have shown the emergency recovery window and tells you to run `roamswitch airgap disable` on that terminal.

**Changed (affects scripts)**

- Exit code of `roamswitch scan-vulns`: 1 when there is at least one ✗ finding (a confirmed vulnerability or misconfiguration), 0 when there is none, 2 when the scan could not run (the reason goes to standard error). ℹ items (nmap's reports and information such as the SSH banner) are not counted, so a host running only sshd gets 0. It is 2 for a dry run without `--confirm`, when `active_vuln_scan_enabled` is not true, when nmap is not installed (and nothing was found), and when roamswitch-mcp cannot be reached. Until now it was 0 whatever the result (1 when MCP could not be reached). To carry on when there are findings, handle 1, for example `roamswitch scan-vulns --confirm || [ $? -eq 1 ]`.
- Exit code of `roamswitch audit-secrets`: 1 when it finds anything, 0 when it finds nothing, and 2 when there is no argument or when an argument that looks like a path does not exist (all of these were 0). A missing path is no longer checked as a string; it says the path was not found. To check a string that looks like a path as a string, add `--text`. `scan-frontend` now also reads `.env`, `.env.*` and `*.env` in the published folder.
- On the Server Edition, `emergency-restore` and `uninstall` ask you to type `RESTORE` (`UNINSTALL` for `uninstall`) instead of y/N when the host is isolated and the cause cannot be confirmed gone. A non-interactive run needs `--force` (the client edition already worked this way). `emergency-restore` and `uninstall` return 1 when cancelled at the confirmation and 2 when they did not run because there was no terminal (a cancel used to return 0).
- In Japanese, the marks of `status --server` and of the Server TUI changed from `[PASS]` / `[WARN]` / `[UNCONFIRMED]` to `[合格]` / `[要対策]` / `[確認不能]`. Items that do not apply are `[該当なし]` (`[N/A]` in English). The English output keeps its words. `roamswitch status` now prints its marks and headings in the other 8 languages too.
- The diagnosis score and counts leave out items that do not apply and items that could not be judged. `totalChecks` is the number of items judged, and a new `notApplicableChecks` is returned. Because items that do not apply no longer count as passed, the score goes down on many hosts. These items newly fail or warn as well: sshd still allowing password authentication, `/tmp` or `/dev/shm` without noexec (a warning on Ubuntu's default), cloud-init's `NOPASSWD:ALL`, and a new kernel installed without a reboot (the kernel CVE item becomes unconfirmed). When an ordinary user cannot read `docker.sock` or `containerd.sock`, the Docker and container-runtime items are unconfirmed instead of not applicable. A host with containerd only is judged as containerd's default runc instead of not applicable.
- A high-confidence isolation is not released automatically when the process that caused it exits (see the fix above). Release it with `emergency-restore` or from the GUI; once it has moved to degraded after the cap, `server ack` on the Server releases it too.
- Other exit codes: `server test-notify` returns 1 when sending fails and when there is no channel at all. `sensor pair`, `sensor request-audit` and `sensor unpair` return 1 when refused and when no Sensor is paired, none matches or several match. `server allowlist remove|renew` returns 1 when there is no such entry (the message goes to standard error). `sharing on|off` returns 1 when refused or when it cannot write (the setting is left unchanged). `forward queue` returns 1 when it cannot read the queue without sudo. `airgap` with an unknown argument returns 1. `roamswitch --version` prints the version and exits with 0.

**Added**

- **Added: `entropy_docroot_action` (Server Edition).** Set to `freeze_writer`, it freezes the writer of an encryption in which a web service replaces existing files in a document root (15 or more replacements among 20 files in 5 seconds); the host is not isolated. Only a writer that is neither root nor in a login session, and that runs as an account such as `www-data` or in a web server or PHP-FPM unit, counts as a web service, so a deployment run with `sudo -u www-data` is left alone. The default is `notify` (notify only), as before.
- **Added: the command line and account of short-lived processes are taken with eBPF** (new crate `roamswitch-exec-bpf`). The `sched_process_exec` tracepoint reads argv (up to 2047 bytes) and the uids, so the command shows in the execution record and in notifications even for a process that exited at once. Environment variables and other memory are not read. Where BPF is not available, the reason is logged at start and everything works as before.
- **Added: a notification when the daemon exited abnormally and was restarted.** When the Server or client daemon exits abnormally (`kill -9`, a crash, an OOM kill, the watchdog) and systemd restarts it, the next start sends one notification. On the Server it goes to the notification history, the configured channels (Telegram, LINE, Webhook), SIEM forwarding and the timeline; on the client, to the desktop notification, the notification history and the timeline. During a crash loop, it is bundled into one notification an hour with the count. Nothing is sent after `systemctl stop` / `restart`, a package update, an OS reboot or a power loss. The Server unit is now `Type=notify`, `NotifyAccess=main` and `WatchdogSec=30` (the output of `systemctl cat roamswitch-server` changes); a hang is restarted after 30 seconds and notified as well. To tell these cases apart, it creates `server_running.json` and `server_abnormal_exit.json` (Server) and `client_running.json` and `client_abnormal_exit.json` (client) in `/var/lib/roamswitch/`.
- **Added: `sudo roamswitch quarantine restore <ID> [<path>]`, `quarantine delete <ID>` and `quarantine delete --all`.** The CLI could only list until now. A restore does not overwrite and does not set the execute bit.
- **Added: `sensor request-audit` works on the Server Edition.** The request used to reach only the client edition's daemon and always failed. The Server daemon now fetches the result every 5 minutes (up to 25 minutes) and sends it to the notification channels.
- **Added: notifications and guidance when used together with fail2ban or CrowdSec.** When fail2ban or CrowdSec bans an SSH source, a notification is sent within 30 seconds; when the source is one with an open session, a lock-out warning with the command to lift it is sent. `unban` shows each tool's own command to lift a ban. `bans` also lists the bans of the web access guard, with their kind and time left.
- **Added: the detection record (timeline) stores a code and values for the kind of sentence (`summary_code`).** For rows of the SSH guard, FIM, DNS, Sigma, canaries, isolation, ransomware, the eBPF guard and others, `roamswitch timeline`, the TUI and the GUI can show the summary in the display language, whatever language it was recorded in. Old records are read as before. The Server's `timeline` also shows port-scan rows.

**Other fixes**

- The parent-process lineage in the timeline uses the same names as the notification, and is filled in from the execution record for processes that have exited; when a pid has been reused by another process, no lineage is shown. Notifications of an exec inside a container show the container and process names (they said "container: unknown").
- The Server's `roamswitch ports` no longer calls a port the firewall drops from outside "fully exposed"; it says it is blocked from outside by the firewall.
- Log-wipe detection notifies again when a log is wiped a second time, and reports wipes made while the daemon was stopped right after it starts. The false wtmp "rewrite" alert after a forced stop is gone.
- Protection levels: `roamswitch guards` and MCP show the level the daemon actually applies. Hand edits to `config.json` take effect in about 3 seconds.
- USB: a device re-plugged within about a second is held and notified like a new device.
- Resource Guard: fixed the CPU sampling period and the crash-loop counting (too few RSS samples are no longer called "correlated"). The "eBPF runtime guard is not running" notification is now once a day across restarts.
- Diagnosis: in English, `status --server` printed the sentence of the container-runtime line twice. The timeline item said "no incidents recorded yet" while there were records; it now shows the count and the latest time. `status --server --json` now prints JSON, and on the Server `roamswitch status` is headed with the Server Edition's 42 items. Viewing `status --server` as root no longer silences the notification about a weakened setting.
- `roamswitch doctor`: the permission check now covers every installed RoamSwitch file (including `/usr/lib/roamswitch` and the systemd units), not just four executables; the Server's `roamswitch-server-daemon` was never checked before. It no longer warns that an inactive ufw is running.
- `scan-vulns`: failed nmap scripts and empty results are no longer findings. A web server whose document root is `/` (such as `python3 -m http.server -d /`) was reported as "path traversal"; it is now the ✗ finding "System Files Served (Document Root Is /)". On the Server Edition, the message when the scan is off explains how to turn it on there.
- Canary notification: when the culprit was known but had already exited and could not be frozen, it said the culprit could not be identified; it now gives the name and PID and says it had already exited. `emergency-restore` shows the gateway MAC lines only for an isolation caused by ARP. During an isolation, `server ebpf` no longer says "safe" and explains that the isolation comes from another detection.
- After `server config set webhook_url` or another destination or token, a hint to set `*_enabled true` is shown if that channel is still off. The usage of `roamswitch-honeytokens` follows the terminal's language.
- Evidence bundles are created readable by root only. Exported notification files are 0600 and owned by the user who ran sudo. The GUI and CLI say why when they cannot save settings or allowlists, and do not overwrite a broken file.
- Fixed wording and display language in `forward status`, `doctor`, `audit-logs`, `vuln-accept`, `server config set`, `prn-recorder status` and others. The days left on an acceptance count the first day as one, like the allowlist. Accepting a KEV finding that has a fixed version shows a caution.
- Checks: the final run of all crate tests (without the eBPF XDP crate), in a disposable Ubuntu 24.04 VM with the Server running, passed 4722 tests (0 failed, 17 ignored). The package-script tests (`test_server_postinst.sh` 56, `test_fim_package_hooks.sh` 11) pass too.

### 1.17.36

Fixes for defects found while running the learning-site lessons for real in disposable VMs.

- **Fixed: file-integrity monitoring (FIM) did not notify about a second tampering after the file was put back.** Adding a line to `/root/.ssh/authorized_keys` (notified), restoring it and adding it again gave no detection and no notification the second time; after a daemon restart it detected again. The record that stops the same tampering from being notified repeatedly is now cleared once the file is confirmed to match the baseline again. Writing the same tampered content again still does not notify, as before.
- **Fixed: on the Server edition, `frozen resume <PID>` did not lift the account-wide network block that was put up for the freeze.** That user's traffic kept failing until `emergency-restore`. Resuming now lifts that block too (for the last one, the whole isolation table; the host-wide isolation is not lifted). Using `frozen resume` on a process that is not frozen no longer reports success: it says "this process is not frozen and has no network block" and exits with code 1.
- **Fixed: after `roamswitch uninstall` (without `--purge`), installing again with apt left the service disabled.** The reinstall was treated as an upgrade and neither enabled nor started the service. `uninstall` now leaves a marker, and a reinstall enables and starts the service.
- **Fixed: the SSH brute-force guard.** (1) After `unban` cleared the failure count, your own next login raised a critical "login succeeded after a brute-force attempt" alert; it no longer does. (2) The ban notice's "also cut N connections it had open" equalled the number of failed attempts; it now counts the connections actually cut. (3) `ssh-allow status` listed the source of one SSH session twice; once now. (4) After `ssh-allow disable`, `status` still said "enabled" for about 30 seconds; it now says "disabled" at once. (5) The Japanese `bans` table was misaligned because of full-width characters.
- **Added: `roamswitch doctor` warns when the port sshd listens on is not in `ssh_ports` (10 languages).** For example, with sshd on port 2222 and `ssh_ports` still 22, you may not be able to get in over SSH after an emergency isolation. It stays quiet when sshd is not running, when `server.conf` cannot be read, and when sshd listens on loopback only.
- **Fixed: `roamswitch forward status` / `test`.** Without sudo, `status` said "enabled: no" while forwarding was running; it now says sudo is needed because the settings cannot be read. After you turned forwarding off and restarted, it kept showing the old "running" counts and the old error; it now says "the daemon is not forwarding". The "last error" stayed after a retry succeeded; it is cleared. The "retry queue=0 (items / 0 bytes)" format is now "retry queue=0 items / 0 bytes".
- **Fixed: the diagnosis counted items it could not confirm as passed.** Calling the MCP `get_security_report` (Server) as an ordinary user reported the firewall item as "inactive" when it simply could not be read. It is now reported as inconclusive with "insufficient permissions". Inconclusive items count neither toward passed checks nor toward the score (a new `inconclusiveChecks` field is returned). **The score often goes up, because inconclusive items leave the denominator** (88 to 90 for an ordinary user in a VM). The client edition's `roamswitch status` and the GUI score use the same counting.
- **Fixed: the display language of `roamswitch doctor`.** In an English session, only the honeytoken line stayed in Japanese. A `roamswitch-updater` run while updates are switched off no longer counts as the "last success".
- **Fixed: the "unknown configuration key" error of `roamswitch server config set` was in Japanese even when `language` was `en`.** It is now in 10 languages like the other errors. `audit-mcp-configs` now says "the list above" instead of "the list below" (the list of files that could not be parsed is above that sentence).
- Checks: all crate tests pass (same flags as CI, without the GUI crate). Tests added: 3 for FIM, 3 for `frozen`, 19 for reinstall (16 shell, 3 Rust), 4 for `doctor` and `forward`, 8 for MCP, 5 for the SSH guard.

### 1.10.0 - 1.17.35 (summarized)

Everything in these releases, grouped by topic (Client and Server Edition unless marked). The full text of each entry is in the git history of this file.

- (1.17.35) **Fixed: the incident "action taken" was shown in English even on a Japanese screen.** The daemon stores the action as a fixed English string such as `Logged & Monitored (guard policy: no containment)` or `Process Frozen (SIGSTOP) + Network Blocked`. The stored value is matched elsewhere, so it is unchanged; it is translated only when shown and when notified (for example 「記録のみ(ガードの設定により、遮断しない)」, "record only, the guard policy does not contain"). This covers `roamswitch timeline` and `incident-summary`, the Server TUI, the GUI timeline, the Server notification (the "autonomous containment action" line) and the action part of the timeline summary. Other languages are unchanged.
- (1.17.35) **Fixed: the Japanese wording of the `roamswitch scan-frontend` advice.** Awkward phrases were rewritten (the Japanese text now says to treat a key or token as leaked if the files were already published, to revoke it at its issuer and create a new one, to keep secrets out of the build, and to publish source maps only to people who need them). The English text is unchanged.
- (1.17.35) **Fixed: nmap's `http-security-headers` is now an information-only script.** It only lists the security headers that came back and does not report a problem, so it no longer appears as a finding that reads "no problem found" (in the verification result and in the Sensor export). The other script that checks for missing headers stays.
- (1.17.35) Checks: the tests for the new translation and the existing notification and TUI tests pass. The full crate tests run in CI on main before the release.
- (1.17.34) **Fixed: a listener on port 8000 was reported as "vLLM exposed" by its port number alone.** 8000 is vLLM's default, but also Django's, FastAPI's, `python -m http.server`'s and the published port of many containers; a container's published port on 8000 raised the finding. The vLLM finding now needs the process name or the path of its executable to say `vllm` (a Python run from a `vllm` environment counts). Any other listener on 8000 gets only the product's own "likely" tier of local AI runtimes, at low.
- (1.17.34) **Fixed: the Sensor audit result export no longer lists nmap scripts that only read something out of a service, and its headline no longer reads as "safe" when ports are open.** An audit of two web ports showed a page of lines (the page title, the server's clock, the headers) under "no problem found". Those scripts report no problem and are left out; the ones that check something (risky methods, a weak certificate, missing security headers) stay. When nothing was flagged but ports are open, the line now says "No known problem was found. The open ports are listed below (this is not proof that it is safe)" (10 languages).
- (1.17.34) Checks: all 4340 crate tests pass (same flags as CI, in Docker; 16 ignored).
- (1.17.33) **Fixed: the isolation status kept saying "isolated" after the isolation rules were gone.** When the isolation nftables tables disappear without going through a release (a reboot, a reinstall of the service), `/var/lib/roamswitch/ebpf_status.json` stayed at "isolated", and `roamswitch server ebpf`, the TUI and MCP went on reporting an isolation that no longer existed (on a real machine, a state from September 26 was still there). Every 15 seconds the daemon now sets the status back to released, but only when all three hold: both tables (`roamswitch_isolate` and `roamswitch_emergency`) are confirmed absent (nothing is decided when nft does not answer), the status was written at least 60 seconds ago, and there is no isolation record. A SIGSTOP-only freeze and the frozen processes are left alone.
- (1.17.33) **Fixed: the secret scan printed "(safe)" when it found nothing.** Finding nothing means no key in a known format was present, which is not proof of safety (a bare AWS secret access key, which has no fixed format, cannot be detected). The word is removed from the CLI, the GUI and the MCP summary in all 10 languages, which now say only that nothing was detected.
- (1.17.33) **Fixed: a .deb that only points to a snap (`thunderbird 2:1snap1-0ubuntu5`, `firefox 1:1snap1-…`) was listed in the package vulnerability match with CVEs that had no fix.** The version of such a .deb is a placeholder, not the version of the snap that actually runs, so it always looked older than the fixed version (`2:140.x`). On a real Ubuntu 26.04 machine, Thunderbird showed CVE-2022-25236 as "no fix yet". Versions of this form are no longer judged. The version of the snap itself is not read, so the Thunderbird and Firefox snaps are outside this match.
- (1.17.32) **Fixed: the log audit notified "check whether you recognize this" for a harmless AppArmor denial of a snap app.** A denial counts as harmless only if the process name matches the profile's own program, but the kernel cuts a process name to 15 bytes and a snap profile is named `snap.<snap>.<app>`, so the same program was judged a mismatch. It showed up live when the Firmware Updater snap (`firmware-notifier`) merely read `/proc/sys/vm/max_map_count`. For a snap the program is now the last part of the profile name, for an ordinary program the last part of the path, and a 15-byte process name is accepted as the first 15 bytes of a longer program name. A denial for a different program (including one that only shares a prefix), a write, an execute, or a sensitive file (`/etc/shadow`, `~/.ssh` and so on) is still notified as before.
- (1.17.31) **Added: a GitLab server (the Omnibus package `gitlab-ce` / `gitlab-ee`, or a source install) is now part of the package vulnerability scan.** GitLab is in no distribution's security data, and the Ruby it bundles is not a package, so the scan never reported it. CVE-2026-97229 (CVSS 10, code execution without logging in) and CVE-2026-85706 (reading any file) were in CISA's KEV list and were missed even on a GitLab two releases behind. The scan reads the release from the `VERSION` file of the Rails application (`/opt/gitlab/embedded/service/gitlab-rails/VERSION`, or `/home/git/gitlab/VERSION`), runs nothing, and compares it with the `gitlab` rows (one range per release line, from NVD) of the map file that the Node.js and PHP checks already use. The "exploited vulnerabilities" item of the diagnosis uses the same comparison. Measured on an Ubuntu 24.04 VM: 19.2.7 is reported for CVE-2026-97229 (fixed in 19.2.8), 18.11.0 for CVE-2026-85706 (fixed in 18.11.12), and 19.2.8 is clean. A GitLab inside a container is not looked at. The rows reach a device with its next map refresh.
- (1.17.31) **Fixed: on an Ubuntu kernel that the Ubuntu fixed-version rows do not cover (an HWE kernel, or a flavour such as `-aws`), the "known kernel CVE exposure" item no longer fails when no kernel update is pending.** Upstream version ranges cannot tell a kernel that was rebuilt from a newer source with the fixes backported. On the newest HWE kernel of Ubuntu 24.04 (7.0.0-42, nothing pending) the item listed 517 CVEs as "action needed" and told the user to update and reboot. It now says it cannot tell (a pass that is not scored), with the reason on the same line. With an update pending, or when that cannot be determined, it still fails.
- (1.17.30) **Added: 31 threat-actor reference briefs as MCP resources, `roamswitch://threat-briefs/{actor}`.** 20 nation-state groups (macOS realism, entry vector, and a mapping to existing features) and 11 commercial-spyware IOC references (domain and hash lists). Commercial-spyware domains are not loaded into any blocklist (reference only): the vendors' own infrastructure has often already been retired and the domains reassigned, so blocking them now risks blocking an unrelated current owner for no detection value. Same content as the Mac edition.
- (1.17.30) **Added: MCP tool calls are logged to a local file** (`~/.config/roamswitch/mcp_tool_call_log.jsonl`). Only the tool name and the names of its argument keys are recorded, never argument values (such as file paths). Same rule as the Mac edition.
- (1.17.30) **Fixed: a snap package's automatic update reloading its AppArmor profile was wrongly reported by the log auditor as a "new pattern."** AppArmor's audit line embeds the package name inside a quoted attribute (`name="snap.<package>.<app>"`), which didn't match the existing exclusion's assumption of a leading space before "snap" — so it went through unfiltered. Found live when Bitwarden's snap auto-update triggered this false positive; the exclusion now also covers the `apparmor_parser` + `profile_replace` context.
- (1.17.30) **Fixed: the result of the active verification is readable.** nmap's scripts that only read something out of a service (the page title, the server's clock, the headers, the state of a port) are no longer listed as findings, and the nmap output that the GUI text left out is shown. nmap's own report has its own evidence kind, `externalToolReport` (SARIF level `warning`, not confirmed); the CLI marks it with a yellow `ℹ` instead of a red `✗`. The heading for checks that did not finish now says `Items that could not be checked` instead of `unreachable or timed out` (10 languages), because a check that gets an answer but finds nothing to test is not a connection failure.
- (1.17.29) **Added: a warning when AnyDesk, ScreenConnect/ConnectWise, TeamViewer or ngrok is downloaded from a source that isn't that vendor's own official site.** These tools have legitimate business uses, so a name match alone never triggers anything; it only sends a desktop notification (never quarantines or blocks), and stays silent when the download's source (the `user.xdg.origin.url` extended attribute) is unknown. Same rule as the Mac edition.
