The port-scan nft rule carries a named counter `syn_logged`; when the counter rises but no line reaches the journal for three windows in a row, it reports `log_not_seen` (the Server Edition port-scan detection has the same watch; the egress, metadata and agent-egress rules do not). `roamswitch exec status` gives the reason when the execution record is disabled or off by default on a small host. With `log_audit_enabled=false`, crash-loop detection does not run even if `resource_guard_enabled` is on, so it is reported as `part_depends_on`. In the SUID watch (Server Edition) the first scan could not finish within 300 seconds, so the baseline was never made. It now waits up to 30 minutes and doubles the wait (up to 4 hours) each time it times out, and reports `no_baseline` with progress until the baseline exists. The warning log is written once a day.
- (1.17.0) **Fixed: container exec guard, DNS tunnel guard and DNS sinkhole** (Server Edition): when the input could not be read, they still showed as running if the setting was on. An unreadable input is now counted. The DNS tunnel guard also counts it as a failure, and retries, when `nft -f -` could not load its table. The DNS sinkhole records the NIC the daemon configured in `/var/lib/roamswitch/dns_sinkhole_applied.json`, and turning it off and restarting restores DNS on that NIC only. A DNS setting someone else made later is left alone. When `resolvectl` is missing, the reason is shown. **A host that set the sinkhole with an earlier version has no record, so DNS is not restored by turning it off until it has been turned on once with this version.**
- (1.17.0) **Fixed: web detection, urgent-notification channels and threats that could not be quarantined** (Server Edition): when the Web Rules have 0 targets, the web access guard follows 0 logs while a web server is running, or the error logs for the web worker crash watch number 0, it warns once at start and reports `no_source`. A stopped Web gate went unnoticed because nginx still passes requests; the gate is now reported and notified when the `ts` in `webgate.status` is older than 60 seconds. The roots and logs being followed are shown by `roamswitch web-rules status`, the Server TUI, `roamswitch doctor` and `status --server`. At start, if no channel can be used or a channel lacks credentials, this is logged and reported as `alertChannels`; a permanent failure is kept in `/var/lib/roamswitch/notify_last_failure.json`. **With no channel, or with a channel that is enabled but has an empty token, destination or URL, an urgent detection reaches nobody and stays only in `journalctl` and `roamswitch notifications`.** Check delivery with `sudo roamswitch server test-notify`. When a file found by YARA or ClamAV could not be quarantined, only a count was logged; the file and the reason are now notified as a detection.
- (1.17.0) **Fixed: Link Guard, shared services, ARP, file scan, `server.conf`, the incident record and AI agent setup**: the Link Guard writes whether it could install its nft table to the state file and shows it in status and MCP. The automatic stop of shared services stops `ssh.socket` first. The GUI and fleet scores use the measured ARP result. The file scan keeps the text of its errors in the log and the state file. Unknown keys and invalid values in `server.conf` are logged at start, and an unreadable file is told apart from one hidden by `server.json`. A hand-edited value does not take effect until a restart, so when one of the 11 keys read at start has changed, "restart required" is shown. A broken incident record looked like "no incidents"; a broken file is now moved to `.bak` before writing, and the reader tells it apart from "no record" (an unreadable file is never overwritten). On the client edition, AI agent setup change detection does not watch continuously and compares only when you run `audit-agent-setup`; the state and the screen text now say so (10 languages).
- (1.17.0) **Added: `roamswitch doctor` checks per feature**: the commands a switched-on feature uses, the update timer and the latest update, mistakes in the config files, places the daemon cannot write (including the homes of `agent_users`), the size of the egress block list, and protections that are not working. The Server Edition also shows the number of Web Rules document roots, the number of logs the web access guard follows, the urgent-notification channels, the `agent_users` accounts and whether they have a baseline, whether the DNS sinkhole is applied, and the allowed UDP ports.
- (1.17.0) **Added: `allowed_udp_ports` to allow incoming UDP ports** (Server Edition, `server.conf`): the default-deny firewall passed only TCP and dropped every UDP packet that was not a reply, so WireGuard, DNS, NTP or HTTP/3 (443/udp) on the same host could not be reached. `allowed_ports` is TCP only. Ports written in `allowed_udp_ports` (comma separated, 1 to 65535) are passed in the input of the same nft table and in the forward that carries DNAT to containers. **The default is empty, and with it empty the nft content is exactly what it was before.**
- (1.17.0) **Changed: `sudo roamswitch server setup` and the after-install message now match what actually happens** (Server Edition): step 2 says only TCP is accepted and UDP goes in `allowed_udp_ports`. Step 7 stops asking about behaviors that cannot work without Falco or Tetragon and shows how to install them. Step 9 says only the directories you name are scanned, and asks for them first. Step 11 lists the protections that work by default from the current settings. **Step 13 (new) asks about the accounts for AI agents (`agent_users`)**; the default answer to the approval is N, and nothing is approved silently. It warns when there is no notification target, or when one is enabled but empty, and prints commands to check the result at the end. The first deb and rpm install says "next, run `sudo roamswitch server setup`" and lists what is not configured yet. **On an upgrade, it enables the signed-data update timer if it is not enabled and `cve_kernel_map_updates_enabled=false` is not set.** A host with `false` stays stopped.
- (1.17.0) **Fixed: guidance of `audit-agent-setup` and `roamswitch xdp-gate enable`**: when `audit-agent-setup` finds nothing, it lists the places it checks, how to make a baseline and what is watched after `--accept` (changes are announced within about a second). `~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/settings.json` and `~/.gemini/config/mcp_config.json` are now audited. `xdp-gate enable` stops without changing anything when the eBPF object (`/usr/lib/roamswitch/ebpf/roamswitch-xdp-gate.o`) is missing, and says the standard package does not carry it. The doctor item `server_xdp_boot_gate` is no longer a fixed pass and is judged from the real state. A pin path used to check the boot gate had a name the loader never creates; it is fixed. **The Server Edition deb and rpm do not carry the `roamswitch-xdp-gate` binary and unit, so on the Server Edition `xdp-gate enable` now always stops and says why.**
- (1.17.0) **Added: a mark on active-check results when the same check was clean last time** (`run_active_vuln_scan`, `previouslySafeAt` in the MCP output): it appears only when the last verdict for the same check on the same port was "no problem". It does not claim that a fix came back; it returns only the fact that is in the log. It has the same form as on the Mac.
- (1.17.0) **What you need to do**: a Server Edition that uses `agent_users` should audit each account with `sudo roamswitch audit-agent-setup --user NAME`, read the result, and make the baseline with `--accept`. Until then, changes in that account are not watched. Decoys cannot be placed in `/home`, so decoys for `agent_users` need `ReadWritePaths=-/home/<account>` in `/etc/systemd/system/roamswitch-server.service.d/agent-users.conf` (step 13 of `server setup` writes it after you confirm). Decoys and file-scan quarantine can write only inside `/var/www`, `/srv`, `/root`, `/opt` and `/etc/roamswitch`; for any other place, run `sudo systemctl edit roamswitch-server` and add `ReadWritePaths=-/path`. After editing `server.conf` by hand, run `sudo roamswitch server restart`. A host that needs incoming UDP must write the ports in `allowed_udp_ports`. Check the document roots watched by web shell detection with `roamswitch web-rules status`, and with 0 write them in `scan_dirs` of `web_rules.conf`.
- (1.17.0) **What was not checked**: nothing was checked on a real systemd, nft or Server host (only tests in Docker and a postinst check with a fake `systemctl`). The same holds for the time of the first SUID scan on a large file system (30 minutes is an unmeasured default), the DNS setting and restoring through `resolvectl`, UDP DNAT to containers on a real Docker, and the added text (translations are based on machine translation and have not been checked by native speakers, other than Japanese). The check that logs arrive for the egress block, metadata watch and agent-egress rules was not added. The after-install message of deb and rpm is in English only, because the shell has no language mechanism.
- (1.17.0) The bundled apps are now AccessScope, PersonalSOC and PathScope, all 1.0.0.
- (1.17.1) **Fix: on a host that runs Docker, the diagnosis warned that IP forwarding was enabled** (`server_ip_forwarding_disabled`). Docker turns `ip_forward` on to route container traffic, but the diagnosis reported it as router behaviour. When the Docker socket (`/var/run/docker.sock`) and a `docker0` or `br-*` network exist, forwarding now passes and the reason "needed by Docker for container traffic" is shown (10 languages). A host without Docker is warned as before.
- **PHP check and session files** (1.17.2): added `roamswitch php-check [--json]` (Client and Server Edition). For the two critical PHP vulnerabilities php.net fixed on 2026-10-21 (CVE-2026-109618 and CVE-2026-109619; fixed in 8.2.35, 8.3.36, 8.4.27 and 8.5.12), it reads, without changing anything, the PHP version (for OS packages, the CVEs in the package changelog), how PHP runs (mod_php, PHP-FPM, FrankenPHP), the settings `session.upload_progress.enabled` and `fatal_error_backtraces`, and the session files. The web rules gained the file type `session` and two rules (`php-session-upload-progress-code`, `php-session-code`), so the bundled rules number 20. The Server Edition watches the session save paths (`session.save_path` and others), and `roamswitch web-scan` without a folder also checks the same paths.
- **Files Python runs at every start** (1.17.2, Client): the autostart watch looks at `.pth` files in `site-packages`, `sitecustomize.py`, `usercustomize.py` and the module a `.pth` file imports (ATT&CK T1546.018). It notifies only about code that reads several kinds of credential stores, decodes data and runs it, downloads content and runs it, or has the shape of a reverse shell; ordinary `.pth` files are only logged.
- **Fixes** (1.17.2): the in-kernel SMB server `ksmbd.service` joined the sharing services stopped on an untrusted network. Shells started by a Node.js service are covered by EXR-001 (`node` and `nodejs` joined the list of service processes). `exec_rule_service_users` added to the default service accounts instead of replacing them, as the settings text says. The setting that refuses unprivileged user namespaces no longer stops systemd units that use `PrivateUsers=` (`colord`, `power-profiles-daemon`). A Server Edition alert now says which host it comes from.
- **A notification for connections to OAST servers, a daily summary of site-structure probing, NXDOMAIN burst detection in the DNS guard, MTA-STS in the domain check, cause links in the timeline** (1.17.3): link protection notifies connections to interact.sh, oast.*, burpcollaborator.net, oastify.com, dnslog.cn and ceye.io (client edition, notification only). `recon_notify` of the Web access guard turns the notifications about probing of the site structure into a daily summary by default (`summary_hour`, 9 by default; Server Edition; blocking, logs and incident records are unchanged). The DNS guard notifies when 40 or more different names under the same domain all return NXDOMAIN within 60 seconds (Server Edition, `dns_tunnel_detect_enabled`, notification only). `domain_check` also checks MTA-STS. Timeline lines gained `parent_event_id`, `root_event_id` and `evidence_refs`, and `roamswitch-incident-capture snapshot --trigger-event-id` links a detection line to its evidence bundle.
- **Changed: the heading of a Server Edition alert now also carries the IP address after the host name** (Telegram, LINE, webhook). The heading is `🚨 [RoamSwitch Server Alert · host name (IP address)] title`. When one chat receives the alerts of several servers, the address tells apart machines even if their names look alike. The address is the first one `hostname -I` returns. If it cannot be read, only the host name is written (no made-up value). If the host name cannot be read but the address can, only the address is written. If neither can be read, the heading is the old one. (1.17.4)
- **USB guard** (1.17.6): in Lockdown, unapproved USB drives and keyboards are held and an approval is asked for, whatever the setting (`usb_storage_guard_enabled`, off by default). Before, unless it was on, a new drive stayed unauthorized with no request and no notification, and even a drive on the allow list could not be used. When you leave Lockdown, held storage devices are authorized. `get_usb_guard_status` reports the guards that are really working (`storageGuardEffective`, `keyboardGuardEffective`) and a note when they are stronger than the setting. The allow list is now one list, `allowed_usb_devices` (the IPC `add_allowed_usb` wrote to a separate list that nothing read, so allowing a device changed nothing). A device is identified by `vendor:product` plus the fingerprint taken at approval (serial number included), so another unit of the same model stays held; a device without a serial number is accepted only with `accept_no_serial`. USB storage is recognized by its kind (interface class 08), not by its name (it was decided only by whether the product name contained `flash`, `disk`, `storage`, `mass` or `cruzer`, so DataTraveler, Ultra and others were not held). The notification for a blocked USB keyboard, which showed only the approval request, is fixed too.
- **ClamAV scan of USB drives, and the Lockdown network** (1.17.6): the diagnosis said "malware on USB drives is scanned automatically", but the implementation was not connected anywhere. It now finds the mount, scans read-only (lowest CPU priority, idle I/O, one at a time, 30-minute limit) and notifies as critical when something is found (it deletes and ejects nothing). If files could not be scanned because of ClamAV's limit (25 MB on Ubuntu), the notification gives their number (ClamAV skips them silently and answers "clean"). It follows `usb_mount_scan_enabled` (on by default) and `clamav_enabled`. The Lockdown firewall dropped IPv6 neighbor discovery and router advertisements, so the host got no SLAAC address and no default route. It now lets through neighbor solicitation and advertisement (hop limit 255) and router advertisements from a link-local source only (other ICMPv6 stays dropped; a fake router advertisement is watched by the network settings monitor). UDP ports in `allowed_udp_ports` now also pass for ports published by Docker (`-p 51820:51820/udp`); before, only the host's input chain let them through and forwarding to containers dropped them.
- **ATT&CK technique IDs, MCP, internal notation in text** (1.17.6): the IDs in detection coverage and the Navigator layer are IDs of v19 or later (`T1562.001` and `T1562.006` are `T1685`, `T1562.004` is `T1686`, `T1562.012` is `T1685.004`, `T1070.002` is `T1685.006`; the log-clearing detection writes the same ID to the timeline; the Navigator layer is v20). If you filter or count by the old IDs, read them as the new ones. MCP `search_exec_events` has a new `chainStatus` (a fixed word, `ok` / `not_chained` / `broken`). The old `chain` (a Rust internal notation) stayed for compatibility, deprecated, and was to be removed in the next version. Internal notation in notifications and command output (`Some(1)`, `exit status: 1`, `Some(1234)`, Rust names such as `LinkGuard`) became sentences about exit code, signal or unknown, and product names; a check now prevents them from coming back.
- **Ransomware entropy watch** (1.17.6): added to the Server Edition (`entropy_watch_enabled`, on by default). It watches places where decoys cannot be put (folders that applications keep data in, document roots): it reads the first 256 KiB of files written in `entropy_watch_dirs` (when empty: web document roots, `/var/www`, `/srv`, and the standard data folders of MariaDB, MySQL, PostgreSQL, MongoDB and Redis) and notifies when one writer writes 20 high-entropy files in 5 seconds (the 4 KiB and 256-byte window checks are included). It also catches "encrypt and delete the original" and the pattern of renaming a temporary file over the original name. The default is notification only; only with `entropy_watch_freeze=true` is the writer frozen with SIGSTOP (`protected_processes` and the system's protected programs are never stopped). It uses no blocking fanotify and reads at most `entropy_watch_max_files_per_sec` files per second (50 by default). Measured (Ubuntu 24.04 VM): a full encryption of 150 files and a replacement of every 2 KiB were detected at the 20th file with one notification; `cp -r`, `tar`, log append and gzip, a SQLite with random BLOBs and `dpkg-deb` gave 0 notifications; at 434 file writes per second it used about 2% of one core. The entropy detection of the client and the Server also covers renaming a temporary file over an existing name (there is no write to the original file, so it was invisible from the write side; it follows `ransomware_allowlist_strict`, jobs that rewrite random files are not counted, and about 30 ordinary rename-over jobs gave 0 detections).
- **Web rules and web shell accounts** (1.17.6): Web rules read more of each file. Only the first few MiB were read, so a web shell appended or placed in the middle of an image or a large file was invisible. Files up to 64 MiB are read in full, larger ones at the head and the tail (the thread runs at lower priority, the amount read is capped, and the write monitor counts what is over the cap in `health.oversize`). The rule scan became 6 to 17 times faster. Locking a web shell's account (`account_action=lock` in `web_rules.conf`) now also stops its outgoing traffic (a running web shell, and a reverse shell it opened, were not stopped by revoking the login shell; for an account that listens on a public address only new outgoing connections are stopped). `account_stop_processes=on` (off by default) stops the account's processes with SIGSTOP, and `unlock-account` restores everything.
- **Auto-start, log clearing, SUID / SGID, network settings, metadata** (1.17.6): `.git/hooks` of working copies are watched as auto-start places (`core.hooksPath` in the global and system git config, submodules and linked worktrees included; searched up to 6 levels under the home, 12 with `git_hook_scan_depth`, hidden folders such as `~/.config` too; a notification comes only when a suspicious line is newly added; a one-liner using zsh `ztcp` is reported as a reverse shell). Log-clearing detection finds rewrites of `wtmp` and `btmp` (even with the same size), edits of `lastlog`, and changes made while the daemon was stopped (up to 256 MiB is remembered as a hash per 1 MiB in `/var/lib/roamswitch/log_tamper_baseline.json`, root-owned, 0600; a lost journal cursor is recorded for a real `rotate` / `vacuum`). SUID / SGID changes are seen at the moment they are made, between scans, with the process that made them; the notification for a change of AI agent settings names the writing process; the exec record and the decoy notification read `/proc` when the event arrives, so processes that end in a millisecond, such as `cat` and `curl`, no longer vanish before they are read. The network settings monitor also sees accepted IPv6 router advertisements, DHCPv6, RDNSS and DHCP option 252 (WPAD), and reports a change of the DNS servers handed out by DHCP while DNS threat protection is on. Connections to the cloud metadata address (`169.254.169.254` and others) are reported with process, PID, parent and command, root included (Falco rule `95-roamswitch-metadata.yaml`). The critical-path file integrity monitor sees a file moved onto a watched name from another folder at once (Server Edition; before, a file built in `/tmp` and moved with `mv` onto `/usr/sbin/sshd` stayed unseen until the periodic scan, about 280 seconds; with `IN_MOVED_TO` it is reported 8 ms after, a same-folder replacement 126 ms after; a move does not tell which process wrote the file, so the "who" says it could not be identified).
- **Diagnosis and data** (1.17.6): package CVE matching shows the CISA KEV mark and "no fixed version" (the Server Edition fetches the signed maps of language dependencies only when both `package_cve_map_languages_enabled`, false by default, and `cve_kernel_map_updates_enabled` are true; about 5 MB, once a week, anonymous receive only; the default outgoing traffic does not grow). The Client Edition has a light periodic check that keeps a history of results (`finding_history_enabled`, on by default: once an hour, no network, lowest priority, kept in `/var/lib/roamswitch/finding_history.json`, so MCP `verify_security_findings` can tell "fixed" from "did not apply from the start"). `audit-url` now checks the on-device threat feed (read only, no network, IP addresses not matched; the Server deb / rpm carry the initial feed). Freezing is stronger: descendants of a frozen process and the parent and sibling workers of the found worker are stopped too, the v1 freezer holds them on cgroup v1 and hybrid hosts, and a repeat detection of a process a person has just released does not redo the Air-Gap, notification and evidence saving.
- **Fixes to the check of protection and to notifications** (1.17.6): the check of whether protection is working reads the settings chosen in the app (with the Fleet overlay applied); the root daemon read `/root` or the defaults, so it did not report protection you had turned off in the app and missed protection that was on but whose dependency was off (`forward status` / `forward test` also reflect the organization's destination, and the language of helper programs started by the daemon follows the app). The "protection is on but not working" notification was repeated at every daemon restart (now held for 24 hours; "nothing to watch" on a host without a web server is not reported as a fault). The client protection-level table (`inet roamswitch`) is rebuilt in about 20 seconds when removed by `nft flush ruleset` or a firewall reload (until then input stayed open on an untrusted network). Port anomaly detection excluded interpreters in `/usr/lib` and `/usr/libexec` (such as OpenJDK's `java`) as system listeners; it now looks at the name, not the place. TLS syslog forwarding checks the certificate and host name before it writes the event (on a slow handshake a failed verification was counted as sent and dropped). The Webhook `content` is cut before Discord's limit (2,000 characters); beyond it Discord answered 400 and the notification was dropped without a retry.
- **Distribution** (1.17.6): the Server Edition carries the `roamswitch(1)` man page, and an Arch Linux tarball and PKGBUILD are placed at `lafine.net/linux/dl/server/` (the files the page pointed to returned 404). The Linux packages carry `pathscope-diff` and `pathscope-fleet`, and the deb and rpm declare, as `Recommends`, the commands the features use (`dig`, `sudo`, `bubblewrap`, `rfkill`, `pkexec`, `resolvectl`). The unused `udisks2` was removed from the client deb's `Depends`. `scripts/check_published.sh` checks after publication that the packages the instructions name are there.
- **php-check and CVE-2026-115560** (1.17.6): `roamswitch php-check` also checked CVE-2026-115560, which has no fix yet (Client and Server Edition; the remote code execution path that PHP announced on 2026-10-23, whose affected versions are not published, so the PHP version cannot decide it). With `session.serialize_handler` set to `php_binary` or `php_serialize`, application code that switches it back to `php` with `ini_set()` before `session_start()`, and `session.upload_progress.enabled` still on, all at once, it gave a High finding (exit code 1); only the configuration or only the code was Info. The code was read from the `.php`, `.phtml` and `.inc` files in `/var/www`, `/srv`, `/usr/share/nginx/html` and `/usr/local/apache2/htdocs` (limit 40,000 files and 2 MiB per file). `php-check` was removed in 1.17.9 and the judgment is part of the periodic diagnosis.
- **Checks and bundled apps** (1.17.6): the workspace tests without the eBPF XDP crate (3835 tests) passed, `cargo audit` showed 0 vulnerabilities and `scripts/audit_no_network.sh` passed. What was checked in a disposable Ubuntu 24.04 virtual machine: USB holding and allowing, the ClamAV scan of a USB drive, IPv6 in Lockdown, the entropy watch, overwriting by `rename`, and the log-clearing detection. The bundled apps were AccessScope 1.0.2, PersonalSOC 1.0.1, PathScope 1.0.1.
- **Added: a "Protection status" card on the dashboard** (desktop app, Client Edition). It shows whether each protection you switched on really works. For one that does not, it lists the name, the reason code (`tool_missing`, `worker_not_running`, `depends_on` and others), what is happening and what to do, in 10 languages. While the daemon is stopped, every protection that is on shows "Daemon stopped". "All protection states" shows the state of all 24 guards. It uses the same reading and the same wording as `roamswitch guards`, and refreshes about every 15 seconds and when you open the tab. The card prefers the values the daemon actually uses over the settings file, so it stays right when another account turns `system_wide_fanotify_enabled` off and in the few seconds after you change a setting. Also, `roamswitch guards` now has names for the 10 guards that had none (10 languages), lines up its list for full-width characters, and shows the protection level name in the configured language when run with `sudo`.
- **Changed: the notification for a protection that is not working is now short on the desktop (a count and the names) and the full text, with the reasons and the fixes, stays in the notification history.** Notification servers cut a long body. (Server Edition notifications are the full text, as before.)
- **Fixed: the card that releases frozen processes could not be opened from anywhere.** After the sidebar redesign, the tab with the release button was no longer called, so a freeze could not be released from the window. The card is now at the top of the notification history screen.
- **Fixed: the hint after you turned "system-wide monitoring" back on said "restart", which is not needed.** The daemon rereads `system_wide_fanotify_enabled` and `pre_exec_blocking_enabled` every few seconds, so no restart is needed (in a VM, 7 seconds after the box was checked again, an EICAR file was detected without a restart). The text now says "it takes effect in a few seconds, no restart needed" (10 languages).
- **Fixed: the reason for a stopped helper process and the reason for a failed start contained system English** (`killed by signal 15`, `No such file or directory (os error 2)` and so on). They are now sentences in the display language. The one line the helper process wrote itself is kept apart from the state and shown as "Detail (in English)".
- **Fixed: in the daemon log written before the app started, `gui_required` was reported as "not working".** A guard that runs inside the desktop app cannot run until the app starts, but that is not a fault. What follows from how it is installed or used (`gui_required`, `on_demand_only`, `default_off_small_host`) is logged at INFO.
- **Fixed: the state of the kernel CVE item could run to dozens of lines of CVE numbers.** A very old kernel matches hundreds of CVEs (about 480 in a VM), so the state text now shows up to 12, the most severe first, and "… (+N)". The full list stays in the MCP result, as before.
- **Fixed: the diagnosis score was recalculated only when you opened the tab, so it could disagree with the card.** While the diagnosis tab is visible, it is recalculated about every 15 seconds (in a VM, changing a setting moved the score from 87 to 84 in about 25 seconds).
- **Fixed: every daemon start made systemd reload all units about 10 times** (disable / mask / unmask / enable on the ClamAV units). Units already in the wanted state are left alone (in a VM, reloads on restart went from 9 to 0).
- **Fixed: the terminal `scan-scripts` told you to press a button.** When no accepted state existed, its output told you to press "Accept the current state". It now tells you to run `roamswitch scan-scripts --accept` (10 languages; the window's text is unchanged).
- **Fixed: typosquat detection missed look-alikes with a js tail on the name, and look-alikes of names that were not in the popular-name list.** Measured on the names of the 10 fake npm packages Socket reported (`typescriptjs`, `nodemonjs`, `zustand.js` and others), only 1 was flagged. When a name ends in `js` or `.js`, a second comparison now takes the tail off the name and compares it with each popular name with its tail taken off (only the name being checked needs the tail, so a real `chart` is not treated as a look-alike of `chart.js`; names ending in `-js` or `_js`, such as `postcss-js`, are left out). The popular-name list gained 7 names: discord.js, ethers, web3, zustand, vitest, hardhat and jotai. 9 of the 10 are now flagged (`deezcord.js` has distance 3 and is still missed). A scoped name (`@solana/web3.js` and the like) lives in its owner's namespace, so it is left out of this tail comparison (the real `@solana/web3.js` is not taken for a copy of `web3`). On 852 real names from a local `node_modules`, false positives do not increase. **When the signed list that is distributed is newer than the embedded one, the signed list wins, so the 7 names reach the distributed list only after the list is rebuilt by the daily job.**
- **Fixed: in the result of an active check, findings from nmap carried the heading "confirmed: reachable without authentication".** An nmap finding is nmap's own result, not something RoamSwitch confirmed. With the heading, even the check of a port that listens on 127.0.0.1 only read as if every line (`http-title` and the like) were "reachable without authentication". Findings from nmap now get no heading.
- **Fixed: a long text in a result window ran past the screen, out of reach of the OK button.** The text now stops at 60% of the screen height and scrolls, so it can be read to the end (for example the result of an active check with many findings).
- **Fixed: on the install-script tab, the old text "No accepted state yet" stayed after you pressed "Accept the current state".** The tab now scans again right after you accept and refreshes the display.
- **Fixed: in the link guard's "Check a link manually", long explanations of a verdict (for example the JPCERT/CC one that says "do not open it") were cut off at the right edge of the window and had to be scrolled sideways.** They now wrap and show in full. The Japanese text for the short-URL explanation, which also appears on screen, said "this command does not connect"; it now says "this diagnosis does not connect", as the other languages already did.
- **Changed: the "(Pro)" label is removed from the screens, the help, `roamswitch --help` and the MCP text.** The Linux client (Community Edition) is free with every feature. This covers the headings and descriptions of dependency lockfile tamper monitoring, the install-script inventory, typosquat detection and npm signature / provenance verification. The "activate a Pro license" line in the last page of the first-run guide is gone too. How the features behave does not change.
- **Changed: the notice about protections that are not working now tells the desktop edition where to look on the screen (Security Diagnostic Suite › Protection status).** It used to name the command `roamswitch status`. The Server Edition has no screen, so it still names `roamswitch status --server` (10 languages).
- **Fixed: on the package CVE matching screen, a CVE summary showed Markdown symbols as they were (the heading `#`, `` `code` ``, bold, links, bullets) and ran to several lines, one per paragraph.** It now uses the same cleanup as the CLI: one line of up to 240 characters, without the symbols.
- **Added: automatic check of dependency vulnerabilities** (the client daemon, on by default). It used to run only when you ran `roamswitch scan-packages` or pressed the button, so a newly found vulnerability told nobody unless you did. It now notifies you when a lockfile in a registered project folder (package-lock.json, requirements.txt, Pipfile.lock, poetry.lock, Cargo.lock, Gemfile.lock, composer.lock, go.sum, pom.xml) holds a CVE that is in CISA's KEV or has an EPSS of 0.9 or higher, and no valid acceptance covers it. It checks once a lockfile has been quiet for 20 seconds after a write, 60 seconds after start-up, and once a day. It reads only files and maps on the device, with no network traffic and nothing executed. What it reported is remembered as a digest and not repeated, and an ecosystem without a CVE map is not reported at all. It is turned off with `dependency_watch_enabled` (config.json) or the switch on the screen, and `get_guard_status` shows `dependencyWatch`. `scan-packages` without a folder uses the registered folders (so the command the notice names works as it is). Matching for language ecosystems now uses an index by package name instead of walking every map row for every dependency (6.8 ms for 12,000 dependencies against 15,000 rows).
- **Added: a notice when an AI agent setup changes** (the desktop daemon, on by default). When an instruction file, a skill, an MCP definition or a Claude Code setting differs from the state you accepted, you are notified (inotify plus a 5-minute timer). While there is no accepted state, neither the check nor inotify runs; the guard status then says "There is no accepted state yet. Once you accept one, later changes are reported" (reason `awaiting_accept`, 10 languages, not counted in the score). The accepted state is read by root after it checks the owner. `agent_setup_watch_enabled` turns it off.
- **Added: a notice when a setting gets weaker** (client and Server Edition, on by default). In the hourly diagnosis, when a setting that passed last time now fails (SSH, sudo, sysctl, core dumps, Docker and more; 14 items on the client, 27 on the Server Edition), you get a single notification. The same item is not repeated within a day, the first diagnosis only records, and items that could not be measured are not counted. On the Server Edition, `config_regression_notice_enabled` in `server.conf` turns it off (`server.conf` now has 130 keys, and the settings editor 167 in all).
- **Fixed: the link guard looked for brand names as a substring, so `online`, `pipeline`, `airline` and `deadline` hit "line", and `pineapple` hit "apple".** It now splits the host name into runs of letters (words) and counts a brand name only when it appears as one word (`line` is also an everyday word, so it counts only with a word such as `login` or `support`). A name where only a public suffix follows the brand, such as `google.de` or `amazon.co.uk`, is taken as the brand's own registration, while `paypal.top` and `apple.com.verify-account.example.net` still count. On the Tranco top million, the names that hit fell from 16,028 to 155 (none in the top 10,000), and in the 2,000 bundled scam domains from 124 to 53 (most of the 71 lost were false hits such as `online`). The manual check and the always-on protection use the same verdict.
- **Changed: the link guard now reports a connection whose name is only suspicious (a domain that merely contains a brand name, a domain with swapped characters and the like) as a notification, without stopping it.** The verdict was already "warn", but no notification went out and nobody was told. In every mode nothing is stopped, and in "Off" nothing is shown. The same name is reported at most once every 6 hours and at most 6 times an hour in all, and it stays in the notification history (type "Name looks suspicious"). The mode name now matches what it does: "Ask first (block if no answer)" (it holds the connection for up to 8 seconds, asks you to allow or block, and blocks it if there is no answer). The link guard screen gained lists of "Allowed names" and "Names kept blocked", where you can revoke an entry or type a name to allow (changes made in the confirmation dialog or the config file show up within seconds while the screen is open).
- **Fixed: in "Ask first", when the hold expired with no answer, the hold notification and the confirmation dialog were shown again for every retransmission from the client.** The same name that is already held is not asked about again, and for 60 seconds after the hold expires it is neither asked about nor reported. A blocked result is reported once every 60 seconds (two kinds: when you chose "Keep blocked", and "Blocked because there was no answer"; 10 languages). On a VM, a 45-second stream of retransmissions gave one hold notification, one blocked result, one dialog and one approval (before the fix, a new one every 8 seconds).
- **Changed: the popular-name list now grows by itself every day from the public rankings of downloads and dependents.** Besides the hand-added names (`packages`), it adds real unscoped names from the npm-high-impact rankings, up to 4,000 (`rankedPackages`, a new key of the distributed list; older versions ignore it and behave as before). Before adding, it runs the same matching as the app on real names it has not seen and leaves out any candidate that makes more real names look like fakes. When the rankings cannot be fetched, have a different shape or fail the check, it distributes the list of hand-added names only. Names added from the rankings are matched up to distance 1 (hand-added names as before), and the check also looks at swapped adjacent letters (`eslint-plguin-promise` and the like) and at up to two typo-like edits to a dependency that carries install-time code (preinstall, install, postinstall or binding.gyp). On 896 real names it had not seen, the real names flagged as fakes were 14 with the hand-added names only and 31 with the ranked names added. **The distributed list is updated after the daily rebuild, so new names do not arrive at once.**
- **Fixed: the dark colour scheme of the screens was not applied.** One `line-height` that GTK 3 does not know made it drop the whole style sheet, and the windows showed the default colours (seen on Ubuntu 24.04 / GTK 3.24.41). It is removed, and rules that cannot be read are written to the log. The input fields, text fields, buttons and tab frames that stayed white now follow the dark scheme, and the small helper text and hint text are dark enough to read on the dark background (about 5.9:1 and 8.4:1).
- **Fixed: long sentences were cut off at the right edge of the window.** Labels that cannot wrap made the minimum width of the whole page wider than the window. Long labels now wrap, rows of buttons wrap, and tables scroll on their own. Result dialogs open at their natural width, and the long text of an approval dialog (unknown port, malware, ARP, clipboard, OS hardening) scrolls too.
- **Fixed: the time labels of the activity-log bar chart showed as boxes in a Japanese environment.** They resolved to a fallback font without digits; they are now drawn with the same font as the rest of the screen.
- **Fixed: the log audit showed an empty date, and in a Japanese environment the audit ran together into one entry.** journalctl prints month names in the language of the environment (`10月 31 06:12:15`), so the start of a line was not recognised and all lines joined the first entry (on a VM, 5 failed sudo attempts became 1). journalctl now runs with `LC_ALL=C`. Template anomalies get the time of their example line, and dates are shown in each language's form.
- **Fixed: an expired acceptance did not reach the incident timeline from the user's app.** The store is root-only and the write failed silently. The app now asks the daemon (only when the caller's record has an acceptance past its expiry, and the same record is not added twice). When an acceptance record cannot be read or is broken, the screen says why in the display language; an English parse error is split off as "Details (in English)" and a system error (`os error`) becomes a sentence in the display language.
- **Changed: in the dependency diagnosis, Yarn Berry 4.15.0 and later is no longer flagged when nothing is set.** The default of `npmMinimalAgeGate` is 0 up to 4.14.1 and 1440 (minutes) from 4.15.0 (it is flagged only when you set the wait to 0).
- **Fixed: the last line of the emergency-restore guide now names `roamswitch-server` on the Server Edition** (the service name differs by edition).
- **Fixed: Falco raised a secret-exfiltration alert for `systemctl --user unset-environment SSH_AUTH_SOCK` at every logout** (the Falco rule the Server Edition installs). It is now an exception (only when the arguments match exactly; every other `systemctl` run is still detected).
- **Fixed: notifications about child processes of the investigation agent were not silenced when the process was short-lived** (Server Edition). A process such as `grep` that ends before its alert arrives has no parent chain in `/proc`, so it could not be recognised as the agent's own work. It is now also recognised from the parent list kept in the exec record (within 10 minutes). Only Warning and below are silenced; Critical and above are still notified.
- Verification: the tests of all crates pass, 4236 in total (Docker, as CI runs them; 16 ignored).
- **Fixed: on the Server Edition, `falcoctl-artifact-follow.service`, which ships with Falco, was reported as a crash loop.** The service asks Falco's built-in web server (port 8765) which version runs, but RoamSwitch's Falco configuration turns that web server off, so the service failed and restarted again and again (about every 2 minutes). On a host with the RoamSwitch Falco configuration, the service is now stopped and disabled (the post-install step of the deb, rpm and Arch packages, and `install.sh`; an upgrade does the same). Automatic rule updates through falcoctl stop too, but they were not working before either.
- **Fixed: the tray user unit of the desktop edition (`roamswitch-app.service`) failed every 3 seconds on a host without the executable.** A host that had only the Server Edition kept the autostart unit of an earlier Client Edition (the restart counter had passed 400,000). The unit now starts only when `/usr/bin/roamswitch-app` exists and restarts at most 5 times in 60 seconds. An existing unit is not rewritten: on a host that still has it, run `systemctl --user disable --now roamswitch-app.service` and delete `~/.config/systemd/user/roamswitch-app.service`.
- **Changed: notifications with the same cause are merged into one.** When one process opened `~/.aws/config` and `credentials` at the same time, each file produced its own notification (both in the credential file watch and in the decoy watch). For 2.5 seconds after the first event, the watch now waits for the other files of the same process and lists them in one notification (the decoy timeline still records each file). The notification history merges entries with the same title and the same body within 1 second (a different body is kept).
- Verification: the tests of all crates pass, 4241 in total (Docker, as CI runs them; 16 ignored). The dependency audit (`cargo audit`) finds no vulnerabilities.

### 1.9.39 - 1.9.94 (summarized)

Everything in these releases, grouped by topic (Client and Server Edition unless marked). The full text of each entry is in the git history of this file.

- **Isolation (Air-Gap) reworked** (1.9.90, 1.9.91): an isolation caused by a high-confidence detection (a tampered canary, suspected ransomware, a critical eBPF detection) no longer releases itself on a timer. After a cap (1 hour by default) without a verified-cleared re-check it moves to a "degraded" mode that never re-opens on a timer, and it is released only by `roamswitch emergency-restore`, the GUI, the server's `roamswitch server ack`, or a verified-cleared re-check. Detections prone to false positives still release on the short timer. Connections that were already open no longer survive an isolation (the server keeps only the maintenance SSH and allowed IPs; the client's degraded mode allows loopback only). The ARP re-check sends a direct ARP request when the gateway has no neighbour entry (`arp_recheck_active_probe`), so ARP-triggered isolations stop getting stuck.
- **Process-execution record, forwarding and Server TUI** (1.9.90): a continuous record of which programs ran, with parents, hashes and containers, on the machine only (default 200 MB / 14 days, hash chain), and seven notify-only correlation rules; event forwarding (off by default: syslog, CEF, JSON Lines, HMAC-signed webhook); and a full-coverage TUI for the Server Edition (`roamswitch server tui`). Also `--json`/`--export`, and a notification when another program opens credential files (off by default).
- **Containers and network** (1.9.43, 1.9.44, 1.9.90): the Docker firewall-bypass guard works with Docker's native nftables backend and IPv6 and heals itself within a minute; Container Exec Guard (Server) marks each container's rootfs with fanotify's notify-only `FAN_OPEN_EXEC` and captures even a self-deleting attacker binary; the Egress Guard covers container traffic; per-process CPU-saturation detection; a wider FIM scope (`authorized_keys`, `ld.so.preload`, new files in watched directories).
- **Investigation agent** (Server, 1.9.45 to 1.9.56): automated first-pass triage of eBPF, FIM and lockfile detections (false-positive likelihood, reasons, next commands, Markdown report) and an optional, off-by-default handoff to an agentic CLI (Claude Code, agy, Codex CLI, OpenCode, custom), skipped right before a host isolation; fixes for it never working under root, for the sudo wrapping, and for the recursion where an agent's own investigation looked like a new incident.
- **Security fixes** (1.9.84 to 1.9.90): a local user could send a forged critical event to Falco's socket and trigger a full Air-Gap (the socket is now mode 0600 with a peer-UID check); look-alike names such as `sshd-x` escaped the protected-process list; allowlist entries could be bypassed by renaming a process (entries can be bound to the real executable with `--exe`); Telegram and LINE tokens and webhook URLs were visible in the process list; the port-scan auto-block could be abused with a forged source address (it now refuses only new inbound connections, never blocks the gateway, DNS resolvers or the host's own addresses, and checks the SYNs' source MAC); a flood of forged SYNs could push scan records out of the log (200 per second limit first, and a notification when detection is saturated); a scan claiming the Sensor's address escaped the block (the Sensor's MAC is recorded at pairing); unknown-listening-port detection could be evaded with a system daemon's name (`/tmp/sshd`) or by an interpreter script from a temporary directory; the dev-server block rules were briefly lifted while being re-applied; log-frequency detection went blind after one large burst; the root daemon created root-owned directories under `~/.local`.
- **Port scans, listeners and vulnerability checks** (1.9.39 to 1.9.94): stronger port-scan detection (memory bounded under a flood, a 24-hour window, IPv6 per /64), unknown-listening-port detection for non-wildcard binds and temporary, in-memory or deleted binaries, UDP listeners from interpreters, alerts that name the real program (`wsdd (python3)`); active checks for Elasticsearch, CouchDB, Jenkins, VNC, RDP (NLA) and SMB (SMBv1, signing); CSV export that explains safe and inconclusive results, scan IDs and locked logs; typosquat detection for npm packages (Pro); the kernel-CVE check no longer flags distro backports that only bump the ABI number; the nmap NSE scan is always on and the CVE maps update monthly.
- **RoamSwitch Sensor** (1.9.55 to 1.9.91): pairing moved from mDNS to a pairing code; an active audit can be requested from a Sensor and its result fetched later (MCP tool `get_sensor_audit_results`); TLS 1.3 with certificate pinning (`roamswitch sensor pair --fingerprint`, `sensor repin`; new pairings need Sensor 0.3.9 or later, existing plaintext pairings keep working with a warning); the setup wizard offers a preset when a Sensor runs on the same host; a failed pairing now says why (30 seconds of waiting, likely causes, a command for the Sensor host).
- **Network control review** (1.9.71 to 1.9.72, 1.9.85 to 1.9.87): firewall settings not re-applied right after a daemon restart, services stopped by shared-service control never restored, and a manual security-level change racing the daemon's patrol; ARP false positives triggering Air-Gap under VirtualBox NAT and in NAT environments; the Frag Gap mitigation (denying user namespaces) became opt-in (`auto_enable_userns_restriction`) because it broke browsers, Flatpak, Electron and rootless containers on the client.
- **Notifications, screens and languages** (1.9.40 to 1.9.94): every notification is in ten languages (FIM, lockfile, eBPF, kernel exploit, all Server alerts, the second-instance message, health-check lines), and the Server Edition's language is set with `language`; the notification history no longer freezes (bodies capped at 2,000 characters, newest 200 drawn); "suspected malware" notifications no longer repeat for a file you keep saving; screens split into tabs (package CVE scan, Ports & DevIsolator); the onboarding wizard defaults to standard protection instead of "open"; several GTK layout, CSV-header and translation fixes.
- **Packaging and updates** (1.9.75 to 1.9.88): the periodic nmap NSE database update and the CVE map updates were failing silently in systemd's sandbox or held back by a shared marker; RoamSwitch did not start at boot on some systems; the health check advised apt on Arch-based systems; an administrator password was requested every time Air-Gap was released; and the apt and rpm repositories held every past version until the publishing job failed, so they were cut to the latest version (1.10.5 keeps the latest three).

### 1.0.0 - 1.9.38 (initial release through the stable era, summarized)

- **Initial release (1.0.0)**: ported the macOS edition's zero-trust
  architecture to Linux (systemd + nftables). Followed by the core
  defense capabilities — VPN tunnel + killswitch (WireGuard/Tailscale),
  unknown-port auto-blocking, passive link protection, daily threat feed,
  BadUSB/USB storage authorization, autonomous ARP-spoofing detection
  (1.0.1 - 1.0.66).
- **Official launch of RoamSwitch Server Edition (1.1.0)**: Falco eBPF
  runtime threat detection & auto-isolation, Critical Path FIM, a 25-item
  server security health check, Webhook/PagerDuty alerts. Followed by
  event-driven FIM, Egress/C2 containment, Tetragon sensor support, and
  fine-grained `guard.yaml` policy configuration (1.1.1 - 1.3.2).
- **Package CVE Scan (1.5.0)**, **Log Audit template anomaly detection +
  automatic secret masking (1.6.0)**, **incident history for Air-Gap
  trigger guards exposed via MCP/CLI (1.7.0)**, **File Scan Guard &
  Docker risk detection (1.4.1 - 1.8.0)**.
- **Evil-Twin SSID warning, BadUSB keystroke-timing anomaly detection,
  Ransomware Canary blast-radius evidence (1.9.0 - 1.9.5)**; **notification
  history, ClickFix detection, and the log audit's background schedule
  added to the client edition (1.9.6 - 1.9.15)**.
- **Critical Path FIM extended to the client edition, Resource Exhaustion
  / Process Anomaly Guard (Server Edition), a root-cause fix for Log
  Audit's masking regex, and fixes for several Server Edition
  success-without-verification bugs (1.9.16 - 1.9.26)**.
- **Four npm-install supply-chain-risk features (Lockfile FIM,
  install-script inventory, npm signature verification, bubblewrap
  sandboxing), typosquat detection, crypto-wallet seed-phrase leak
  detection, and a critical fix for the Default-Deny firewall blocking
  100% of Docker containers' outbound traffic (1.9.27 - 1.9.38)**.

---

## RoamSwitch for Mac

## 2.0.0

The Mac and Linux editions are both 2.0.0. The app now has an Endpoint Security system extension: who wrote a file or read a decoy is identified by signature, not guessed. The diagnostic score is counted against the strictest settings preset.

- **Added: support for an Endpoint Security (ES) extension (a system extension).** Approve it as the app guides you: allow the extension in System Settings > "Login Items & Extensions", and allow "ESClient" under Full Disk Access. If the extension cannot be used, the app keeps working with the previous method (`eslogger`; it switches automatically).
- **Changed: process execution records, records of which process touched a file, and the detection of signals sent to frozen processes are now received from the extension.** Fewer events are missed and the load is lower. On a test VM, at the same load, the extension used about 0.4 to 0.7 times the CPU of `eslogger`; under a full-speed `open` load, `eslogger` missed about 75% of the events and the extension missed none (one measurement each, in a VM).
- **Changed: "who wrote it" and "who read it" are identified by signature instead of being guessed.** This applies to the ransomware decoys and entropy detection (counted per writer), the containment process table, credential decoys (honeytokens), browser credentials (allowed by signature), critical-file tampering (FIM), dependency lockfiles, AI agent settings, startup files, download checks (started the moment the file is written) and the evidence of an alert (a process that has already exited can be looked up). Without the extension, the previous method is used.
- **Added (Pro, on by default, notification only): a notification when a program that is not allowed reads a real credential file** (`~/.aws`, `~/.ssh`, `~/.netrc`, `~/.git-credentials` and others). It matches the same detection on Linux.
- **Added (off by default, notification only; on in the "Maximum security" preset): detection of clearing history and logs; of account changes (sudo, su, failed authentication, Screen Sharing, a configuration profile added, TCC changes, a user created or a password changed); of process injection; and of stripped quarantine attributes.**
- **Added: a notification, at the moment of registration and with who did it, when a LaunchAgent, LaunchDaemon or login item is registered.** Name spoofing (a signature that claims to be Apple but does not match, or a file named like a system process) was added to the execution-record rules.
- **Changed: SSH login and failure records and the XProtect detection that starts an Air-Gap are received from the extension** (the previous method is used automatically if it is not available). Detection of reverse shells, Docker and web-worker crashes scans right after a start (the timed scan stays).
- **Fixed: the detection of web-worker crashes missed the Mac signal numbers (SIGBUS=10, SIGSYS=12).**
- **Fixed: before a containment stop, the process's start time is checked, so a different process that reuses the PID is not stopped.**
- **Changed: the diagnostic score is counted against the "Maximum security" preset as the ideal.** A protection that the settings presets switch counts as passed when it is on and as failed when it is off; a protection an existing item already checks is not counted twice; protections that need Pro or whose prerequisites are not met are not applicable. A new item, "Gap to the preset" (`preset_gap`, the 26th), is not part of the score; it lists the difference between the selected preset and "Maximum security". A lower score with a lighter preset is the correct behavior.
- **Added: the evidence chain has a start mark and a copy of its head,** so cutting the end off a chain can be found, and the manifest records the time each piece of evidence took. The bundle has `process/network.json` with the address, port and state of both ends of the process's TCP and UDP sockets (unspecified addresses are `*`, UDP shows no state, the content of the traffic is not kept).
- **Added (Pro, off by default): a pre-roll of the traffic before a detection** (`incidentPrerollEnabled`, `incidentPrerollSeconds`, default 30, 10 to 120; in the settings window). The root helper keeps a ring of `tcpdump -s 96` captures; a bundle gets each packet cut at the end of its transport header (headers only, no content) in `packet_preroll/`, in the same form as on Linux. The ring itself can hold a few dozen bytes of content after a short header. The socket record of a process of another user is `skipped` when it cannot be read.
- Verification: 4,153 XCTest tests pass (0 failures). The signed and notarized build was verified with the checks for the bundle (signature, hardened runtime, the Endpoint Security entitlement and the embedded profile). The extension's CPU, the missed-event rate and the response times were measured on a virtual machine; the approval flow with a notarized build on a Mac with SIP enabled is being verified separately.

## 1.17.36

Fixes the honeytoken (credential decoy) notices that kept coming after the app started and put the app under heavy load.

- **Fixed: for some minutes after a launch, notices about "access" to the decoy files kept coming and the app could run at about 100% CPU.** The change notice that the app's own reset of a decoy's access time raises could not always be told from a real one (the check used timing only). A decoy that is still in the state it was in right after the reset is now treated as untouched, and a decoy that is already reset is not written again. A change of attributes only is handled once a minute per decoy. A read is still detected by the periodic check of the access time.
- **Fixed: the `lsof` that looks for the process that read a decoy no longer runs on the main thread.** It could freeze the app's windows while detections went on.
- **Fixed: `get_access_log_report` includes detections that could not be evaluated (`coverage.limited` / `not_evaluable`) in the caution that "nothing found" does not mean "safe".**
- **Added: the knowledge base has entries for `agent-check` and for the check of browser extension permissions (10 languages).**
- Verification: 3,690 XCTest tests pass. The start-up trigger was observed on a real Mac and examined with a small program that copies the same check, but it is not pinned down; the weak point of the check is changed to a check by state.

## 1.17.35

Adds defenses inspired by ARTEX and gogo; the network protection level keeps the same name as on Linux, "Maximum lockdown".

- **Added: when the URL safety check could not follow a redirect, it says so and does not call the URL safe.** Following classifies every address and pins the connection target (a redirect cannot lead to an internal address).
- **Added: `RoamSwitchMCPServer agent-check`.** Checks the destination from an AI agent's pre-tool hook.
- **Added: a check of the permissions of Chromium-family browser extensions** (notification only).
- **Added (off by default, notification only): detection of scans from this Mac to internal networks** (Pro; the menu "Internal network scan detection" and its exclusions; on in the "Maximum security" preset).
- **Added (off by default, notification only): an AI agent repeating the same operation** (`exec.agent_repeat`).
- **Added: SNMP exposure in the diagnostics (25 items).** Ports of debug and proxy services (JDWP, Node inspector, PHP-FPM/Xdebug, SOCKS) reachable from the network are shown with the process name. A connection to a closed sentinel port counts as four closed ports.
- **Fixed: the detection coverage table notes that T1046 only sees TCP SYN, and has a row for T1595.**
- Verification: 3,684 XCTest cases pass. Detection of scans from this Mac to internal networks has not been checked on a real machine.

## 1.17.34

Tells "settings preset" and "network protection level" apart by showing each with its category.

- **Changed: "settings preset" and "network protection level" are always shown with their category.** For example "Settings preset: Standard security" and "Network protection level: Standard protection", in the menu (the preset submenu, the status line and the choice submenu of the protection level, registered networks), the confirmation before and the notification after applying, the profile-switch notification, the notification of lowering the level by the state of the Mac, the description of `get_guard_status` of MCP, the knowledge base and the README. The wording of the protection status window is aligned too.
- Verified: the 3,543 XCTest tests pass (0 failed, 0 skipped).

## 1.17.33

Mac gets protection presets. Every reason in the protection status window now says which feature it is about, where it is, what is happening and what to do. The bundled AccessScope is replaced with 1.0.8.

- **Added: protection presets (Pro only).** Choose Light, Balanced, Standard security or Maximum security at the top of the menu bar menu (a check on the current one, "Custom" after a manual change, "Show contents…"). Light stops the always-running work that costs resources and lengthens scan intervals; Standard security is the standard setting without regard to resources. Maximum security includes the items that cause many false positives or stop operations and shows a strong warning for each one before it is applied. On Free the item reads "Protection presets (Pro)…" and opens the licence screen. Applying a preset can be undone. `get_guard_status` of MCP has a `preset` field (read only).
- **Added: 8 setting keys so that Light is really light** (on/off and intervals of always-running work). The defaults are the same as before.
- **Changed: a Mac that newly activates Pro starts on Balanced.** Right after activation, a confirmation asks once: "Apply Standard security" or "Stay on Balanced". A Mac that already uses Pro is not rewritten by the update.
- **Changed: the protection status window, the tooltips in the menu and the notifications now give every reason the feature name and role, its place in the menu, what is happening, what is not being seen, numbered steps and buttons.** All 30 reason codes. For example, "Development web folder scan" has no switch, runs by itself once a day, and its manual scan and settings are inside "Web shell detection rules…". Buttons include "Open this feature", "Open Full Disk Access", "Restart this guard" and "Scan web folders now".
- **Changed: the names and reasons of files that could not be read are recorded.** For the web folder scan, lockfile watch, persistence watch, entropy detection, canary and the download static scan, up to 5 items the last scan could not examine are shown in the window with one of: no permission, protected by macOS, type unreadable, too large. A file that disappeared during the scan is not counted. They also appear as `skippedSamples` in the readers of `get_guard_status`.
- **Fixed: after Pro expired and was activated again, a guard sometimes did not resume.**
- **Changed: the confirmation text of the process-exec recorder states the helper's memory use.**
- Verified: the 3,537 XCTest tests pass (0 failed, 0 skipped).

## 1.17.32

Fixes for defects found by re-running the lessons that also apply to the Mac. The bundled PersonalSOC is replaced with its newer version (see the PersonalSOC section; PathScope and AccessScope are unchanged).

- **Fixed: when the automatic-response limit prevented isolation, the emergency dialog said "full block failed".** It now says the action was skipped because of the limit. Re-applying while already isolated does not count against the automatic-response limit, and decoys in a folder that is gone are no longer watched.
- **Fixed: adding an AI agent setting and reverting it right away produced no notification.** A change that differed from the baseline since the last check and was then reverted is now notified as "an AI agent setting changed once and was restored", together with the process that wrote it.
- **Fixed: the target list on the MCP configuration check screen did not include Codex and OpenCode.**
- Verified: The 3,453 XCTest tests pass (0 failures).

## 1.17.31

Fixes for defects found by re-checking the lessons. Limits on automatic responses, an action journal, maintenance windows, rotation of the update signing keys and more are added too. The bundled PersonalSOC and PathScope are replaced with their newer versions (the changes are listed under each app; AccessScope is unchanged). If your scripts rely on what is forwarded or on MCP results, check the "Changed (affects scripts)" items.

- **Fixed: right after isolation was lifted, resolving a name that had been looked up during the isolation waited from 1 to about 9 seconds.** In a disposable macOS VM, 2 out of 10 tries went over 5 seconds. mDNSResponder kept waiting on the retries of the queries made during the isolation. When the isolation (pf's block drop all) is removed, the DNS cache is now emptied and mDNSResponder restarted (0.04 to 1.1 seconds in the VM). This covers "Unisolate" in the menu, the timeout, lifting after the cause is confirmed resolved, and lifting the startup block. Network emergency recovery never had the wait.
- **Changed: with the USB guard off, the notifications for a USB stick whose identity differs from an allowed device say so.** This is a device with the same model, or the same volume UUID, as an allowed USB stick but a different serial number or the like. 'It does not have the identity of the allowed device "…".' is added to its notifications for the read-only mount, the scan result and the read-write reconnect. The stick is not held and stays usable. If you have two sticks of the same model and allowed only one, the other gets this line too. With the guard on, it is held and approval is asked for, as before.
- **Fixed: in MCP `get_incident_timeline`, the action taken for blocking a port scan source showed the internal name (`port_scan_block`).** It now shows "Blocked the scanning source" (10 languages).
- **Fixed: updates of the active-check CVE map, the package CVE map and the web rules accepted versions older than the installed one.** Sending an old signed manifest again could bring back holes that had been fixed. An older version is now refused as a rollback. A manifest created more than 60 days ago, or dated more than 1 day ahead, is refused too. The link guard's threat feed gets the same date check.
- **Fixed: event forwarding by Webhook and syslog could send to loopback, the LAN and cloud metadata (169.254.169.254).** A check that refuses sending to internal addresses is added. Host names are resolved before sending, and every returned address is checked. Add a SIEM inside the LAN to the destinations allowed even at internal addresses. It is on by default for new settings. Settings that had saved a syslog or Webhook destination before this version keep it off.
- **Added: when a decoy was read by an AI agent or one of its children, the alert says so.** Instead of "possible malware", the last line of the alert asks you to check the instructions given to the agent and the repositories and pages it read.
- **Changed: the decoy credential files (`.aws/credentials`, `.pypirc`, `.env.backup`) now have realistic formats** (the same as on Linux). The helper does not read files that are hard links.
- **Added: a protected-detection class.** Decoy reads, sudoers and sudoers.d, PAM and root's `authorized_keys` are exempt from the 10-minute interval for the same notification, from maintenance windows and from automatic baseline intake. The critical file monitor no longer learns additions and removals of protected paths automatically. PAM is no longer taken in automatically after a macOS update; the notification says it was found after a macOS update. The 5-second thinning of decoy reads applies only to the same decoy and the same reader, and reads by another process are reported.
- **Added: maintenance windows.** Start one with administrator approval from "Recovery and uninstall" in the menu (up to 4 hours; a reason is required). During one, notifications that are not protected detections are not shown but kept in the history, and one summary is shown when it ends.
- **Added: limits on automatic responses.** They set how far automatic bans, freezes and Air-Gap isolation go (`max_auto_tier`) and how many per hour (`ban_per_hour` 60, `freeze_per_hour` 20, `airgap_per_hour` 6). Beyond them, the response is not taken, and "An automatic response became a recommendation" is notified once an hour per action. Responses taken by a person are not affected. Change them with administrator approval from "Recovery and uninstall" in the menu.
- **Added: an action journal.** The helper records SSH source bans, port scan source bans, Air-Gap isolation, process freezes and web server account locks in `/Library/Application Support/RoamSwitch/action_journal.jsonl` (root, 0600), with their start and done, failed or refused. File quarantine is recorded by the app. The real state is read back after each action, and only an action that took effect is marked done. Network emergency recovery first undoes the recorded network actions, newest first.
- **Changed: the check that keeps the same notification from firing again compares the wording with the values left out.** It compared the exact title and body, so a different port, address or count made it fire as a new notification. Notifications of the same form are shown once per 10 minutes, and each is still kept in the notification history.
- **Added: hashes of the listeners, Homebrew packages and main settings are recorded once a day** (up to 90 days). The new MCP tool `get_system_timeline` shows the daily differences (listeners added and removed, packages added, removed and changed in version, changes in the state of settings) and the difference from the current state. The settings are `/etc/hosts`, `sshd_config`, `pam.d`, sudoers, `~/.ssh/authorized_keys`, shell startup files, the names of LaunchDaemons and LaunchAgents and others; their content is not stored. Nothing is sent over the network.
- **Added: incident records are chained by the hash of the previous record.** The chain is checked when the records are read, with a warning if it is broken (MCP, the activity log). A record is marked when the detection's input was interrupted around it. Incident grouping now also links the same source address (within 60 minutes) and the same SSH session.
- **Added: MCP `get_incident_timeline` shows whether the timeline's evidence references still match the current record chain.** For example, the bundle is gone, the manifest differs from the recorded one, or the chain is broken.
- **Added: revocation and rotation of the update signing keys.** The key list, revoked keys and the lowest accepted version in the signed manifest are checked, and only keys that are signed by an already trusted key, not revoked and not expired are used. A manifest signed with the old key that lists a new key hands trust over to the new key. Revocations and the lowest version are stored and never go back. If the state file is damaged, updates stop. A manifest without the new fields is accepted with the built-in key, as before.
- **Added: 3 findings in the MCP configuration audit.** A remote server without authentication (`remote_no_auth`, medium), a server listening on all interfaces (`mcp_wide_bind`, high), and a server that reads outside text in the same file as a server that can send data out (`egress_exposure`, medium). It only reads the configuration files and sends nothing.
- **Added: a class for each package CVE result** (listed in KEV, EPSS 0.9 or higher, or other). The order and the verdicts do not change.
- **Changed: the wait before resending forwarded events now has some jitter.** When a destination that can resend (TCP or TLS syslog, Webhook) fails 5 times in a row, sending to it stops for 30 seconds (doubling on further failures, up to 10 minutes), and new events go into the resend queue. While it is stopped, the settings window says so.
- **Fixed: following a web server log read a file from the middle after copytruncate when more was written than before.** It is now recognized by a hash of the first 256 bytes. When the link guard's record was truncated, the notifications and the "Allow / Block" windows for old events came back; this is fixed too. Following the ClickFix history no longer shows old commands again when the shell rewrites its history.
- **Changed: when saved records are read, JSON with the same key written twice is treated as damaged.** One of the values used to be taken silently. Before it is rebuilt, the file is kept as `<name>.corrupt-<time>`, as before.
- **Changed: state files are all written the same way.** Nothing is written to a symbolic link or a file that is not a regular file. Files written 0600 (the SSH records, web account locks, the original DNS settings, the WireGuard configuration and others) are no longer visible as 0644 for a moment. The incident records, the MCP tool call record and the watchdog state get a lock.

- **Fixed: the exec log now keeps the last 72 hours.** It can grow to four times the size limit, and nothing is deleted while the free disk space is above 5% and 1 GB. The menu heading is "Maximum size".
- **Fixed: `curl | sh` from `http://127.0.0.1` was counted as a raw-IP URL.** The 127.0.0.0/8 range is now left out. If another aggravating factor such as base64 or eval is present, it is reported as before.
- **Changed: when the same execution repeats within 10 minutes, the exec log keeps the first entry and one "repeat" line.** The helper search, the process tree, the list, the export, the MCP `search_exec_events` and `get_process_tree`, Sigma and the exec context read it back as separate runs. Logs in the old format are still read.
- **Added: the standby key (K2) for the update manifest is built into the app's keys.** Signing still uses the same key as before.

**Changed (affects scripts)**

- Forwarding: the `summary` of timeline lines and notification lines sent to the forwarding destinations (syslog, file, Webhook) is in English whatever the display language (the same as on Linux). The keys and fields, and the "title: first line of the body" form of notifications, do not change. Old lines recorded without a code, and notifications that cannot be read back, keep the text they were recorded with. If your SIEM rules match a Japanese `summary`, change them to English. What the windows show does not change.
- Forwarding destinations: newly set destinations refuse sending to internal addresses.
- MCP: the new tool `get_system_timeline` is added. `chain`, `chainBreak`, `blindNear`, `evidenceChecks` and `evidenceChecksNote` are added to `get_incident_timeline`, and `port_scan_block` in `actionTakenLabel` is shown as text. `audit_mcp_configs`, `run_package_cve_scan` and `run_package_cve_scan_languages` return SARIF 2.1.0 with `format: "sarif"`, and `tier` is added to each package CVE result. The notes (caveats) MCP returns to the LLM and the descriptions in `audit_mcp_configs` are in English whatever the display language.
- Notifications: notifications of the same form that differ only in values are shown once per 10 minutes (each is kept in the history).
- Updates: data older than the installed version, and manifests created more than 60 days ago, are not accepted.
- Checks: all 3443 XCTest tests pass (0 failures, 0 skipped).

## 1.17.30

Fixes for defects found by re-checking the lessons. The bundled PersonalSOC and AccessScope are replaced with their newer versions (the changes are listed under each app; PathScope is unchanged).

- **Fixed: with the Tailscale or WireGuard kill switch on, the dev-server port block and the port scan ban did not apply to traffic coming through the tunnel.** In the pf rules, the rule letting the tunnel through came first (in a disposable macOS VM, both the blocked port and the banned address got through). The blocks now come first, and both were confirmed to be refused.
- **Fixed: the port scan notification interval (once an hour per source) was checked before deciding whether the source is a paired Sensor and before banning.** After a Sensor audit was notified and the pairing then removed, scans from the same source within that hour were neither notified nor banned. When the treatment changes, and when the scan goes on after the ban ends, it is notified and banned regardless of the interval.
- **Fixed: the daily folder check of the web rules reported a file left in place again every day.** Reported files are remembered and not reported while unchanged. A changed content or another matching rule is reported again.
- **Fixed: masking in the execution record hid ssh settings that are not passwords, such as `NumberOfPasswordPrompts`.** The command behind `chroot` now gets that command's own rules too.
- **Fixed: when a RoamSwitch with the same signature in another location read a decoy credential file, it was notified as a reader.** Readers are now told apart by their signature (both the identifier and the Team ID) as well as the app's location.
- **Fixed: the AI agent setup audit said nothing when it hit its limits (2,000 files in all, 200 files per skill).** The number of files it did not read (at least so many) is now shown in the window, MCP and SARIF. Skill folders one level deeper (`<root>/<group>/<skill>`) are read too.
- **Fixed: text in English and other languages contained Japanese separators ("、" and "・"), the "・" bullet and full-width spaces for indentation.** The reasons for risky containers and suspicious autostart entries, infected files on USB, the vulnerability acceptance summary, the bullets of the score-drop and weakened-setting notifications, the first-run guide and the like now use the way of writing of each language (in English "; ", "• ", " · " and ASCII spaces). The notification history redraws bullets with the marks of the reading language. Ranges in Chinese and Korean are "1~2". Japanese is unchanged.
- **Added: a "Tailscale exposure" item in the health check (`overlay_exposure`).** It fails when ShieldsUp (inbound block) is off and a risky port listens where the tailnet can reach it, and when ngrok or cloudflared runs next to Tailscale. Exit node and subnet routes and Tailscale SSH are shown only. The macOS firewall does not count as a reason to pass. It only reads `tailscale status --json` and `tailscale debug prefs` (keeping only ShieldsUp, AdvertiseRoutes and RunSSH and throwing the private keys away), the list of listeners and process names; it changes no setting and sends nothing. When Tailscale is missing or not running, the item does not apply.
- **Changed: the pf block of the 3 attack-server IP addresses published by JPCERT/CC, and the link check warning for them, are gone.** Fixed addresses go stale, and blocking a destination reassigned to someone else is a false positive.

**Changed (affects scripts)**
