- MCP: the health check of `get_security_report` has 24 items. `skippedFileCount` and `truncated` are added to the result of `audit_agent_setup`. `actionTakenLabel` in `get_incident_timeline` no longer shows the values `notified`, `evidence_captured`, `cascade_decoy_deployed` and `air_gap_failed` as they are, but as text in the display language.
- Checks: all 3208 XCTest tests pass (0 failures, 0 skipped).

## 1.17.29

The RoamSwitch app itself is unchanged from 1.17.28. The bundled apps are replaced with their newer versions.

- **Fixed: 1.17.28 shipped older versions of PersonalSOC, PathScope and AccessScope.** 1.17.29 bundles PersonalSOC 1.0.4, PathScope 1.0.5 and AccessScope 1.0.6 (1.17.28 had 1.0.3, 1.0.4 and 1.0.5). The changes in those three are listed under each app. If you installed 1.17.28, the in-app update takes you to 1.17.29.

## 1.17.28

Fixes for defects found by re-checking the lessons and in a disposable macOS VM.

- **Fixed: an isolation from a decoy, entropy or XProtect opened about 10 minutes after the process that caused it exited.** It now lasts until a person releases it, and moves to degraded at 60 minutes. The isolation notification says how to release it and whether it opens on its own. Releasing these isolations from the menu or by uninstalling needs the confirmation and check box even on a trusted network.
- **Fixed: during an isolation the helper grew to tens of GB and the block was delayed.** `systemsetup -setremotelogin off`, run to stop sharing services, kept printing a confirmation question nobody answered, and the helper read all of it (in a VM it reached 42 GB in about 80 seconds and was ended by macOS, and the outbound block took about 80 seconds to apply). It now runs with `-f`, and command output is read up to 4 MB (stopped beyond that).
- **Fixed: pressing Return in the emergency window released the isolation without confirmation.** "Confirm it is safe and release the isolation" was the default button, and the window comes to the front by itself, so a Return typed in Terminal released the isolation (in a VM, 20 seconds after it began). All three windows (ransomware, runtime threat, ARP) no longer have a default button and release only through the same confirmation as the menu. A release sent while the helper restarted did not arrive, leaving the block in place while the window showed it released; it is now resent after 2, 5, 10 and 20 seconds.
- **Changed: Remote Login (sshd) is no longer stopped during lockdown and isolation.** Connections to port 22 from outside are dropped by the firewall in lockdown and by pf during an isolation (applying an isolation cuts open SSH sessions). Until now it was stopped, and on macOS 26 the helper could not turn it back on, so it had to be turned on again by hand. File Sharing and Screen Sharing are stopped and restored as before, and a notification is sent when they could not be restored.
- **Fixed: a broken SSH source restriction file opened SSH to everyone.** The restriction now continues with the previous list; with no record of a list, new SSH connections are refused except from this Mac itself (established sessions are not cut). A broken kill switch, Air-Gap or startup block marker keeps the block in place. When any of these happens, one notification is sent and "⚠️ Some protection settings cannot be confirmed (N)" appears at the top of the menu. A `<name>.last-valid` file is placed next to the helper's state files.
- **Fixed: broken or unreadable records were treated as empty.** When the quarantine record, the notification history, the allowed keyboards and USB devices, the registered networks, Sensor results and the like cannot be read, the reason is shown as "cannot be confirmed", and broken records are kept as `.corrupt-<time>` or `<key>.unreadable`, the newest 3. Records of decoy files, encryption, decoy credentials and saved browser passwords survive an app restart.
- **Added: SSH tunnels are detected.** When someone who came in over SSH uses `ssh -R` (Pro, with the port anomaly guard on), "An SSH session opened a listener on this Mac" is notified once. When a process without a terminal (one started by launchd, an SSH session without a terminal) opens an `ssh -R` / `-D` tunnel, the new rule `exec.ssh_remote_forward` (severity medium) notifies it.
- **Added: a notification when the protection level changes** (automatic and manual). Autostart detection is now a sign for lowering trust (on a registered "trusted" network, protection drops to Balanced for 6 hours). An autostart plist or script deleted and put back at once is notified again.
- **Fixed: the notification history stayed in the language it was recorded in.** Notifications recorded from this version on are shown in the current language in the history window, CSV and MCP (including notifications with plural words). The watchdog's automatic restart and stop notifications are kept in the history too. When the app's main thread stops for 30 seconds or more, the watchdog restarts it and notifies.
- **Fixed: a paused process that caught SIGTERM did not end on kill or a quit.** It now ends at once (when the helper has Full Disk Access).
- **Fixed: a repeat of the same execution-rule detection within 5 minutes left nothing in the record or the timeline.** It is kept as a repeat, without a banner, and counted in "N more" on the next banner.
- **Fixed: handling of `/etc/pam.d` across a macOS update.** Only system files that matched the baseline are taken into the baseline; notified tampering and `sudo_local` remain after the update. Files newly added by the update are not taken in silently but notified once as new. Ordinary runs of the signed docker CLI (Docker Desktop) and of an aws CLI installed for all users no longer raise decoy notifications.
- **Fixed: uninstalling left pf rules and the decoy records behind.** Reinstalling with the settings kept put a ransomware isolation in place right after start. The result now has an "Air-Gap isolation released" row, and failures show the item name instead of an English error.
- **Fixed: in English, German, French, Spanish, Italian and Portuguese, a plural was shown even for 1.** The form now matches the number. In Spanish, French, Italian and Portuguese, "%d infected files were found on "%@" and it was ejected" mixed up its arguments and either broke the sentence or crashed the app.
- Other fixes: results of audits requested from a new Sensor show their titles, descriptions and recommendations (CVE findings included) in the display language. When a scanner was not banned because the source was judged spoofed, the reason is notified. The owner of port 22 is shown as "launchd (com.openssh.sshd)". With Time Machine's automatic backups off, the backup check fails. Public keys are masked as a `SHA256:` fingerprint. Adding one MCP definition gives one notification. The score-drop warning is not repeated at every start while the state stays the same. The local network permission dialog is in 10 languages.

**Changed (affects scripts)**

- New exports are 0600 (an existing file chosen for replacement keeps its mode). When a file cannot be written, the place and the reason are shown. The settings files of the port scan record and the process execution record are 0600 too.
- MCP: `unreadable` is always present in `get_quarantine_status`, `get_notification_history` and `get_sensor_audit_results`. `summaryCode` is added to the timeline. The severity of `exec.ssh_remote_forward` is medium. The "could not be identified…" in `suspectedProcess` of `get_honeytoken_status` is in the language MCP answers in. Each finding in the saved `sensor_audit_results.json` gets `textKeys` (older apps skip it).
- Checks: all 3163 XCTests pass (0 failed, 0 skipped).

## 1.17.27

RoamSwitch itself is unchanged from 1.17.26. This release updates the bundled apps.

- **AccessScope 1.0.5**: English output picks the singular or plural to match the number (German, Spanish, French, Italian and Portuguese too). Reports, blocklists and RoamSwitch config drafts are written readable by the owner only (0600) when the file is newly created. An IP-based suppression rule no longer makes a high-severity chain disappear without going through the decision.
- **PersonalSOC 1.0.3**: an SSH attempt on a user that does not exist is no longer counted twice for its two log lines. The details of unresolved RoamSwitch detections are shown in the display language. Sentences in 10 languages are built from the sentence-kind code attached to the records. When run with sudo, the starting point of the PathScope paths is the user who ran sudo, and that starting point is shown in the output.
- **PathScope 1.0.4**: when run with sudo without `--start`, the starting point is the user who ran sudo, and it is printed to standard error.
- PersonalSOC and PathScope also arrive through the in-app update.

## 1.17.26

- **Fixed: checks that could not be measured raised the security report's pass count and score.** The ARP spoofing monitor (gateway unknown, or no earlier reading to compare with) and the Wi-Fi encryption strength (no Location permission, so the reading is unreliable) were counted as "passed" although nothing had been measured. A check whose result could not be established is now left out of both the total and the pass count, like these two. What the screen shows (the neutral "could not be determined" display) and the `inconclusiveReason` in the MCP are unchanged. Where ARP has no baseline yet, the score may differ a little from before.
- **Fixed: an event-forwarding destination kept showing its "last error" after it started delivering again.** The status view kept an old failure next to a destination that had recovered. A success now clears the last error. The count of failures is kept.
- Checks: all 2937 XCTests pass (1 skipped). One Sensor display test had been failing since the 1.17.25 change that made `http-security-headers` a read-only script; the test now matches the implementation.


## 1.10.0 - 1.17.25 (summarized)

The contents of these releases, grouped by theme. The full text of each entry is in this file's git history.

- (1.17.25) **Fixed: a listener on port 8000 was reported as "vLLM exposed" by its port number alone.** 8000 is vLLM's default, but also Django's, FastAPI's, `python -m http.server`'s and the published port of many containers; Docker Desktop's backend on 8000 raised the finding and the unprompted "unauthenticated service exposed" notification. The vLLM finding now needs the process name or the path of its executable to say `vllm` (a Python run from a `vllm` environment counts). Any other listener on 8000 gets only the product's own "likely" tier of local AI runtimes, at warning at most.
- (1.17.25) **Fixed: the Sensor audit result card and export no longer lists nmap scripts that only read something out of a service, and its headline no longer reads as "safe" when ports are open.** An audit of two web ports showed a page of lines (the page title, the server's clock, the headers) under "no problem found". Those scripts report no problem and are left out; the ones that check something (risky methods, a weak certificate, missing security headers) stay. When nothing was flagged but ports are open, the line now says "No known problem was found. The open ports are listed below (this is not proof that it is safe)" (9 languages).
- (1.17.25) Checks: all 2932 XCTests pass (1 skipped).
- (1.17.24) **Fixed: the secret scan printed "(safe)" when it found nothing.** Finding nothing means no key in a known format was present, which is not proof of safety (a bare AWS secret access key, which has no fixed format, cannot be detected). The word is removed from the texts in all 10 languages, which now say only that nothing was detected.
- (1.17.24) Checks: all 2927 XCTest tests pass.
- (1.17.23) **Added: 31 threat-actor reference briefs as MCP resources, `roamswitch://threat-briefs/{actor}`.** 20 nation-state groups (macOS realism, entry vector, and a mapping to existing features) and 11 commercial-spyware IOC references (domain and hash lists). Commercial-spyware domains are not loaded into any blocklist (reference only): the vendors' own infrastructure has often already been retired and the domains reassigned, so blocking them now risks blocking an unrelated current owner for no detection value. Mac-only for now; not yet ported to the Linux edition's MCP server.
- (1.17.23) **Added: a warning when AnyDesk, ScreenConnect/ConnectWise, TeamViewer or ngrok is downloaded from a source that isn't that vendor's own official site.** These tools have legitimate business uses, so a name match alone never triggers anything; it only notifies (never quarantines or blocks), and stays silent when the download's source is unknown (same rule as the Linux edition).
- (1.17.23) **Added: MCP tool calls are logged to a local file** (`~/Library/Application Support/RoamSwitch/mcp_tool_call_log.jsonl`). Only the tool name and the names of its argument keys are recorded, never argument values (such as file paths). Mac-only for now.
- (1.17.23) **Fixed: the result of the active verification is readable, and process names in the exposed-port list are no longer cut.** nmap's scripts that only read something out of a service (the page title, the server's clock, the headers, the state of a port) are no longer listed as findings, and the nmap output is shown under each finding. The heading for checks that did not finish says `Items that could not be checked` instead of `unreachable or timed out` (10 languages). The list of listening ports asks `lsof` for the full command name, so `com.docker.backend` is no longer shown as `com.docke`.
- (1.17.22) **Fixed: eleven protection options in the menu switched on or off at a single click, without asking.** "Automatically check npm dependencies", "Automatically check dependency vulnerabilities", "Tell me when an AI agent setup changes", "Notify when a setting gets weaker", "Watch for reverse shells all the time", "Reverse shell detection: include destinations in the local network", "Watch network setting changes", "Move a trusted network to standard protection on signs of compromise", "Block the source of an incoming port scan automatically (10 minutes)", "Automatic update of the link protection list" and the Tailscale kill switch now ask first, like the other protections do (Web and mail protection, DNS, tamper monitoring and so on). Switching on says what the option does, switching off says what stops working, and Cancel leaves it as it was (10 languages). The launch-at-login switch is left as it is, because macOS tells you about it itself.
- (1.17.22) **Fixed: the "Exposed ports" list in the menu was out of date, and it did not tell which ports the firewall really holds back.** The list is now measured again each time the menu opens (if the last reading is older than 5 seconds). With "block all incoming connections" on, an app on the macOS firewall allow list (for example Docker Desktop) is still let in. Such a listener is now marked "on the firewall allow list, not blocked" in the menu, the header no longer says it is blocked from outside, and the port audit no longer counts it as blocked. Measured: Docker published ports 3000 and 8000 on all interfaces and a Sensor on the same network reached both, while the menu said the firewall blocked them.
- (1.17.21) **Fixed: the MCP configuration audit did not read OpenCode servers written in OpenCode's current form `mcp.servers.<name>`.** It counted one server named `servers` and audited none of the real ones, so none of the findings (unpinned launchers, shell wrappers, plain `http://`, traffic-interception variables and so on) appeared for a current `opencode.json`. Both forms are read now, `mcp.timeout` is not taken for a server, and an older entry that happens to be named `servers` or `timeout` is still a server.
- (1.17.20) **Added: PHP that Homebrew does not own is now part of the vulnerability scan.** PHP installed with MAMP, XAMPP, a tarball or source build, phpbrew, asdf, phpenv, or mise is not in `brew list`, so PHP2Shell (CVE-2026-109618 / CVE-2026-109619) was not reported. It is added to the package CVE scan as `php <version>` and matched against the `php` rows of the same map as the Homebrew list. The version is read without running the binary (`php_version.h`, then the directory name such as `php-8.4.24` or MAMP's `php8.4.24`, then the `X-Powered-By: PHP/8.4.24` string inside the binary). What Homebrew and MacPorts own is left out. A running `php`, `php-fpm` or `php-cgi` is checked too. Measured: the version of a real arm64 Mach-O PHP (the Homebrew bottle, 8.5.12) was read.
- (1.17.20) **Fixed: three switches in the menu were marked with the system check mark (✓).** "Check npm dependencies automatically", "Notify when AI agent settings change" and "Reverse shell detection: include destinations on the local network" now use the same marking as the other switches, ✅ when on and an icon when off. The system check mark is kept for choosing one of several options, such as a language, a mode or a backend.
- (1.17.20) **Added: the app's built-in help (MCP `get_app_help`) now explains the PHP check** (10 languages).
- (1.17.19) **Added: Node.js that Homebrew does not own is now part of the vulnerability scan.** A Node.js installed with the official installer, nvm, fnm, Volta, asdf, mise or n, or from a tarball under `/opt` and the like, is not in `brew list`, so the scan did not see it (a 22.23.3 install was not reported for CVE-2026-111497, Brotli, "Node2Shell", which is in KEV and fixed in 22.23.4, 24.22.1 and 26.12.1). It shows up in the Package CVE Scan window, in MCP `run_package_cve_scan` and in the diagnosis item "Exploitable Vulnerabilities (Installed Software)". The version is read without running the binary (the `node_version.h` beside it, the directory name, then the source URL inside the binary). What is found is added to the Homebrew list as `node <version>` and judged by the `node` rows of the same map. Anything Homebrew or MacPorts owns is left out. On a Mac without Homebrew, what was found still counts as checked. A binary whose version cannot be read is not reported. The rules are the same as on Linux.
- (1.17.19) **Changed: a port that was blocked automatically is released automatically once it listens on 127.0.0.1 (and ::1) only** (Pro). After exposing a service with `OLLAMA_HOST=0.0.0.0` and having it blocked, moving it back to 127.0.0.1 left the block in place. When every listener of the port, with a known address, has been local for two checks in a row (about 4 seconds), the block is lifted and one line goes to the notification history. The program that was blocked is removed from the "known" list, so exposing it again is blocked again. A port blocked by hand (including one touched in the port diagnosis window), and a port with no listener at all, are not released (10 languages).
- (1.17.19) **Added: the in-app help (MCP `get_app_help`) now explains the Node.js check and the automatic release of the block** (10 languages).
- (1.17.18) **Fixed: on a Mac that does not run a web server, "Protection status" showed the worker-crash watch as "not working" in red.** It was also counted in the number at the top and in the warning line of the menu. There is just no log to follow, which is not a fault. The row now reads "not applicable", is left out of the count and the warning line, and says it starts by itself within 10 minutes once the log exists. The values of `get_guard_status` (`off` / `log_not_found`) are unchanged (10 languages).
- (1.17.18) **Fixed: in the notification history, two notifications with the same title in the same second showed the second one as an empty frame.** Rows are now identified by their position instead of time and title.
- (1.17.18) **Changed: notifications with the same cause are merged into one.** When one process read `~/.aws/config` and `credentials` at the same time, the honeytoken watch sent one notification per file. For 2.5 seconds after the first detection it now waits for the other files of the same process and lists them in one notification. The notification history merges entries with the same title and the same body within one second (a different body is kept as a separate event).
- (1.17.17) **Fixed: the result of "Run active check" on a port was shown in an alert, so for a port with many findings (the CVE list of an Ollama port, for example) it ran past the screen and could not be scrolled.** It now appears in a sheet that scrolls. The text can be selected and copied.
- (1.17.17) **Fixed: in the result of an active check, findings from nmap carried the heading "confirmed: reachable without authentication".** An nmap finding is nmap's own result, not something RoamSwitch confirmed. With the heading, even the check of a port that listens on 127.0.0.1 only read as if every line (`http-title` and the like) were "reachable without authentication". Findings from nmap now get no heading (the same as on Linux).
- (1.17.17) **Fixed: typosquat detection missed look-alikes with a js tail on the name, and look-alikes of names that were not in the popular-name list.** Measured on the names of the 10 fake npm packages Socket reported (`typescriptjs`, `nodemonjs`, `zustand.js` and others), only 1 was flagged. When a name ends in `js` or `.js`, a second comparison now takes the tail off the name and compares it with each popular name with its tail taken off (only the name being checked needs the tail, so a real `chart` is not treated as a look-alike of `chart.js`; names ending in `-js` or `_js`, such as `postcss-js`, are left out). The popular-name list embedded in the app gained 7 names: discord.js, ethers, web3, zustand, vitest, hardhat and jotai. 9 of the 10 are now flagged (`deezcord.js` has distance 3 and is still missed). A scoped name (`@solana/web3.js` and the like) lives in its owner's namespace, so it is left out of this tail comparison (`@solana/web3.js` is not taken for a copy of `web3`). **The signed list that is distributed wins over the embedded one, so the 7 names reach the signed list only after the list is rebuilt by the daily job.**
- (1.17.17) **Added: the app's built-in help (MCP `get_app_help`) now explains the match with the js tail removed in its typosquat detection entry** (10 languages).
- (1.17.17) **Added: automatic check of dependency vulnerabilities** (Pro, on by default). It used to run only when you pressed the button. It now notifies you when a lockfile in a registered project folder holds a CVE that is in CISA's KEV or has an EPSS of 0.9 or higher, and no valid acceptance covers it (npm, PyPI, crates.io, RubyGems, Packagist, Go, Maven). It checks once a lockfile has been quiet for 20 seconds after a write (at most 120 seconds), 45 seconds after start-up, and once a day. It makes no network traffic and rewrites none of your files. The same finding is not repeated, and an ecosystem without a CVE map is not reported. The menu item "Check dependency vulnerabilities automatically (Pro)" turns it off, and `get_guard_status` shows `dependencyWatch`. The rules and wording are the same as on Linux.
- (1.17.17) **Added: a notice when a setting gets weaker** (Pro, on by default). After a diagnosis, when a setting that passed last time now fails, you get a single notification (10 items: SSH, sudo, SIP, Gatekeeper, automatic updates, XProtect, Safari's phishing warning, accessory-connection protection, FileVault and backups). The firewall and stealth mode are left out because their result depends on the protection level of the connected network. The first run and items that could not be measured are not reported, and the same item is not repeated within 24 hours. The menu item "Notify when a setting is weakened (Pro)" turns it off.
- (1.17.17) **Added: the notice for a changed AI agent setup now has a switch to turn it off** (Pro, on by default). Turning it off stops watching the files, stops comparing, and forgets what was reported. The check window says it is stopped.
- (1.17.17) **Fixed: the link guard's verdict now matches a brand name in a subdomain as a whole word, not as a substring** (manual check). `online`, `pipeline` and `airline` hit "line". Real impersonation shapes (`apple.com.verify-account-id.example.net`, `login-microsoft.example.net` and the like) are detected as before. It is the same verdict as on Linux.
- (1.17.17) **Fixed: the link guard's mode name and description did not match what it does.** They said "Warn only (do not block)", but it holds the connection for about 8 seconds, asks you to allow or block, and blocks it if there is no answer. The mode is now named "Ask first (block if no answer)" (the same as on Linux; 10 languages). The built-in help and knowledge base said "to disable it temporarily, choose warn only", which was wrong; they now say to choose "Off".
- (1.17.17) **Changed: the popular-name list now grows by itself every day from the public rankings of downloads and dependents** (the same rules as on Linux). The signed list that is distributed carries the hand-added names (`packages`) and the names added from the rankings (`rankedPackages`, unscoped, up to 4,000). Names from the rankings are matched up to distance 1, and the check also looks at swapped adjacent letters and at up to two typo-like edits to a dependency that carries install-time code. To keep thousands of names fast, the per-name preparation is done once per scan (8,310 names: about 1,100 µs down to about 300 µs per check). **The signed list arrives after the daily rebuild, so new names do not arrive at once.**
- (1.17.17) **Fixed: after you added the npm and pnpm aliases, npm and pnpm stopped working.** The value of the alias was split at the space in "Application Support". The value is now wrapped in single quotes. A broken line written by an earlier version is detected and replaced by the new one on the spot with "Add alias" (no duplicate; a `.roamswitch-backup` is taken before the replacement). The wrapper finds the real npm by walking the absolute paths in PATH (skipping a symlink to itself and anything inside the project's `node_modules`). Uninstalling removes the new and old lines from both the zsh and bash rc files. The shell is taken from the login shell, and nothing is written for shells such as fish that do not read the zsh rc.
- (1.17.17) **Fixed: adding or removing the history setting in `~/.zshrc` damaged lines that are not UTF-8.** Adding now only appends to the original bytes, and removing works on bytes line by line.
- (1.17.17) **Changed: in the dependency diagnosis, Yarn Berry 4.15.0 and later is no longer flagged when nothing is set.** The default of `npmMinimalAgeGate` is 1440 (minutes) from 4.15.0 (it is flagged only when you set the wait to 0).
- (1.17.17) **Changed: the ClamAV search now includes the official package location (`/usr/local/clamav`).** Homebrew's 1.5.4 cannot verify the signature database when combined with OpenSSL 4.1. The signed official installer puts ClamAV in `/usr/local/clamav`, so it is looked at first.
- (1.17.16) **Added: a "Protection status" window.** It lists, guard by guard (36), whether each protection you switched on really works. Open it from the menu (Mac Security Diagnosis > "🛡️ Protection status…") or with "Show all protections…" on the card at the top of the diagnosis report (a summary and only the protections that are not working). There are five states: Working, Partly working, Not working, Off and Pro only. For a protection that is not working, it shows the reason code (`pro_not_active`, `helper_not_connected`, `system_extension_not_approved` and others) and a sentence on the cause and the fix; depending on the reason, there is also a button to activate Pro, approve the helper or open System Settings. It reloads every 15 seconds and with "Check again". The verdict and the text come from the same data as the menu marks, the notifications and the MCP `get_guard_status`. While a protection is not working, the top of the menu also has a line "Some protections are not working (N). See why…". A Pro feature that is on only by default on a Mac without Pro shows "Pro only", not "Not working".
- (1.17.16) **Fixed: DNS threat protection wrote its default "on" into the settings at every launch.** On a Mac without Pro that counted as "on by your choice" and showed "Not working" (`pro_not_active`). The default is no longer written; a missing setting is read as on.
- (1.17.16) **Fixed: a stop in the update of the detection data (`state_stale`) became the notification "Inputs cannot be read".** It is no longer a notification; it shows only in the menu and the window.
- (1.17.16) Three guards that had no name now have one (checking the clipboard for secrets, turning Wi-Fi off during isolation, proof-based vulnerability scan).
- (1.17.16) **Added: the in-app help (MCP `get_app_help`) now explains the protection status** (10 languages).
- (1.17.14) **Added: detection of a child process of an AI coding agent trying to send credentials out** (exec recorder, notify-only; `exec.agent_sensitive_send` and `exec.agent_sensitive_then_send`; while the exec recorder is on, which is off by default). The first fires when a command started by claude, codex, gemini, opencode, aider, goose, cursor-agent, openclaw, qwen or crush sends out a file such as a key in `~/.ssh`, `~/.aws`, `.env`, an agent's sign-in file, browser data or the keychain, or carries a secret value in its arguments (`curl -T`, `curl -d`, `scp` or `rsync` to a remote host, `rclone`, `aws s3 cp`, `nc` and the like); High when a file is named, Medium for a secret value alone. The second fires when the same agent sends something out within five minutes of reading credential files (the sending command need not name the file). Sends to this machine (localhost, 127.0.0.1) are not watched. The Claude Code binary under `~/.local/share/claude/versions/<version>` is recognized too. Only commands the agent starts as child processes are seen: the agent's own file reads, what it sends to its model provider, `git push` and connections that a script runtime opens itself are not, and an agent whose process name is `node` is not recognized.
- (1.17.13) **Added: the ClickFix clipboard warning now also opens a dialog that stays in front of the other windows** (while the ClickFix guard is on). A notification banner is gone in a few seconds, so the warning could be missed before the paste. The dialog's button "Empty the clipboard" empties it, only if it still holds the text that was warned about (the notification's button still works as before). The clipboard is not emptied automatically.
- (1.17.13) **Fixed: the ClickFix check missed most ways of decoding base64 and running the result.** With `| zsh`, `base64 -D | zsh`, `| /bin/bash`, `| sudo bash`, `bash -c "$(…)"`, `eval`, `source <(…)` and a `tr` step in between, it caught only 6 of 16 ways of decoding and running. The shell name (sh, bash, zsh, dash, ksh, csh, tcsh, ash, fish) is now read up to a word boundary, and 15 of the 16 are caught (passing the result to python3 is not covered). `base64 -d | shasum` and `base64 -d | sha256sum` were also wrongly caught as handing the result to a shell; that is fixed. Official install one-liners (`curl … | bash`) are still not flagged.
- (1.17.13) **Fixed: the description of the ClickFix guard said it empties the clipboard automatically; it now describes what happens (it warns, and a button empties the clipboard)** (README and the site).
- (1.17.13) **Bundled app**: AccessScope is updated to 1.0.4 (see the AccessScope section). AccessScope.app is in the DMG.
- (1.17.13) Checks: all 2691 tests of the Mac app pass, with 0 skipped.
- (1.17.12) **Added: the audit of AI agent setups also reads the start-up hooks of Gemini CLI, Codex, Cursor and OpenCode** (the menu bar's "Audit AI agent instruction files", MCP `audit_agent_setup`). Until now it read only Claude Code's `.claude/settings.json` and `.vscode/tasks.json`, so a hook that runs just by opening a repository was invisible when it sat in another tool's settings file. In the planting in 42 real GitHub repositories that JFrog reported on 26 October 2026, the hook in `.gemini/settings.json` was not visible either. The places it reads are the project's `.gemini/settings.json`, `.codex/hooks.json`, `.codex/config.toml` (`[hooks]`), `.cursor/hooks.json`, `opencode.json(c)` (`plugin` and `formatter`) and `.opencode/plugins/`, and the same kinds of files in the home folder. A hook in a project file is Medium severity; one that downloads and runs, or decodes and runs, is High. A hook in a home file is reported as an increase from the accepted state. The text of a hook is not shown in the result, only the number of hooks.
- (1.17.12) **Added: the MCP configuration audit also reads Codex's `config.toml` (`[mcp_servers]`) and OpenCode's `opencode.json(c)` (`mcp`)** (both home and project). The findings are the same as for the JSON configurations so far.
- (1.17.12) **Changed: when a scan is cut off at its limit and finds 0 results, MCP `search_exec_events` and `get_process_tree` now add a note to `caveats`, so that 0 is not read as "there was none".** `get_exposed_ports` gains a `note` saying that `isFirewallShielded` shows the state of RoamSwitch's protection level (block all incoming), not the setting of macOS's own firewall (10 languages).
- (1.17.12) **Bundled app**: PathScope is updated to 1.0.3 (see the PathScope entry). PathScope.app is in the DMG.
- (1.17.12) Checks: all 2688 tests of the Mac app pass, with 0 skipped. On a fake home and project, the fake of the five places in JFrog's report gave 3 findings, and adding Codex, Cursor and OpenCode gave 7, the same result as the Linux edition.
- (1.17.11) **Added: the diagnosis item "Vulnerabilities likely to be exploited (installed software)" is checked every hour, and a notification is sent when it newly fails** (the first check is 5 minutes after launch). It notifies once when a new vulnerability is added, or when 24 hours pass with the same contents. Pro gets a push notification; on Free it stays in the notification history. Nothing is sent when an update brings the item back to pass, or when it could not be measured. What was installed with Homebrew can be updated with `brew upgrade`. There is no switch in the settings.
- (1.17.11) Note: the Mac already ran a diagnosis every four hours, but it notified only when the score dropped, and nothing was notified when this item alone failed.
- (1.17.10) **Added: `run_active_vuln_scan` takes a `format` argument** (`json` by default, or `sarif`). It returns SARIF 2.1.0 in the same form as the Linux edition. The rule ID is the probe name, and the log carries `evidenceKind`, the checks that could not be made (`inconclusive`) and whether the scan was complete (`complete`). A rule that SARIF rule IDs are never renamed is written down, with a test that fails when a published ID is removed or renamed.
- (1.17.10) **Added: the dependency source scan (the "Dependency sources (Pro)" tab and MCP `run_dependency_source_scan`) reports `release_age_gate_missing`.** For a project with an npm, pnpm, yarn Berry, bun or uv lockfile, it reports with Low severity when neither the project nor the home folder sets a rule that keeps out versions published a moment ago. The setting names and the way of reading them are the same as in the Linux edition (npm `min-release-age` or `before`, pnpm `minimumReleaseAge`, yarn Berry `npmMinimalAgeGate`, bun `minimumReleaseAge`, uv `exclude-newer`). It does not say that a bad package is installed. pnpm 11 and later is on by default, so it is not reported. Environment variables and settings elsewhere are not read. It uses no network (10 languages).
- (1.17.10) **Added: `get_incident_timeline` returns `incidents` (events put together per incident) and `openIncidentCount`.** Events in the same chain of cause, or about the same process within 15 minutes, become one incident. `events` stays the record, and the grouping is not proof of one attack. A saved evidence bundle and an expired risk acceptance do not count as detections, and an incident made only of them is `recorded`. Values are spelled in camelCase, as in `events`.
- (1.17.10) **Fixed: when ClamAV exits with code 2 and checks nothing, the app now shows a failure instead of "No threats found (0 files)"** (10 languages). This covers a signature database that cannot be loaded and the like. Homebrew's ClamAV 1.5.4 combined with OpenSSL 4.1.0 cannot load any signature database, so it checks 0 files (a fix is under way in the upstream pull request #1873 of Cisco-Talos/clamav). Before, the app showed "No threats found" in this state too.
- (1.17.10) **Fixed: the probe `webvuln-xss` was missing from `probesRun` of `run_active_vuln_scan`.**
- **The diagnosis item for vulnerabilities likely to be exploited, and the daily KEV list** (1.17.9): the diagnosis has a new item, "Vulnerabilities likely to be exploited (installed software)" (22 to 23 items). It compares the packages installed with Homebrew with the known-CVE data on this Mac, and fails when a vulnerability that CISA has confirmed as exploited (KEV), or whose probability of exploitation (EPSS) is 0.9 or higher, is still unfixed. PHP installed outside Homebrew is checked by reading its version and configuration directly. It uses no network. A signed KEV list (about 30 KB) is fetched every day and added to the KEV marks in the data. The "Check PHP settings" button in the Web rules settings (added in 1.17.1) is removed and folded into this item.
- **The generation of the Homebrew vulnerability data** (1.17.9): it did not cover php, php@8.x, tailscale, node, nginx, openssl, python, postgresql, brotli and about 25 other packages. They are now covered (the data itself was published separately).
- **The text of "Automatic security updates"** (1.17.9): it now matches what is checked (whether Software Update's automatic check is on). It does not check whether macOS itself is installed automatically. It used to say "latest patches applied automatically" and "the configuration is optimal" (10 languages).
- **Bundled app update** (1.17.8): updated to AccessScope 1.0.3, PersonalSOC 1.0.2 and PathScope 1.0.2 (see the entry of each app). PathScope.app and PersonalSOC.app are in the DMG. AccessScope can also be updated from "Update" in the settings of its window app. The features of RoamSwitch itself were unchanged from 1.17.6; the version was aligned with the Linux edition.
- **Bundled app update** (1.17.5): AccessScope is now 1.0.1. There are fewer false High and Medium findings on a site that answers every URL with the same page, and the IPs and domains in the LLM's text are written with plain dots. The window app can also be updated from AccessScope's own settings ("Update"). The features of RoamSwitch itself were unchanged from 1.17.4; the version matched the Linux edition. Bundled: AccessScope 1.0.1, PersonalSOC 1.0.0, PathScope 1.0.0.
- **Bundled apps** (1.11.0 to 1.11.2): 1.11.0 bundled AccessScope 0.2.0 and PersonalSOC 0.1.9, 1.11.1 AccessScope 0.3.0 and PathScope 0.1.7, and 1.11.2 AccessScope 0.3.1. AccessScope's detections became larger, stronger and quieter, and it reads more kinds of logs (0.2.0); it now finds the behavior of the same address across several logs, logins from an unfamiliar source and differences from the usual, and the "Diagnose logs" screen became easier to follow (0.3.0); 0.3.1 unified the Korean and Chinese terms for "severity" and "confidence" in the screens and reports. PathScope refreshed its known-vulnerability data (0.1.7). PersonalSOC gained the import of known vulnerabilities in dependency packages and a ledger of confirmed facts (0.1.9). See each app's entry. RoamSwitch itself arrives by the in-app update (Sparkle), and the bundled apps can be updated from inside each app (Settings → Update).
- **MCP tools and the config audit** (1.11.0 to 1.11.1): added the MCP tool `verify_security_findings` (31 MCP tools in all). It re-evaluates the items of `get_security_report` (18 checks) in their current state and returns, per item, still present (stillPresent), fixed (resolved) or cannot confirm (inconclusive), with a language-independent reason; it is read-only, and is meant for checking that something is really fixed. `checkIds` takes at most 50, and omitting it or an empty array means all items. An item that could not be evaluated or is not reliable becomes inconclusive and is never treated as safe. ARP becomes fixed only when it matches the gateway MAC of a registered trusted network. For the host firewall, stealth mode, gateway ARP pinning, malware protection, DNS and USB items, the result notes that they reflect the app's settings and do not re-measure the real OS state. Each item of `get_security_report` carries `inconclusiveReason` only when needed. `get_guard_status` gained port-scan detection and lock-file tamper monitoring (28 items). The MCP config audit (`audit_mcp_configs` and the screen "Audit MCP settings (AI integration)…" in the Malware Protection menu) checks the MCP config files of AI tools for free, without any network access and without printing secret values (auto-approve, plain http, shell one-liner launch, a connection to HexStrike, writable configs, `enableAllProjectMcpServers`, launchers without a pinned version, container host privileges). It also fixed the detail showing "0 tool(s)" when `autoApprove: true`. In 1.11.1 a non-loopback `http://` is also flagged as plain HTTP (`remote_plain_http`) when the destination is passed as a launch argument such as `--server http://host` or `--url=http://host` instead of the `url` field (as in the HexStrike AI sample config); that form was not flagged before. Loopback and `https://` are out of scope, and an `http://` inside a shell or interpreter one-liner is not a server address, so only the one-liner finding is shown, as before. One server gets one finding even if both the `url` field and an argument carry it, and a URL with credentials shows only the host name. The MCP skill `closing-out-roamswitch-findings` was added too (it ends each finding as confirmed, denied with a reason, or undetermined, and lowers the severity without deleting the finding even when there are mitigating circumstances).
- **Diagnosis fixes** (1.11.0): the diagnosis reported "enabled" or "disabled" without having measured anything. `fdesetup`, `csrutil`, `spctl` and `softwareupdate` were judged enabled or disabled even when they returned an error message or empty output; now a verdict is made only when the exit code and the output exactly match a real machine, and anything else is "could not be measured" (SIP's `enabled (Custom Configuration)` counts as partially enabled). A failing `lsof` was also judged "no public ports"; "no listeners" now requires exit code 1 with no output, and anything else is a failure (standard output and standard error are read at the same time so it cannot hang). Items that could not be measured are removed from the score and the failure count, and the app's own view and the dashboard show "not confirmed" instead of "disabled" (when the port list has not been measured yet it is shown apart from an `lsof` failure, and the menu shows a stale list as stale). The diagnosis through MCP read the MCP process's own settings (UserDefaults) instead of the app's (`malware_scanning`, `dns_threat_guard`, `usb_zero_trust`); fixed. `sshd_config` is parsed by its real rules (`ssh_hardening`: `Include`, the first value wins, everything after `Match` is out of scope). The MCP tool descriptions now match the real network behavior (the flat claim "does not touch the network" was corrected: the diagnosis may send an ICMP ping into the LAN to find the gateway MAC, normally once and up to 3 times when the MAC cannot be resolved; `get_exposed_ports` sends one HTTP GET to 127.0.0.1 per public port; the only tool that contacts an outside host is `run_npm_audit_signatures`; the MCP knowledge base (10 languages) was updated too).
- **New and stronger detections** (1.11.0): the active vulnerability check gained detection of an API that accepts execution by AI without authentication (HexStrike AI, `hexstrike-noauth`); it sends a single `GET /api/processes/list` to a Python listener, and since that applies to every Python listener it is not shown by the passive diagnosis. The process execution log gained two rules that find chains of attack tools (notification only): when one process looks up 8 or more kinds of attack tools with `which` or similar within 30 seconds (`exec.offensive_tool_inventory`), and when a script runtime or an AI agent starts 3 or more kinds within 2 minutes (`exec.offensive_tool_burst`); a person typing in a terminal is excluded. Link guard treats numeric hosts such as `2130706433` or `0x7f.1` as a direct IP, and also detects URLs that put a fake destination before an `@`, such as `https://apple.com@evil.example/`. Listener detection no longer reports the interception proxy Caido when it listens on loopback only, reports it when it is reachable from the network, and knows the MCP Inspector ports (6274 and 6277).
- **Detection rules for web shells and injected code (Web Rules)** (1.11.11): finds PHP, JavaScript, JSP and ASP web shells and injected code in files of local web development (`~/Sites`, MAMP, Herd, Local, `/Library/WebServer/Documents`, and the project folders registered for package-CVE scanning). The 18 rules are shared with the Linux edition; none is extracted from a malware sample and they describe publicly documented techniques only. The rules come in three layers: built into the app, delivered once a day by the signed receive-only update, and your own files in `~/Library/Application Support/RoamSwitch/rules.d/*.json` (custom rules are read only from files owned by root or you that group and others cannot write). Quarantine is `critical` only by default. `high` findings leave the file alone and show up as a notification and in a list in Settings (with "do not detect again"). The level can be set in Settings to "do not quarantine", "critical only" or "critical and high", and the list of restored files that are not detected again is separate from the ClamAV exclusions. In folders the app already watches (for example Downloads), detection is right after a write. Web development folders are checked once a day and with "Check the web development folders now" in Settings (the daily check skips `node_modules` and `.git` for time; a check of a folder you pick does enter `node_modules`). The Mac cannot stop anything before it runs (as before, all detection is after the fact). A post-detection hook: an executable at `~/Library/Application Support/RoamSwitch/hooks/post-detection` is called with the hits as JSON on standard input (only a file owned by root or you that nobody else can write runs; it is stopped after 30 seconds). An account response (off by default) needs the administrator password to turn on; it revokes the login shell of the owner of a quarantined file, but never touches root, UIDs below 501, or anyone who is logged in (the previous shell is recorded and can be restored in Settings). 0 findings in 114,501 files of a development folder. Verified in a macOS 26 virtual machine, as root under the helper's own sandbox: the real dscl shell change and restore, and the exclusion of the logged-in user, root and low UIDs; the Settings screen was checked in the same virtual machine, which also found and fixed a defect where the screen could not be opened without ClamAV installed (the Malware Protection menu now always shows "Web shell detection rule settings…"). Not verified: the administrator-password confirmation and the app-to-helper call path (they need a signed helper). The new text is in 9 languages (machine-translated; not reviewed by native speakers). Linux-specific changes (`kernel-modules`, `bluetooth-audit`, write monitoring in the Server Edition and so on) are in Linux 1.11.23.
- **The MCP configuration audit has an item for environment variables** (1.11.11, `code_loading_env`): it reports `LD_PRELOAD`, `DYLD_INSERT_LIBRARIES`, `BASH_ENV` and `PYTHONSTARTUP` in `env`. For `NODE_OPTIONS` and the Java-family variables, only when the value has a code-loading option such as `--require` or `-javaagent`. Variable names are matched case-insensitively, and values are not shown in the result (10 languages).
- **Default protections with Pro** (1.11.0): fixed protections that should have turned on with Pro but did not. Inbound port-scan detection was not wired to turn on by default when Pro is activated, and it now is (the source is blocked automatically for 10 minutes only; the gateway, DNS and this Mac itself are never blocked; only the blocking can be turned off in the menu). Deactivating and re-activating the license did not bring the default "on" back; fixed (settings you turned off yourself are respected; on a Mac that had already deactivated once, the "off" left by an older version cannot be repaired automatically, so turn it on once from the menu). The free edition also ran web and mail protection and the autostart monitor and showed the folder permission dialog; those two now run only with Pro. The protections that are on by default with Pro are: automatic blocking of unknown ports, inbound port-scan detection, ransomware decoys and entropy detection, credential honeytokens, automatic log audit, tamper monitoring of important files and lock files, and automatic blocking on ARP spoof and XProtect link. ClickFix, Docker, USB, Bluetooth, gateway pinning, process execution log, browser credential monitoring, VPN and Sensor stay off by default because they need permissions or settings, or could interrupt normal use.
- **Wording about network use** (1.11.0): the in-app "Zero Telemetry" wording flatly said "nothing is sent to an external server" and "no data is sent at all", although the minimum necessary connections are made (license activation, receiving update data, expanding shortened URLs). The security policy in the overview, the link safety diagnosis sheet, the help (10 languages) and the knowledge base now say that there is no telemetry that sends usage or diagnosis results, and that the necessary connections are published in the design document. "No other tab connects at all" on the package CVE match screen now says that the matching itself does not connect. It is also stated that expanding a shortened URL in the link safety diagnosis accesses the target URL directly. The description of the update frequency of the package CVE map and the vulnerability diagnosis CVE map was corrected too (the expiry is checked daily and the data is fetched only about once every 30 days; the knowledge base and help (10 languages) said "once a day"; the link protection threat feed is still daily).
- **AccessScope bundled** (1.10.28): AccessScope 0.1.0 is now in the DMG, next to RoamSwitch.app. A separate app that finds attack attempts in web server and SSH access logs and shows the evidence (see AccessScope 0.1.0). You can update it from inside the app (Settings → Update).
- **MCP security report** (1.10.28): fixed the MCP `get_security_report` always reporting the gateway ARP lock as "disabled", even when it was on. The report was generated without the ARP lock setting. `get_guard_status` already returned the correct value.
- **Links to the product page and others** (1.10.28): the links to the purchase page, help and the MCP knowledge base inside the app now point to roamswitch.com.
- **Bundled PathScope** (1.10.22 to 1.10.26): Even with "unrestricted", private key files and API keys are no longer sent (see PathScope 0.1.2). 1.10.23 fixes one German label (0.1.3). 1.10.24 fixes the update section text and the unreadable drop-down text on Linux, among other things (0.1.4; the Mac screens hardly change). If 0.1.0 or later is already installed, you can update from inside the app (Settings → Update). 1.10.25 adds checking installed software versions against known vulnerabilities (0.1.5). In 1.10.26, an explanation is added when PathScope itself is the subject of a finding (0.1.6). 1.11.11 bundles PathScope 0.1.8 (it adds CVEs with a high EPSS and keeps them; see the PathScope 0.1.8 entry).
- **False ransomware detection** (1.10.26): Fixed a false ransomware detection when copying many compressed files. When a lot of files were copied, already-compressed files such as `.gz` and `.jpg` (which have high entropy by nature) were counted as "encrypted file writes", and this could even trigger the full network cut-off (air-gap isolation), naming Spotlight's indexer as the culprit. A file whose first bytes are a genuine compressed or media format (gzip, zip, JPEG, PNG, mp4 and so on) is now left out of the check. Encryption destroys those first bytes, so detection of real encryption is not weakened. Folders such as `.venv`, `node_modules` and `.git` are skipped, and Spotlight's worker processes (`mdworker_shared` and the like) and any process started from macOS's own locations (`/System`, `/usr`) are treated as safe.
- **Tailscale reconnect loop** (1.10.26): Fixed the exit node reconnecting and disconnecting about every 40 seconds after a Tailscale disconnect. While the network service reconnected after the disconnect, the gateway's MAC address could not be read for about 40 seconds; the 30-second grace period ran out first and the network was judged to be an unknown one (Lockdown). So even at a Standard-protection home network, it briefly went to Lockdown and the VPN connected, then disconnected when the MAC came back, and reconnected again, with traffic stopping on and off. The grace period is now 90 seconds and ends as soon as the gateway is back.
- **App quitting after an isolation release** (1.10.26): Added protection against the app quitting after an isolation is released. When the app reached its limit of open files (256), starting ClamAV raised an exception and the app quit. The crash report does not prove that this limit was the cause. The limit is now raised, and the pipe for ClamAV's output is always closed, even when the launch fails.
- **Privileged helper hardening** (1.10.5): the helper now runs inside a sandbox from launch and self-checks it. It only talks to the genuine app, and Mach-O, `eslogger`, `tcpdump` and Sensor traffic are parsed outside root. Snapshot restore runs with the user's own privileges.
- **Quarantine and port scans** (1.10.5, 1.10.6): ARP-spoofing quarantine is lifted automatically once the cause is confirmed gone. Fixed quarantine release trusting a spoofed gateway and ARP pinning locking in a forged MAC. Automatic port-scan blocking now verifies the source MAC, and IPv6 scans are detected. A manual quarantine that failed no longer keeps showing "quarantined".
- **Ransomware defense and recovery** (1.10.0 to 1.10.8): added generic entropy-based detection and reworked decoy files so they no longer break real tools. "Ransomware Recovery" lets you pick deleted files from snapshot contents, and detects deletion of recovery snapshots by a third party. Added a separate "Recovery and Uninstall" menu.
- **Tailscale and VPN** (1.10.1 to 1.10.5): the Exit Node is now checked against the real OS route rather than tailscaled's self-report. VPN tool upgrades are announced, and a pinned folder with files outside the manifest is rejected.
- **Link guard and download guard** (1.10.1): fixed brand-impersonation (homograph) detection that never worked for Apple and PayPal.
- **Notifications and UI** (1.10.0 to 1.10.9): identical notifications stay silent for 10 minutes (history keeps them all). Fixed a sound with no banner. Added explanations and confirmations when toggling features, showed block state in the exposed-port list, and separated "cannot verify" from "failed" in the overall diagnosis.
- **Security fixes** (1.10.4, 1.10.5): Sensor control traffic never falls back to plaintext. The license signing key was rotated (both old and new public keys are accepted). Out-of-range settings (ports, block durations) can no longer break firewall rule rebuilds.
- **Privileged-helper resource leak and busy loop** (1.10.10, 1.10.11): fixed pipes for external-command output not being closed, so open file descriptors reached the limit (256) and the app showed "helper not connected"; the firewall and DNS being rewritten roughly every 2 seconds; the helper possibly stalling on large output; and the gateway pinning being deleted and re-added on every re-evaluation even when already pinned.
- **NDP pinning and diagnostics** (1.10.10, 1.10.11): pinning the IPv6 router's NDP entry did not actually work (`ndp -s` without an interface returns exit code 0 but does not pin). It now pins with `fe80::…%en0` and verifies afterwards. Also fixed: critical-file tamper monitoring reporting "deleted" for a file that was merely unreadable for a moment because of a race, and the ordering of the "proven vulnerability check" status.
- **Activity log and false-positive audit** (1.10.12): a Security Activity Log screen to search and filter past detections. An alert-fatigue audit fixed 27 findings: the ransomware emergency-containment banner now names the affected file and the suspected process, ClickFix protection only notifies when a command matches a known installer's pattern, the risky-Docker-container notification has a 24-hour cooldown, link protection states its triggering signal, and the Pickle-format AI model download warning shows where the file came from.
- **PersonalSOC bundled** (1.10.13): a separate app that gathers this machine's logs and defense status read-only into a report is now bundled in the DMG. 10 languages, Apache-2.0. Its LLM integration is optional and off by default. It is not updated by the in-app updater (Sparkle) but by a new DMG (from 0.1.1 it also has its own updater).
- **PersonalSOC in-app updater and more** (1.10.14): PersonalSOC got an in-app updater (Mac): it fetches the latest version information only when you press "Check for updates" in Settings, and verifies the signature before installing. The fixed "Response" section was removed from the reports, and chart legends and axis labels that overlapped in English and other languages were fixed.
- **Link guard and threat feed** (1.10.17): hostnames with a trailing dot (`evil.com.`) no longer slip past (they are normalized before the decision). The privileged helper now verifies the threat feed's signature before publishing it; before, a program running as the same user could empty the feed to disable the link guard or add hosts. When verification fails the previous feed is kept and protection is not removed, and a swap to a feed older than the recorded manifest is refused. A TLS ClientHello split across several records is now inspected up to 16 KB.
- **Quarantine and malware scanning** (1.10.17): a scanned file name containing line breaks and a fake detection line could make RoamSwitch quarantine an arbitrary user file; only the detected path is now scanned again before quarantine. Scans no longer hang on large output.
- **Secret masking in the execution record** (1.10.17 to 1.10.18): MCP execution-record search runs on the masked content, so secrets in command lines cannot be guessed from search hits, and the app itself applies output masking and the search rate limit. Masking now covers API keys such as `sk-svcacct-`, GitHub, GitLab, npm, Slack and Google tokens, `mysql -ppassword`, `DB_PASS=` and more. In 1.10.18 wrappers such as `sudo`, `env` and `nohup`, interpreters such as `sh`, `python`, `ruby`, `perl` and `node`, and the contents of `sh -c '…'` are seen through so the real command's rules apply. The integrity chain detects rewriting of a sealed segment and deletion of the oldest segment.
- **Data protection and state saving** (1.10.17 to 1.10.18): state saving (kill switch, port protection, Sensor pairing and more) is atomic, so an interruption cannot restore a state with the kill switch off. Fixed the stored endpoint being overwritten when a WireGuard import failed, and traffic staying blocked when `wg-quick up` failed and the previous kill switch was not restored. When the trusted-Sensor list cannot be read, other pairings are no longer erased by overwriting with an empty list; a copy is kept in `trusted_sensors.json.corrupt-<time>` (0600, latest 3) and the operation fails with a clear error (shown in 10 languages).
- **Working with Sensor** (1.10.17 to 1.10.18): pairing sends a proof of key possession (PoP). The control signature version is remembered at the highest confirmed level and is not downgraded on connections without a pinned certificate. The reason "Sensor's own storage file is corrupt, so pairing is impossible" returned by Sensor 0.3.15 is shown in 10 languages. The calmer warning during a Sensor audit returns to the normal warning once the report arrives (1.10.16).
- **Other fixes** (1.10.17): SSH remote-login state detection, DNS restore (disabled services and the like), resuming port-scan detection, and symlink handling in the process-execution record's plist check.
- **Decoys and notifications** (1.10.15): added `~/.pypirc` and `~/.env.backup` as decoys (honeytokens). A real access to a decoy places and watches one related decoy (the chain stops after one step). The process-execution record (Pro) gained a rule that detects commands naming a decoy file directly (11 rules). Log-audit notifications are limited to security-relevant anomalies such as authentication failures and XProtect detections. Warnings in the roughly 25 minutes after requesting a Sensor audit use calmer wording. Full Disk Access guidance was unified (the permission is decided by "RoamSwitch" in the System Settings list).
- **Bundled PersonalSOC** (1.10.15 to 1.10.27): 0.1.2 to 0.1.8 in turn (see each PersonalSOC release). In 1.10.27, the details of a CVE (known vulnerability) finding began to show the package name and version (0.1.8). In 1.10.21, the unreadable drop-down text on Linux was fixed (0.1.7; the Mac screens do not change). In 1.10.19, Settings is split into tabs by function, the message after a report is created is shorter, and the white margin around the app icon is gone (0.1.6).
- **Bundled PathScope** (1.10.19 to 1.10.20): 0.1.0 and 0.1.1 (see each PathScope release). In 1.10.20, 0.1.1 added reading config files with an LLM to find risks, 10 languages in the app, and in-app updates on Linux.
- **Bundled PersonalSOC** (1.11.4): PersonalSOC 0.1.10 is now bundled. From the command line, `personalsoc consent` sets consent to automatic log collection (see PersonalSOC 0.1.10). PersonalSOC can update itself from inside the app (Settings > Update). RoamSwitch itself reaches your Mac through the in-app update (Sparkle). There are no other changes.
- **Network isolation (Air-Gap) guide screen** (1.11.3): the "ℹ️ Why you're isolated & how to release…" screen gained a button to the "First Hour After an Incident" page ("See the first hour after an incident"). Pressing it opens the page in the app's display language (10 languages) in your browser. The page explains the order of checking, preserving, deciding, releasing and watching, for the Mac, the Linux desktop edition and the Linux Server Edition. It opens only when you press the button; RoamSwitch never connects to this page by itself. There are no other changes.
- **Four weaknesses of the privileged helper fixed** (1.11.5): snapshot restore no longer reads and hands over, as root, anything other than the user's own files. An ordinary user can create a hard link in their home to a file only root can read (for example `/private/etc/master.passwd`), and `O_NOFOLLOW` does not stop a hard link, so restoring from a later snapshot copied what root had opened into the user's folder; a file whose owner is not the console user is now refused. Unmounting a snapshot-browse mount could unmount an unrelated mount through a name containing links or `..`; the name is now matched exactly and checked as a real root-owned directory before unmounting. Pinning VPN tools (copying a tool from a user-writable place into a root-only place to run as root), uninstalling, and changing Sensor trust now require the administrator password (releasing quarantine does not, because it lifts automatic containment when nobody is present). The paths that succeed after entering the administrator password need on-screen interaction and were not checked on a real Mac. New text was added in 9 languages by machine translation.
- **Bundled apps** (1.11.5): AccessScope 0.3.2 (tidier terminal output) and PersonalSOC 0.1.11 (the "consent" to reading logs automatically was removed). The button name in the Air-Gap guidance screen was aligned to "View the first hour after an incident".
- **Bundled apps** (1.11.6): PersonalSOC 0.1.12 (a `sudo` example was added to the message shown when logs cannot be read). PersonalSOC can be updated from inside the app (Settings, "Update"). There are no other changes.
- **URL audit: shortened URLs and look-alike domains, and untranslated text**(1.11.7): Fixed: shortened URLs and domains that add words like "secure" or "login" to a brand name were judged safe. The URL audit now shows a notice, "Shortened URL (the destination is not visible)", for links such as `bit.ly` (penalty 25). A domain such as `paypa1-secure-login` or `microsoft-security-alert`, which adds words like "secure" or "login" to a brand name (including one written with digits or look-alike characters), is judged a severe risk (penalty 45). Official domains and names without a brand stay safe. What the link guard blocks or warns about does not change. Fixed: some text stayed in English after changing the language. 13 items (the Wi-Fi and ARP monitor states, the protection level names Maximum Lockdown, Balanced and Trusted, and the rename prompt) were English in German, Spanish, French, Italian, Portuguese and Korean (Simplified and Traditional Chinese: the three protection level names). They are now translated. These translations are based on machine translation and have not been checked by native speakers. The package vulnerability check (Homebrew) does not cover Ubuntu packages, so the Linux fix does not apply.
- **Bundled AccessScope 0.3.3**(1.11.8): The bundled AccessScope is now 0.3.3 (it gained detection of clients that behave very differently from the other visitors; see the AccessScope 0.3.3 entry). AccessScope can be updated from inside the app (Settings, "Update"). RoamSwitch itself reaches your Mac through the in-app updater (Sparkle). There are no other changes.
- **Bundled apps and the link protection seed data** (1.11.9): the bundled AccessScope is now 0.3.4 (the command line can run LLM analysis as root too, and the terminal output is now Markdown tables; the window app still does not run LLM analysis as root). The source of the link protection's bundled seed data changed. It used to be host names (about 280) from OpenPhish's free feed; to match OpenPhish's terms of use (commercial use and passing the data to third parties need its written consent), OpenPhish is no longer used, and the seed is now 2,000 randomly chosen currently active phishing domains from Phishing.Database (MIT licence). The seed only bridges the time until the feed's first update. The full threat feed received with the daily update never contained OpenPhish, so it is unchanged.
- (1.11.10) **New: the "Why you're isolated & how to release" window now shows a QR code for the first-hour guide page.** While the Mac is isolated, it cannot open the guide. A phone on its own connection can, so the window shows a QR code and one line, "Scan this QR code with your phone to read the guide on another connection." (10 languages), next to the link. The code holds only the public, fixed address (the page in the app's language): no host name and no query string. Nothing is sent over the network, and it uses macOS's built-in CoreImage, so there is no new component. It is drawn on white even in dark mode. I checked that the address in each of the 10 languages reads back from its QR code, and that the view, drawn to an image in both light and dark mode, can be read.
- (1.11.10) Linux (Ubuntu and others) gets the same display (Linux 1.11.20).
- (1.11.12) **Changed: the clipboard watch now checks every 0.1 s instead of every second.** When you copy a ClickFix-style malicious command, the app clears it from the clipboard, but with a once-a-second check it could be pasted until then. The check now runs every 0.1 s, and also while a menu is open. I did not measure the time from copy to clear on a real Mac (only the build and `ClickFixGuardTests` passing).
- (1.11.12) **Improved: the dependency CVE scan now says which project (repository) each finding comes from.** The scan walks the folders you point it at, but the results did not say which project a finding belonged to, so you could not tell which repository it was about. The results gained a "Project" column (the folder of the lockfile and the lockfile's name), and the first column of the CSV and the result of the MCP tool `run_package_cve_scan_languages` now carry the lockfile's path. Results are grouped by project; the same package in two projects is shown as two findings (10 languages).
- (1.11.12) **Changed: the "suspicious Terminal command" detection now waits less for changes to the shell history file** (the FSEvents latency from 1.0 s to 0.1 s, and the wait that merges consecutive changes from 0.5 s to 0.1 s). This detection reads the history after the command has run; it does not clear the clipboard.
- (1.11.12) Linux (Ubuntu and others) gets the same review (Linux 1.11.26).
- (1.11.14) **Changed: the bundled AccessScope is now 0.3.6 and PersonalSOC 0.1.13.** AccessScope includes the automatic decision of the real client for logs behind a reverse proxy, the tidying of chains and the fix for wrong web shell verdicts. PersonalSOC includes the new web log sources on Mac (the Apache that ships with macOS, and nginx and httpd from Homebrew). Both also arrive through the in-app update. There is no change to RoamSwitch's own features.
- (1.11.13) **Added: local AI service diagnosis, AI agent setup audit, an SSRF check in the active scan, and blocking of traffic with the 3 attack-server IPs published by JPCERT/CC using pf.** The AI services (Ollama, Jupyter Server, MLflow, Gradio) get one read-only GET to the listener on 127.0.0.1 and are reported only when reachable without authentication. The AI agent setup audit (MCP tool `audit_agent_setup`) checks instruction files, skills and OpenClaw settings read-only. The attack-server block puts its rules right after the anchor call and reads them back with `pfctl -sr` after applying (the read-back as root on a real Mac was not checked).
- (1.11.13) **Updated: the bundled AccessScope is now 0.3.5** (detection of XSS in headers and OAST call-back targets).
- (1.11.13) Linux (Ubuntu and others) has the AI service diagnosis, the AI agent setup audit and the SSRF check too (Linux 1.11.27).
- **SSH brute force blocking** (1.12.0): on a Mac with Remote Login (sshd) enabled, the unified log is followed, failures are counted, and pf drops only new connections to the SSH port (by default 6 failures in 10 minutes; the first ban is 1 hour, a repeat offender gets 4 times as long, the cap is 7 days). Established connections are not cut. The gateway, DNS, the Mac itself and loopback are never blocked; the exclusion list, sources that succeeded with a public key (30 days) and sources of authenticated SSH sessions are exempt; private IPs are not. With Remote Login disabled the log is not read. On by default when Pro is activated; when fail2ban or similar is active it only detects and notifies. Turning off the guard or blocking releases all current bans, and the guard is part of the emergency recovery steps. **Brute force coming from IPv6 on the same LAN is not blocked automatically, so as not to cut off the whole LAN** (it is only recorded). Notifications only for a ban and for a login that succeeds after failures (critical). Read-only MCP tool `get_ssh_guard_incidents` (the Mac tools became 33). Measured on a disposable macOS 26 VM: about 0.25 seconds from the 6th failure to the block. The sshd log cannot be read by a standard user or the sandboxed helper (measured), so it is read by a small root process (`--ssh-log-reader`) that starts before the sandbox and only launches `/usr/bin/log` with fixed arguments; the lines are interpreted by a sandboxed parse worker running as `nobody`, because an attacker can write them.
- **FIM notifications carry the SSH logins of the same period** (1.12.0): the owner and last modification, the SSH logins that started from 30 minutes before the change to about 1 minute after it (source, user, authentication method, time, still connected or not), and sessions that had been running since earlier. **macOS does not tell which process wrote a file, so this is a coincidence in time, not proof of cause** (the notification says so too).
- **Log audit fix on macOS 26** (1.12.0): the periodic log audit did not pick up SSH logins and failures, because OpenSSH 10 writes the login lines as `sshd-session`, not `sshd`. `sshd-session` is now covered, specified by name (matching by prefix would pick up unrelated `sshd-*` helpers).
- **Advisory score from a rewrite classifier in ransomware detection** (1.12.0, a port of the Linux edition's classifier): when a burst happens, 22 features are taken from the first, middle and last 4 KiB of up to the 20 most recent files, and a logistic regression computes a "ransomware-likeness" from 0 to 1. **On Mac it is advisory only**: the score is kept in the log and in the saved alert, the response does not change, a model that fails verification is not used, and bursts that touched canaries or honeytokens are not scored. FSEvents does not tell which process wrote, so the meaning of the inputs differs from Linux and the Linux numbers do not carry over.
- **AI service diagnostic fix** (1.12.0): "usable from the network" was shown even for services listening only on 127.0.0.1; only listeners reachable from the network are now diagnosed (the same construction as the bug confirmed on a real Linux machine).
- **MCP config audit** (1.12.0): a new finding `injection_exposure` (medium) in `audit_mcp_configs`, once per file where the same config has both a server that makes third-party text readable (web fetch, browser, search, chat, email, GitHub and so on) and a server that can run commands or change files, the arrangement that indirect prompt injection (ATT&CK T1698) depends on. It is only a match against known package and image names; combinations across files are not added up, and arguments, environment variables and tokens are not included in the result.
- **Bundled apps** (1.12.0): AccessScope 0.3.7, PathScope 0.1.9 and PersonalSOC 0.1.14 (see each app's entry), also delivered through the in-app update. Of the PersonalSOC changes, the ones that concern Mac are the new notice for an OS package CVE matching that could not be completed, and the order of the Settings tabs. The added wording is in 10 languages (based on machine translation, not reviewed by native speakers).
- (1.12.1) **Fixed: the honeytokens (decoy credential files) silently dropped every detection of a read.** To watch a decoy, the app itself holds the file open. On each detection it runs `lsof` to find who has it open, but the reader has usually exited by then, so only the app itself is left, and it was taken for the reader. A process of our own bundle counts as an "expected access", so the detection was dropped without a notification or a record. I installed the published 1.12.0 on a real Mac (a macOS 26 VM), confirmed that reading all five decoys produced no event at all, and then fixed it. The app's own process is now excluded, and when no reader is found it is reported as "could not be identified".
- (1.12.1) **Fixed: the honeytokens missed every read of a decoy after the first.** macOS (APFS) updates a file's access time only on the first read after a write (a second `cat` of the same file leaves it alone), and kqueue reports no reads. A detection that looked at the access time every 30 seconds saw only the first read of a decoy, and when the app had read an existing decoy itself after a restart (to check it), it saw none of the attacker's reads. The access time is now set back to one second before the modification time at start, when a related decoy is added, and after every detection, so the next read moves it again. The kqueue notification caused by that is ignored for 2 seconds.
- (1.12.1) On a real Mac (a VM), with the fixed build, the first read of a decoy, a second read of the same decoy, and a read of another decoy were all detected within 30 seconds.
- (1.12.1) The Linux honeytokens use a different mechanism (fanotify) and were not affected by this (Linux 1.12.1 fixed a different missed-read problem).
- (1.12.1) The bundled apps are unchanged (AccessScope 0.3.7, PathScope 0.1.9, PersonalSOC 0.1.14).
- **Lockfile tamper notification repeating** (1.12.2): the record of already-announced changes lived only in memory, so the same notification came at every launch. The baseline is deliberately never advanced on its own, so even the change from your own `npm install` was announced each time. The record is now saved and carried across restarts (a resolved change drops out, so the same file changing again is announced again), and the menu gained "Update lockfile baseline to current state…" (after a confirmation the current lockfiles become the baseline and only new changes are announced). The Linux edition had the same repeat fixed (Linux 1.12.2). The added text is in 10 languages (based on machine translation, not reviewed by native speakers).
- **Fix: the autostart detection never reported the same path twice** (1.12.3, `PersistenceMonitorGuard`): a LaunchAgent or LaunchDaemon plist that had been reported was skipped for as long as the app ran, so deleting it and creating it again under the same name, or rewriting it with another payload, raised no second alert. Reported plists are now remembered by path and by the SHA-256 of their content: a deleted file is forgotten, and a changed one is examined again. It was found while measuring how long the notification takes: from creating the file to handing the notification to macOS, 1.08, 1.09 and 2.59 seconds in three measurements (the FSEvents delay of up to 1.5 seconds and a 1.0 second buffer). 6 tests added. The Linux client's autostart watch already reported a changed or re-created file again; its comparison by modification time and size became a comparison by content in Linux 1.12.5.
- (1.12.5) **Added: the AI agent setup audit gains four detections and change detection against an accepted state** (the "AI Agent Setup Audit" screen and the MCP tool `audit_agent_setup`). It closes the gap that the instruction files, skills and MCP settings an AI agent reads could be swapped later without anyone noticing. The idea was taken from reading NVIDIA's SkillSpector (a scanner for AI agent skills); the rules are RoamSwitch's own, and no rule or regular expression was copied. The finding ids, thresholds and wording are the same as in the Linux edition (1.12.6).
